### OpenSpec
`https://github.com/Fission-AI/OpenSpec`

**Category:** Spec-driven development (SDD) framework / workflow layer for AI coding assistants (multi-tool, Markdown-based, CLI-installed)

**One-liner:** A lightweight, tool-agnostic spec-driven development framework that makes AI coding assistants (including Claude Code) align on a written proposal + spec + tasks before writing code, storing all that intent as Markdown next to your repo.

**Philosophy:** Spec-driven development: get the AI and human to agree on WHAT to build (and HOW) in a written spec before any code is generated, then implement against that spec. OpenSpec's stated principles (README/site, quoted verbatim): 'fluid not rigid -> iterative not waterfall -> easy not complex -> built for brownfield not just greenfield -> scalable from personal projects to enterprises', plus 'specs live in your code' as living, version-controlled documentation and 'explicit deltas instead of implicit drift' (specs/ = truth, changes/ = proposed deltas).

**How it works:** OpenSpec adds a planning/spec layer to AI-assisted coding centered on a change lifecycle. You install a Node CLI, run `openspec init` (which scaffolds an `openspec/` directory and writes tool-specific instruction/command/skill files), then drive the workflow through slash commands inside your AI tool.

Core three-phase lifecycle: (1) PROPOSE - `/opsx:propose <idea>` (older naming `/openspec:proposal`) creates a change folder under `openspec/changes/<change-id>/` containing proposal.md (rationale/scope), design.md (technical approach), tasks.md (implementation checklist), and spec deltas under specs/ describing requirement/scenario changes. (2) APPLY - `/opsx:apply` implements the tasks per the spec. (3) ARCHIVE - `/opsx:archive` finalizes the change, merging the spec deltas into the canonical `openspec/specs/` (the source of truth) and moving the change to an archive. Extended/profile-dependent commands include /opsx:new, /opsx:continue, /opsx:ff, /opsx:verify, /opsx:explore, /opsx:sync, /opsx:bulk-archive, /opsx:onboard.

Directory model: `openspec/specs/` = current accepted state (truth); `openspec/changes/` = active proposals; plus config (config.yaml). The CLI also provides supporting commands: list, view (interactive TUI dashboard), show, validate (structural/strict checks), status, archive, instructions, templates, schemas, workspace*, context-store*, update, config, feedback, completion.

Note: OpenSpec markets itself as 'fluid not rigid' (update any artifact anytime, no hard phase gates), but third-party reviewers describe the propose->apply->archive flow as effectively a three-phase state machine, so in practice there is real structure.

**Claude Code integration:** Multi-tool, NOT Claude-Code-only. Per the docs/supported-tools page it targets 30 AI coding assistants (the README says '25+ tools and growing'): Claude Code, Cursor, GitHub Copilot, Gemini CLI, Windsurf, Cline, RooCode, Amazon Q, Codex, Kiro, Kimi CLI, Mistral Vibe, Trae, ForgeCode, Junie, Lingma IDE, Pi, and more.

For Claude Code specifically, integration is via SLASH COMMANDS + SKILLS generated as plain files by `openspec init` — it is NOT a native Claude Code plugin and does NOT run as an MCP server, and it does not install hooks (CONFIRMED via the supported-tools doc, which lists only the skills and commands paths for Claude Code and mentions no MCP/plugin). Files written (verified against docs/supported-tools.md): slash commands at `.claude/commands/opsx/<id>.md` and skills at `.claude/skills/openspec-*/SKILL.md` (skills became default-synced in v1.4.0). Earlier/alternative integration relied on agent-instruction files (an `openspec/AGENTS.md` convention the agent reads). Because the integration is just Markdown command/skill/instruction files, the actual 'engine' is the AI model following those instructions plus the `openspec` CLI for scaffolding/validation/archiving — no runtime daemon. The CLI requires Node.js >= 20.19.0 (confirmed via npm engines field).

**Install:** npm install -g @fission-ai/openspec@latest
cd your-project
openspec init        # scaffolds openspec/ and writes tool files; supports --tools <claude|cursor|all>, --profile <core|custom>, --force
# after upgrading the CLI later:
openspec update      # regenerates instruction/command/skill files for your selected tools/profile

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained as of June 2026 (CONFIRMED). Latest release v1.4.1 published 2026-06-03 (authoritative npm CLI: time.modified = 2026-06-03T09:36:10Z, time.created = 2025-09-06T16:47:25Z); repo pushed_at/updated 2026-06-09. Repo created 2025-08-05, so it is roughly 10 months old with a fast roughly-monthly release cadence (v1.4.0 ~Jun 1, v1.3.1 ~Apr 21, v1.3.0 ~Apr 11, v1.2.0 ~Feb 23 — day/month confirmed via the releases page; the WebFetch summarizer mislabeled the year as 2024 but the repo did not exist until Aug 2025, so these are 2025/2026 dates). Recent releases added new tool integrations (Kimi CLI, Mistral Vibe, Junie, Lingma, Pi, Kiro), profiles, and path/telemetry fixes. Repo is MIT, not archived/disabled, ~389 open issues. Governance: published under the Fission-AI GitHub org (footer 'Fission'), has a Discord. The sole npm publisher/maintainer is 'tabish_' (tabishbidiwale@gmail.com) and a MAINTAINERS.md exists — so despite the org wrapper it appears to be a very small team / largely solo-led project. No fork-of-another-project history found; it is an original repo. CONFIDENCE: medium on team size (org membership is private). Minor note: the npm 'next' dist-tag points to a stale 0.3.0; only 'latest' (1.4.1) is current.

**Popularity:** Approximately 53,800 GitHub stars as of June 2026 (CONFIRMED). GitHub API returned 53,805 stars / 3,763 forks / 246 watchers on 2026-06-09; independent searches corroborate ~52.7k-53.7k in May/June 2026, consistent within normal growth. For context among SDD frameworks: GitHub Spec Kit is now ~111k stars (verified directly on GitHub; the previously cited '~80k+' is stale/low), BMAD-METHOD ~37-48k. Note the star count is unusually high for a ~10-month-old repo whose sibling org repo (PR-QUEST) has only ~18 stars; the figure is corroborated by the GitHub API and third-party articles, but the rapid growth is worth noting.

**Best for:** Teams and developers working on EXISTING (brownfield) codebases who want disciplined, reviewable AI-assisted changes without the heavyweight multi-agent ceremony of BMAD. Especially good for: mixed-tool teams (Claude Code + Cursor + Copilot) that want one shared, in-repo spec workflow; developers who value keeping intent/specs as version-controlled Markdown; and projects where 'align before code' and reducing cross-session drift matter more than maximal automation. Less ideal for quick one-off scripts/prototypes where the propose/apply/archive loop is overhead, or for teams wanting strong code-enforced gates rather than instruction-following.

**Risks:** ["Trust/governance: young (~10 mo) repo under an org with no public members and a single npm publisher (tabish_); MIT and open, but bus-factor/abandonment risk exists. The npm global install + 'openspec init/update' writing files into your repo and .claude/ config means you are running third-party-generated instruction files - review what init writes before trusting blindly.", "Supply-chain: it's an npm global package (@fission-ai/openspec); pin versions and review changelogs. No malware, rug-pull, or supply-chain incident found in searches as of June 2026, and GitHub's Security tab shows no published advisories. Mitigated by MIT/open source and the fact that artifacts are inspectable Markdown.", "Lock-in: LOW at the data layer (specs are portable Markdown, multi-tool), but MODERATE at the workflow layer - your team's process and command muscle-memory become OpenSpec-shaped; migrating away means re-tooling the workflow though the spec content remains usable.", "Complexity creep: the CLI's expanding feature set (workspaces, context-store, schemas, profiles) can outgrow the 'lightweight' promise.", "Determinism/security of execution: because enforcement is via model-followed instructions rather than hard gates, a model can deviate; do not treat generated specs/tasks as a security control.", "No confirmed independent security audit found, and the repo has no SECURITY.md disclosure policy."]

**Strengths:**
  - Tool-agnostic / no lock-in to the model layer: artifacts are plain Markdown in your repo (openspec/ proposal.md, design.md, tasks.md, spec deltas) that any AI tool or human can read
  - Brownfield-first design (explicitly built for existing codebases, not just greenfield) - frequently cited as its differentiator vs Spec Kit/BMAD
  - Lightweight and low-ceremony relative to BMAD's multi-agent personas; runs at a fraction of BMAD's token cost per third-party comparisons (BMAD can hit $2,000+/mo/dev in API spend; OpenSpec is among the cheapest)
  - Clear, auditable change lifecycle (propose -> apply -> archive) with a single source of truth in openspec/specs/, reducing 'prompt drift' across sessions
  - Broad native integration (30 tools per docs) via generated slash commands/skills, so a team using mixed tools (Claude Code + Cursor + Copilot) shares one spec workflow
  - Actively developed, MIT licensed, frequent releases, has docs, CLI validation (openspec validate), and a TUI dashboard (openspec view)
  - Scored highest overall in one independent 2026 multi-category evaluation on a medium backend (though ranking is priority-dependent)

**Weaknesses:**
  - Not a native Claude Code plugin and not an MCP server - integration is just generated Markdown command/skill files, so behavior depends entirely on the model faithfully following instructions (less deterministic than code-enforced gates)
  - Marketing ('fluid not rigid, no phase gates') vs reality: reviewers describe a real three-phase state machine; the structure can feel like overhead for tiny/throwaway changes
  - Requires a global Node.js >= 20.19.0 install and a per-project init step; adds files to your repo and to tool config dirs (.claude/commands, .claude/skills)
  - Governance opacity: Fission-AI org has no public members, a single sole npm publisher (tabish_); bus-factor/long-term-stewardship risk for a young project
  - Surface area is growing fast (many CLI subcommands: workspaces, context-store, schemas, profiles) which cuts against the 'minimal process' pitch and can confuse newcomers
  - No SECURITY.md / vulnerability-disclosure policy set up on the repo as of June 2026 (GitHub Security tab shows zero published advisories and no policy)
  - Effectiveness is model-dependent - weaker models may not respect the spec discipline the framework assumes

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Independently re-verified the riskiest claims against authoritative sources; corrected two items and confirmed the rest.

GitHub Security tab shows zero published advisories AND no SECURITY.md disclosure policy (added this as a weakness/risk - the profile didn't note the missing policy). Governance opacity (private org membership, sole npm publisher tabish_) confirmed; bus-factor risk is real but there is no abandonment signal given the June 2026 release.

NET CHANGES vs submitted profile: (1) Spec Kit star context updated ~80k+ -> ~111k; (2) tool count pinned to 30 (from docs) rather than '~26-30'; (3) added 'no SECURITY.md / disclosure policy' to weaknesses and risks; (4) noted stale npm 'next' dist-tag; (5) reconfirmed npm timestamps via CLI and resolved the 2024-vs-2025/26 year ambiguity. Confidence raised to HIGH overall - all riskiest claims (stars, maintenance, integration mechanism, security) independently corroborated by primary sources (GitHub API, npm CLI, repo docs).

### cc-sdd
`https://github.com/gotalab/cc-sdd`

**Category:** Spec-Driven Development (SDD) harness / agentic-coding workflow toolkit for AI coding agents (multi-tool, Claude Code-first)

**One-liner:** An npm-installed, "Kiro-style" spec-driven development harness that installs structured discovery -> requirements -> design -> tasks -> autonomous-implementation workflows (as ~17 Agent Skills) into Claude Code and 7 other AI coding agents (8 platforms total).

**Philosophy:** Specs are treated as CONTRACTS between parts of the system, not as a master command document handed to the agent; code remains the source of truth. Core thesis (from docs/guides/why-cc-sdd.md): "Code remains the source of truth. Specs make the contracts between parts of the code explicit, so humans and agents can work in parallel without constant synchronization." It argues the bottleneck in AI coding is coordination, not agent capability ("Agents are fast... The bottleneck is not capability. It is coordination."), and that explicit, right-sized contracts make team-scale AI development FASTER, not slower. It separates a specification layer (requirements, boundaries, file structure = what must be respected) from a design/implementation layer (where agents explore freely within approved boundaries). Borrows from large-team engineering: explicit interfaces, contract-style boundaries, continuous verification, focused boundary review. Modeled on AWS Kiro's mental model (requirements/design/tasks) plus an AI-DLC (AI-Driven Development Lifecycle) framing.

**How it works:** Phased, human-gated workflow. Phases (v3.x): Discovery (route an idea into a new spec, an extension, or direct implementation; emits brief.md / roadmap.md) -> optional Steering (capture persistent project knowledge into .kiro/steering/*.md) -> Spec init (create the feature workspace) -> Requirements (EARS-format requirements.md with acceptance criteria) -> Design (architecture with Mermaid diagrams + a File Structure Plan in design.md) -> Tasks (tasks.md with _Boundary:_ and _Depends:_ annotations and parallel "wave" mapping) -> Implementation -> optional Quality/Gap gates -> Validation -> Status tracking. Each phase pauses for human approval unless bypassed (e.g. -y / --auto). Slash commands / skills include: /kiro-discovery (entry point), /kiro-spec-init, /kiro-spec-requirements, /kiro-spec-design, /kiro-spec-tasks, /kiro-impl (long-running autonomous implementation), /kiro-steering, /kiro-validate-gap, /kiro-spec-batch (multi-spec parallel creation). The autonomous /kiro-impl loop spawns a FRESH implementer subagent per task plus an INDEPENDENT reviewer subagent, runs TDD (RED->GREEN), and on a task blocked after two remediation rounds spawns a DEBUG subagent in a fresh context (with web search). Artifacts: brief.md, roadmap.md, requirements.md, design.md, tasks.md, plus steering docs; default living under a .kiro/ directory (specs in .kiro/specs/<feature>/, settings/templates/rules in .kiro/settings/).

**Claude Code integration:** Claude Code is the primary/"stable" target and the default install. Current v3.x integrates as native Claude Code Agent Skills: the installer writes ~17 skills (README states "17 skills per install, loaded on demand (progressive disclosure)") into .claude/skills/, creates/updates a project CLAUDE.md, and creates the .kiro/ workspace (steering, specs, settings/templates/rules). The default `npx cc-sdd@latest` installs Claude Code skills; the explicit flag form is `--claude-skills`. Subagents are spawned through each platform's NATIVE primitive (Claude Code's own subagent/Task mechanism) for the per-task implementer, reviewer, and debug agents. It is NOT a Claude Code plugin in the marketplace sense, NOT an MCP server, and does NOT rely on hooks (verified: no MCP/plugin/hooks integration in README/tools README; GitHub security page shows no advisories). Invocation is via slash commands (/kiro-*). A legacy/deprecated mode (--claude flag, older /kiro:* commands) installs traditional slash commands into .claude/commands/kiro/ instead of skills; README confirms "Legacy /kiro:* command modes are still available (--claude, --cursor, etc.) but are deprecated." It is explicitly MULTI-TOOL, not Claude-only: the same ~17-skill set targets 8 platforms — Claude Code (--claude-skills) and Codex (--codex-skills), both marked Stable, plus Cursor, GitHub Copilot, Windsurf, OpenCode, Gemini CLI (all Beta) and Antigravity (Beta/experimental). README states the skills/templates are "identical across all 8 platforms." 13 UI/output languages supported via --lang.

**Install:** Run in the project root (no global install needed). Default = Claude Code skills:
  npx cc-sdd@latest          (equivalent to --claude-skills)
Other agents:
  npx cc-sdd@latest --codex-skills
  npx cc-sdd@latest --cursor-skills
  npx cc-sdd@latest --copilot-skills
  npx cc-sdd@latest --windsurf-skills
  npx cc-sdd@latest --opencode-skills
  npx cc-sdd@latest --gemini-skills
  npx cc-sdd@latest --antigravity
Useful flags: --lang en|ja|zh-TW|zh|es|pt|de|fr|ru|it|ko|ar|el (13 languages) ; --kiro-dir docs (relocate spec dir) ; --dry-run --backup (preview) ; --os (override OS detection) ; -y/--auto (skip approval gates). Legacy/deprecated slash-command mode: --claude (and --cursor/--copilot/--windsurf/--opencode/--gemini/--qwen). After install, use /kiro-discovery (or /kiro-spec-init) inside Claude Code to begin. License: MIT.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained as of mid-2026 — NOT abandoned. Latest release v3.0.2 published 2026-04-13 (confirmed via GitHub releases page and npm registry: published version is 3.0.2). Dense activity: v3.0.0 -> v3.0.1 -> v3.0.2 all within 09-13 Apr 2026; v2.1.x in Feb 2026; more than 10 releases total (releases page is paginated). Repo last pushed 2026-05-20 (GitHub API pushed_at 2026-05-20T08:45:14Z); updated_at 2026-06-09; archived=false, disabled=false (confirmed via GitHub API). CAVEAT / RISK: this is a SOLO project, not an org — GitHub owner type is "User" (verified via GitHub API and the repo HTML page; account login "gotalab", display name "Gota", account created 2020-12-06, 21 public repos, 154 followers). The bulk of contributions are by the owner, the rest by bots (claude[bot], devin-ai-integration[bot]) and a few one-off external contributors. Bus-factor is essentially one. Any prior claim that the maintainer is an "organization" is INCORRECT per the GitHub API. Repo created 2025-07-17 (about 11 months old as of 2026-06-09). Note: project has NOT set up a SECURITY.md file (GitHub security overview explicitly says "This project has not set up a SECURITY.md file yet").

**Popularity:** 3,458 GitHub stars and 256 forks (GitHub API, fetched 2026-06-09; repo HTML page shows ~3.5k stars / 256 forks / 39 watchers, consistent). Open issues: API reports 20 (this count includes open PRs); the repo's Issues tab shows ~15 issues-only. npm package "cc-sdd": 9,331 downloads in the last week (npm downloads API, 2026-05-27 to 2026-06-02) and 33,060 in the last 30 days (2026-05-04 to 2026-06-02). Current published npm version 3.0.2, MIT. Popular and well-known within the SDD/agentic-coding niche, but far smaller than GitHub's spec-kit (reported to have passed ~90k stars by 2026); frequently appears in 2026 SDD tool comparison roundups. No malware reports, no security advisories (GitHub Advisory Database returns 0 results for cc-sdd), and no rug-pull/supply-chain compromise affecting this package were found.

**Best for:** Teams or individuals doing LARGER, multi-file, longer-lived changes (system expansions, refactors with cross-module boundaries, security/performance work, features needing an audit trail) where coordination and documented decisions matter — especially Claude Code or Codex users (the two Stable targets) who want a structured, repeatable discovery->requirements->design->tasks->autonomous-implementation pipeline and persistent project memory. Also good for teams standardizing one SDD workflow across multiple AI assistants. NOT a good fit for tiny edits, quick bug fixes, throwaway prototypes, or solo one-off sessions, where the phase/approval overhead outweighs the benefit.

**Risks:** ["Trust/continuity: SOLO maintainer on a personal GitHub account (verified owner type 'User', not an org). If abandoned, there is no governance backstop; you would be relying on a fork. No SECURITY.md is published. Mitigated somewhat by MIT license and plain-markdown artifacts that remain usable even if the tool stops shipping.", "Supply-chain: installed via 'npx cc-sdd@latest' which pulls and executes the latest published package at install time. As with any npx tool it runs with your local permissions and writes files into your repo (.claude/, .kiro/, CLAUDE.md). No advisories/malware found for this package (GitHub Advisory DB returns 0 results), but pin a version and/or review the package if you have strict supply-chain requirements; release notes reference 'safer filesystem handling' and a vitest v4 bump to clear a dev-dependency vuln, implying past FS-handling/dependency hygiene work. Beware a separate third-party derivative package 'cc-ssd-enh' — not the same project.", "Autonomy risk: /kiro-impl is a long-running autonomous loop that spawns subagents and can web-search during debug; running with -y/--auto removes human gates. Use approval gates and run in a sandbox/branch for untrusted or high-stakes code.", "Lock-in: LOW on the tool itself (multi-agent across 8 platforms, MIT, portable markdown) but it does impose a fairly opinionated Kiro-style workflow and a .kiro/ directory convention; migrating off the methodology mid-project has switching cost.", "Complexity: rapid version churn (v2->v3 skills rework, legacy /kiro: commands vs current skills modes) and a multi-phase model mean a learning curve and a risk of following stale third-party docs.", "Maturity: 6 of 8 supported targets are Beta/experimental, so behavior may be inconsistent outside Claude Code and Codex."]

**Strengths:**
  - One-command setup (npx cc-sdd@latest) that drops a complete, opinionated SDD workflow into a project with no global install
  - Genuinely multi-agent: identical ~17-skill set across 8 platforms (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Copilot, Windsurf, OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Antigravity) — reduces lock-in to a single assistant
  - Human approval gates at each phase (discovery/requirements/design/tasks) catch misunderstandings early when fixes are cheap; EARS-format requirements force precision
  - Persistent project memory via steering docs (.kiro/steering) survives across sessions, avoiding re-explaining the codebase
  - Autonomous /kiro-impl with separate implementer + independent reviewer + fresh-context debug subagent, TDD (RED->GREEN), and per-task review is a strong loop for larger work
  - Modern Agent Skills integration (progressive disclosure) rather than dumping everything into context; uses each platform's native subagent primitive
  - MIT licensed, transparent file-based artifacts (plain markdown) that are easy to review, diff, and version-control
  - Actively developed with frequent releases (3 releases in 4 days in April 2026) and i18n (13 languages)
  - Clean security posture: no published advisories, no Dependabot-visible vulns, no malware reports as of 2026-06-09

**Weaknesses:**
  - Solo maintainer (bus-factor ~1, confirmed User-type account not an org) — long-term continuity, security response, and governance depend on one individual; no SECURITY.md policy is published
  - Overhead/verbosity: the multi-phase steering->requirements->design->tasks gate sequence is overkill for small edits or prototypes; the maintainer's own guidance is to use plain agentic coding for small changes and SDD only for large ones
  - Markdown sprawl: like other SDD tools it generates many docs that can be repetitive vs. each other and vs. existing code, and tedious to review (a common SDD-tool critique)
  - Young project (created 2025-07-17) with rapid major-version churn (v2 -> v3 reworked around skills); workflows/commands and integration modes (skills vs legacy /kiro: commands) have shifted, so external tutorials/articles can be out of date
  - Beta status for 6 of 8 supported agents (Cursor, Copilot, Windsurf, OpenCode, Gemini, Antigravity) means uneven reliability outside the two Stable targets (Claude Code, Codex)
  - It is a workflow scaffold, not magic — quality still depends on the underlying model and on humans actually reviewing the gates; bypassing gates (-y/--auto) removes most of the safety value
  - Conceptual overhead: 'specs as contracts' plus boundary/dependency annotations and wave planning is a learning curve for solo devs used to ad-hoc prompting

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-checked the riskiest claims against PRIMARY live sources on 2026-06-09; the profile holds up well with only minor corrections. (1) POPULARITY — CONFIRMED via GitHub REST API (Invoke-RestMethod): stargazers_count=3458, forks_count=256, open_issues_count=20, license MIT, default_branch main. Repo HTML page independently showed ~3.5k stars / 256 forks / 39 watchers and 15 open ISSUES (the API '20 open issues' figure includes PRs, so I clarified the 15-vs-20 discrepancy rather than treating either as wrong). npm CONFIRMED: last-week 9,331 (2026-05-27..06-02), last-30-days 33,060 (2026-05-04..06-02), published version 3.0.2, MIT. (3) OWNER — CONFIRMED solo User, NOT an org: GitHub API owner.type='User', user type='User', login 'gotalab', name 'Gota', created 2020-12-06, 21 public repos, 154 followers. Any 'organization' claim is wrong. ### awesome-claude-code-subagents (VoltAgent)
`https://github.com/VoltAgent/awesome-claude-code-subagents`

**Category:** Curated subagent (agent-definition) collection / Claude Code plugin marketplace — NOT a workflow framework with phases

**One-liner:** A curated library of 154+ specialized Claude Code "subagents" (prompt-defined personas with scoped tools), distributed as a native Claude Code plugin marketplace plus an interactive bash install script — it is a catalog of agents, not an opinionated multi-phase orchestration framework.

**Philosophy:** Design philosophy: composable, single-responsibility "specialist" agents over a monolithic assistant. Each subagent is an isolated, domain-specific helper with (a) its own context window to prevent cross-task contamination, (b) a tightly scoped tool allowlist for least-privilege, and (c) a model tier matched to task difficulty. The collection deliberately rides Claude Code's native subagent/plugin primitives rather than inventing a runtime, so agents stay portable plain-text and team-shareable. It is curation-first ("awesome list" ethos): maximize breadth of ready-to-use expertise and let Claude Code's delegation pick the right specialist, rather than imposing a fixed development process.

**How it works:** There are no "phases" or a prescribed workflow — this is fundamentally different from process frameworks like spec-driven kits. The repo is a collection of individual subagent definition files. Each subagent is a single Markdown file with YAML frontmatter (name, description, tools list, model: opus/sonnet/haiku/inherit — confirmed; README example shows model: sonnet) followed by a detailed system prompt describing the role. Files are grouped into 10 numbered category folders under categories/ (e.g. 01-core-development, 02-language-specialists ... 04-quality-security ... 10-research-analysis — folder structure confirmed via API/tree). Once installed, Claude Code's NATIVE subagent feature does the rest: Claude Code auto-delegates a task to a matching subagent based on its description, or the user invokes one explicitly (e.g. "have the code-reviewer subagent analyze my commits"; the built-in /agents command lists installed agents). Each subagent runs in its own isolated context window with only the tools its frontmatter grants. The repo also ships an optional catalog tool under tools/subagent-catalog/ — confirmed to contain README.md, a single shell script config.sh, and four Markdown command-doc files (search.md, fetch.md, list.md, invalidate.md) that back slash commands to browse/fetch agents from the catalog on demand. (Note: there is NO SKILL.md in that folder — describing it as a packaged 'skill' is imprecise; it is command docs plus one config script. The previously claimed 12h-TTL cache at ~/.claude/cache/subagent-catalog.md could NOT be re-verified this pass because the SKILL.md fetch 404'd — treat the exact cache path/TTL as unconfirmed.) Artifacts created: the install copies .md agent files into ~/.claude/agents/ (global) or .claude/agents/ (project) — confirmed in install-agents.sh; the catalog tooling writes a cache file. No spec docs, plans, or task files are generated — agents just augment normal Claude Code sessions.

**Claude Code integration:** Deeply Claude-Code-native and Claude-Code-ONLY. Integration vectors confirmed: (1) Native Claude Code plugin MARKETPLACE — ships .claude-plugin/marketplace.json (manifest version 1.0.3, confirmed) defining exactly 10 category plugins (voltagent-core-dev, voltagent-lang, voltagent-infra, voltagent-qa-sec, voltagent-data-ai, voltagent-dev-exp, voltagent-domains, voltagent-biz, voltagent-meta, voltagent-research — all 10 names verified verbatim). (2) Claude Code's built-in SUBAGENT system — the .md files use Claude Code's exact subagent frontmatter format (name/description/tools/model) and rely on Claude Code's delegation engine. (3) SLASH COMMANDS via the optional tools/subagent-catalog tooling: /subagent-catalog:search, :fetch, :list, :invalidate (all four confirmed in README); plus the built-in /agents command. NO hooks shipped. NO MCP server shipped by the repo (it only references third-party MCP projects — e.g. airis-mcp-gateway, moai-adk, taskade, BGPT MCP — in an ecosystem list; it does not bundle one). The subagent definitions reference Claude Code built-in tools only (Read, Write, Edit, Bash, Glob, Grep, WebFetch, WebSearch). It is NOT multi-tool: no Cursor / Copilot / OpenCode / Gemini / Windsurf support of any kind (README confirmed to mention none of these) — the format and plugin system are specific to Claude Code.

**Install:** Plugin marketplace (recommended): `claude plugin marketplace add VoltAgent/awesome-claude-code-subagents` then `claude plugin install <plugin-name>` (e.g. `claude plugin install voltagent-lang` or `voltagent-core-dev`). Interactive script: `git clone https://github.com/VoltAgent/awesome-claude-code-subagents.git && cd awesome-claude-code-subagents && ./install-agents.sh` (interactive menus: choose global vs project, local-repo vs GitHub source, multi-select install/uninstall). Standalone (no clone): `curl -sO https://raw.githubusercontent.com/VoltAgent/awesome-claude-code-subagents/main/install-agents.sh && chmod +x install-agents.sh && ./install-agents.sh`. Manual: copy any agent .md into `~/.claude/agents/` (global) or `.claude/agents/` (project). CONFIRMED: install-agents.sh has a `#!/bin/bash` shebang, depends on curl (check_curl() aborts if missing), and makes NO mention of Windows/WSL/PowerShell — it is Unix/Linux/macOS-oriented. On Windows (this user's environment) use WSL/Git Bash, or just copy files / use the cross-platform `claude plugin` command. No npx package.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained as of the research date. Org-owned (owner type confirmed: Organization = VoltAgent, the open-source company behind the separate VoltAgent TypeScript agent framework at github.com/VoltAgent/voltagent), NOT a solo hobby repo — lowers rug-pull/abandonment risk vs. an individual maintainer. Created 2025-07-30; last push 2026-05-27 (~13 days before the 2026-06-09 research date — recent); repo last touched/updated 2026-06-09. Governance: company-led with community contributions via CONTRIBUTING.md and active PR merges. Not a fork (fork:false confirmed), not archived (archived:false confirmed), not disabled. IMPORTANT CAVEAT (re-confirmed): there are ZERO formal GitHub releases AND ZERO git tags (both /releases and /tags API endpoints returned empty arrays) — versioning exists only as the marketplace.json manifest version (1.0.3) plus README edits, so there is no changelog/semver release cadence to pin to; you track a moving main branch. License: MIT (confirmed).

**Popularity:** ~21.5k GitHub stars (21,468 confirmed via API on 2026-06-09), 2,509 forks, 220 watchers/subscribers, ~14 open issues. (Note: the API 'watchers_count' field returns 21,468 because GitHub aliases it to the star count; the real watcher number is the 220 'subscribers_count'.) One of the most-starred Claude Code subagent collections; amplified by the VoltAgent org's broader marketing reach. Competing collections such as supatest-ai/awesome-claude-code-sub-agents exist but are far smaller.

**Best for:** Claude Code users who want a fast, low-commitment way to add domain expertise to ad-hoc sessions — e.g. solo devs, consultants, and teams who want a ready-made code-reviewer, language specialist, security auditor, or infra expert without authoring prompts themselves. Best when you cherry-pick a few high-value agents (or one category plugin) rather than installing all 154+. Good for teams standardizing a shared set of reviewer/specialist personas across a repo via project-level .claude/agents/. NOT the right choice if you need a structured, repeatable, phase-based development methodology (use a spec/workflow framework instead) or if you need cross-tool portability beyond Claude Code.

**Risks:** ["Security/trust: agents are unaudited community prompts that can grant Bash and write/edit tools; a malicious or careless agent prompt could instruct destructive shell actions. The repo's own disclaimer says subagents are 'as is' without warranty and explicitly NOT audited (verbatim confirmed), with no liability accepted. No specific malware/CVE/supply-chain incident was found against THIS repo as of 2026-06-09. MITIGATION: read each agent's frontmatter tools list and prompt before installing; prefer minimal tool grants; install per-project, not globally.", "Supply-chain: installing via plugin marketplace or curl|bash executes/pulls from a third-party org's main branch with NO pinned release or tag (both confirmed empty) — review install-agents.sh before running and avoid blind curl-piping. The script does download remote .md files when GitHub source mode is chosen.", "Lock-in: LOW — content is plain Markdown using Claude Code's open subagent format; removing it is just deleting files. The only soft lock-in is that the format is Claude-Code-specific (not reusable in Cursor/Copilot/Gemini).", "Abandonment/rug-pull: MODERATE-LOW — org-backed and active as of mid-2026 (last push 2026-05-27), but the company's primary product is the separate VoltAgent framework; this catalog is partly a marketing funnel and could see reduced attention. No releases/tags means you must self-pin (fork or vendor the agents you depend on).", "Complexity/quality drift: installing many overlapping agents can cause unpredictable delegation and bloated ~/.claude/agents; keep the set small."]

**Strengths:**
  - Huge breadth: 154+ ready-made expert personas across 10 categories (core dev, 30+ language specialists, infra, QA/security, data/AI, dev-ex, domains, business, meta-orchestration, research) — covers most common dev roles out of the box
  - Truly native integration: uses Claude Code's own plugin marketplace + subagent system, so zero custom runtime, no proprietary engine lock-in — agents are just portable Markdown files
  - Modular install: pick individual agents, a single category plugin, or all — low commitment and easy to remove (delete the .md). The interactive installer supports multi-select install/uninstall
  - Per-agent isolated context windows + scoped tool permissions reduce context pollution and limit blast radius of any one agent
  - Per-agent model routing (opus/sonnet/haiku/inherit) lets cost/quality be tuned per task
  - Org-backed and high-visibility (~21.5k stars), MIT-licensed, with an active contribution pipeline
  - Optional catalog tooling (/subagent-catalog:search|fetch|list|invalidate) enables on-demand fetching without bulk-installing every agent

**Weaknesses:**
  - NOT a workflow/process framework — provides no phases, planning, spec generation, or orchestration discipline; if you expected a spec-driven or multi-phase pipeline, this is just a persona library
  - Quality is uneven and explicitly UNAUDITED: the repo's own disclaimer states it does not audit or guarantee the security or correctness of any subagent (verbatim confirmed); many prompts are long, generic, and overlapping
  - No formal releases AND no tags/changelog (both confirmed empty) — hard to pin a known-good version; you track a moving main branch and self-pin by forking/vendoring
  - Curated 'awesome list' style means lots of agents you'll never use; potential agent-name/description collisions can cause Claude Code to mis-route delegation
  - Install script and catalog tooling are bash/curl-centric (Unix/macOS); the installer has a #!/bin/bash shebang and no Windows/WSL/PowerShell handling, so Windows users (this environment) need WSL/Git Bash or the cross-platform `claude plugin` command / manual file copy
  - Marketing overlap: README cross-promotes the unrelated VoltAgent TS framework and lists third-party ecosystem MCP tools, which can blur what is actually being installed
  - Effectiveness depends on Claude Code's auto-delegation heuristics, which are not deterministic; explicit invocation is often needed for reliable results
  - Documented agent count is inconsistent across the project (README/badge say 154/154+, the GitHub repo description still says 100+), so the exact number drifts

Updated headline to '154+' and flagged the cross-source inconsistency as a weakness. ### Claude Squad
`https://github.com/smtg-ai/claude-squad`

**Category:** External terminal/TUI orchestrator for running multiple AI coding-agent CLIs in parallel (multi-agent session manager). NOT a Claude Code plugin/skill/MCP server.

**One-liner:** A standalone Go terminal UI that runs several AI coding-agent CLIs (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, Aider, OpenCode, Amp, or any shell command) at once, each isolated in its own tmux session and git worktree.

**Philosophy:** Isolation and human-in-the-loop control over autonomy: give each agent task its own sandboxed git worktree + tmux session so parallel work never collides, keep a mandatory review/diff step before changes are shipped, and stay deliberately tool-agnostic (orchestrate any agent CLI rather than build a bespoke agent). It favors composing existing primitives (git, tmux, gh) over a heavyweight bespoke runtime.

**How it works:** Claude Squad is a Bubbletea-style Go TUI launched via the `cs` command (default binary name). Each "instance" (task) is spawned with two layers of isolation: (1) a detached tmux session named `claudesquad_<title>_<timestamp>` that runs the chosen agent CLI as a child process and captures its pseudo-terminal output; (2) a dedicated git worktree under `~/.claude-squad/worktrees/` on a branch named `<prefix>_<name>_<timestamp>`, so parallel agents never collide in the working tree. The TUI lists all instances on the left with a preview/diff pane on the right. It polls instances on two timers (~100ms for the preview pane, ~500ms for status/diff metadata) and uses SHA256 hashing of terminal output (`TmuxSession.HasUpdated()`) to flip an instance between Running and Ready states. Lifecycle ops: Start (create worktree+branch, spawn tmux, launch agent), Pause (commit changes, detach tmux, delete the on-disk worktree to save space), Resume (recreate worktree from branch, reattach), Kill (tear down tmux + worktree + branch). Key bindings: n (new), N (new with a starting prompt), Enter/o (attach into the agent's tmux session), Tab (toggle preview/diff and a Terminal shell tab), s (push/ship — commit and push the branch, used with the `gh` CLI to open a PR), c (checkout/checkpoint), r (resume), D (delete), q (quit), Shift+J/K (reorder). Workflow phases: spin up N tasks -> let agents work in the background (optionally unattended) -> review each agent's diff in the diff pane -> push/PR the ones you approve. Artifacts/files it creates: `~/.claude-squad/config.json` (default program, named profiles, settings), `~/.claude-squad/worktrees/` (per-instance worktrees), persisted instance state loaded on startup, plus the tmux sessions and git branches above. The binary itself installs to `~/.local/bin`.

**Claude Code integration:** Integration with Claude Code is purely external process orchestration: Claude Squad launches the `claude` CLI binary as the default child program inside a tmux pane and reads/writes its terminal. There is NO native Claude Code plugin, NO Claude Code skill, NO MCP server, NO slash commands, and NO hooks shipped by this project (confirmed absent across the repo, README, website, and DeepWiki). It does not register anything inside Claude Code; Claude Code is unaware it is being run under Claude Squad. Because the agent is just "whatever command you configure," it is explicitly multi-tool, not Claude-Code-only: it ships support/profiles for Claude Code (default, `claude`), OpenAI Codex (`export OPENAI_API_KEY`), Gemini CLI, Aider (`aider --model <model>`), OpenCode, and Amp, and you can point `-p/--program` (or a named profile in config.json) at any arbitrary CLI. The optional `-y/--autoyes` mode starts a background daemon that polls instances and auto-presses Enter (TapEnter) to clear agent confirmation prompts, enabling unattended/"yolo" runs (explicitly experimental and not recommended for unattended use). GitHub integration is via the external `gh` CLI (used by the push/ship action), not via any Claude-specific mechanism.

**Install:** Homebrew (macOS/Linux): `brew install claude-squad` then optionally alias to `cs`: `ln -s "$(brew --prefix)/bin/claude-squad" "$(brew --prefix)/bin/cs"`. Script install: `curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/smtg-ai/claude-squad/main/install.sh | bash` (custom binary name: append ` -s -- --name <your-binary-name>`; default binary name is `cs`). The script auto-detects OS/arch (darwin/linux/windows; amd64/arm64), downloads the matching release asset from GitHub Releases (.tar.gz on Unix, .zip on Windows), installs to `~/.local/bin` (override with BIN_DIR), edits your shell profile (.zshrc/.bashrc/fish config/.profile) to add it to PATH, and will auto-install the tmux and gh dependencies (using sudo on Linux/macOS). Prerequisites: tmux and gh (GitHub CLI). Run with: `cs` (flags: `-p/--program <cmd>`, `-y/--autoyes`). No npm/npx package; it is a Go binary.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained as of mid-2026; not abandoned, deprecated, or archived. Latest release v1.0.18 published May 23, 2026 (releases page shows May 23, 03:25 UTC; the tags page's May 18 reflects the underlying tag/commit date — the published-release date is May 23). Release cadence is irregular and slowing into 2026: v1.0.13 (Aug 28, 2025), v1.0.14 (Dec 24, 2025), v1.0.16 (Mar 1, 2026), v1.0.17 (Mar 12, 2026), v1.0.18 (May 23, 2026) — i.e., a roughly 5-month gap (Aug-Dec 2025), then a cluster in March, then May. Repo first created March 2025. Governance: GitHub org `smtg-ai`, but effectively maintainer-led by Mufeez Amjad (@mufeez-amjad / @moofeez), a University of Waterloo alum currently an engineer at Linear in New York (ex-CockroachDB, ex-Meta), with a handful of outside PR contributors — closer to a solo/small-team project than a company-backed one. No evidence of a fork-of-an-earlier-project history; this is the original/canonical repo. Recent work: Terminal shell tab, source-branch selection, preset profiles, worktree startup-time and diff-performance improvements (diff computed only for the selected instance), instance reordering (Shift+J/K), and pause-error state-consistency fixes.

**Popularity:** Approximately 7.7k-7.8k GitHub stars as of June 2026 (live repo page fetch reported 7.8k; an aggregated directory snapshot ~1 week old reported 7.7k), with 552 forks (live repo page). Strong upward trajectory: third-party snapshots show 2,817 stars (Jul 2025) and 3,997 (Aug 2025), so the count roughly doubled over the following ~10 months. Widely featured in "awesome Claude Code" / agent-orchestrator lists and round-ups. Star count is confident to within a couple hundred (live page fetch + recent aggregator agree at ~7.7-7.8k).

**Best for:** Individual developers and small teams comfortable in a terminal (tmux/git) who want to fan out several independent coding tasks across multiple AI agents at once and review each in isolation before merging — e.g. knocking out a batch of small features/bugfixes in parallel, or comparing how Claude Code vs Codex vs Gemini tackle the same task. Best when you are already a heavy CLI agent user and want orchestration without committing to one vendor. Poor fit for GUI/IDE-centric users, Windows-native-only setups, teams blocked by AGPL, or anyone wanting tight in-editor plugin/MCP-style integration with a single agent.

**Risks:** ["Trust/supply-chain: install via `curl | bash` runs a remote script that edits your shell profile/PATH and uses sudo to install dependencies (tmux, gh); prefer Homebrew or inspect install.sh first. Pin a release if you want reproducibility.", "Autonomy risk: the autoyes daemon auto-accepts agent prompts, so an agent can run commands and modify the repo unattended — it is explicitly experimental; only use on disposable branches/sandboxes.", "Maintainer/continuity risk: effectively a one-person project (Mufeez Amjad / smtg-ai); if the maintainer steps away, support could lapse. No evidence of abandonment, malware, or a rug-pull as of mid-2026 — it is still shipping (latest release May 23, 2026), though cadence has slowed.", "Lock-in: low at the tool level (it's just orchestration over standard CLIs, tmux, and git), but the AGPL-3.0 license is itself a legal/redistribution constraint for commercial use.", "Complexity/cost: running many agents in parallel increases API token spend, system load, and the human effort to review/merge potentially conflicting changes.", "Data exposure is inherited from whichever agent CLI you run (Claude Code/Codex/etc.) — Claude Squad adds no extra sandboxing of what the agent itself can read or send."]

**Strengths:**
  - True parallelism: run many coding agents simultaneously, each fully isolated, from one terminal window
  - Strong isolation model — git worktrees (no working-tree collisions) plus tmux sessions (process isolation, detach/reattach for debugging)
  - Tool-agnostic: works with Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, Aider, OpenCode, Amp, or any CLI; no lock-in to a single vendor's agent
  - Built-in diff/preview review step so you inspect each agent's changes before shipping; push/PR via gh
  - Optional unattended autoyes daemon for background 'yolo' runs
  - Lightweight single Go binary, simple install, low runtime overhead
  - Pause/resume reclaims disk by tearing down idle worktrees while preserving the branch
  - Permissive-enough to extend: state is plain config.json + git branches you can inspect manually

**Weaknesses:**
  - Hard dependencies on tmux and the gh CLI; not a self-contained app and weakest on native Windows (best on macOS/Linux; Windows generally via WSL because of the tmux requirement)
  - No deep/native integration with Claude Code — it only drives the CLI via a terminal, so it cannot use Claude Code plugins, skills, MCP, hooks, or slash commands as first-class features
  - Task-completion detection is heuristic (SHA256 diffing of terminal output + Enter-tapping), which can misjudge 'done' vs 'waiting'; the autoyes mode is explicitly experimental/risky and not recommended for unattended use
  - AGPL-3.0 license can be a non-starter for some companies embedding/redistributing it or turning it into a network service
  - Effectively solo-maintained (one primary maintainer), creating bus-factor/continuity risk; release cadence is irregular and has slowed in 2026
  - Parallel agents multiply token spend and can produce conflicting or duplicated work that still needs human reconciliation at merge time
  - Documentation is light on advanced internals (MCP/hooks explicitly absent); some behavior must be learned from source or DeepWiki
  - Multiple concurrent agents on a non-trivial repo can be resource-heavy and hard to supervise

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-verified the riskiest claims against primary sources on 2026-06-09. CORRECTED: maintainer affiliation was wrong in the input profile — it said Mufeez Amjad is at 'Georgia Tech'; his GitHub profile (github.com/mufeez-amjad) shows University of Waterloo alum, currently an engineer at Linear, based in New York, ex-CockroachDB and ex-Meta. No Georgia Tech mention anywhere. RESOLVED the v1.0.18 date ambiguity: the published release date is May 23, 2026 (releases page shows v1.0.18 at May 23, 03:25 UTC; the tags page's May 18 reflects the tag/commit date, not the release). CONFIRMED via live repo page: 7.8k stars / 552 forks; aggregator snapshot ~1 week old shows 7.7k — so ~7.7-7.8k, now high confidence (no longer relying on rate-limited API). CONFIRMED description 'Manage multiple AI terminal agents like Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, and Amp' and AGPL-3.0 license. CONFIRMED integration model: pure external CLI orchestration, with NO plugin/MCP/skill/hooks/slash commands present (none found on repo/README/website). CONFIRMED multi-tool support (Claude Code default, Codex, Gemini, Aider, OpenCode, Amp). CONFIRMED install.sh behavior: curl|bash, edits shell profile/PATH, uses sudo for deps, auto-installs tmux+gh, OS/arch detection, installs to ~/.local/bin (BIN_DIR override), default binary name `cs`, `--name` flag. SECURITY/TRUST: dedicated searches for claude-squad malware/supply-chain/abandonment/rug-pull returned NOTHING specific to this project — the prominent 2026 'Claude Code' supply-chain/source-leak incidents are about Anthropic's Claude Code CLI, not this orchestrator, and the 'dangerous no-permissions mode' references point to a separate TMux-Orchestrator project. A competitor (Crystal/Stravu) was deprecated Feb 2026, not Claude Squad. Independent round-up (Nimbalyst) corroborates AGPL constraint, no native Windows (tmux dependency), and experimental autoyes. Adjusted maintenance text to note the slowing/irregular release cadence in 2026 (Aug 2025 -> Dec 2025 gap, March cluster, May). Note: live GitHub REST API was not used (historically rate-limited), but rendered page fetches provided the needed figures, so confidence is high. Confidence raised from the input's 'High/Medium' mix to HIGH overall, with the one factual correction (maintainer affiliation) applied.

### claude-code-router
`https://github.com/musistudio/claude-code-router (npm: @musistudio/claude-code-router; docs: https://musistudio.github.io/claude-code-router/)`

**Category:** Model-routing proxy / provider-switching infrastructure for Claude Code (NOT an agentic workflow/spec-driven methodology framework). It is a request router/middleware, comparable in spirit to LiteLLM but purpose-built to sit in front of Claude Code.

**One-liner:** A local CLI + HTTP proxy ("ccr") that intercepts Claude Code's API calls and routes them to any LLM provider (DeepSeek, Gemini, OpenRouter, Groq, Ollama, Qwen, etc.) based on task type and context length, so you keep Claude Code's UX while swapping the underlying model.

**Philosophy:** "Use Claude Code as the foundation for coding infrastructure, allowing you to decide how to interact with the model while enjoying updates from Anthropic." Stated docs principles: Speed/Simplicity (get started in minutes with one command, no complicated configuration), Intelligent Routing (auto-route to the best model by context length/task type/custom rules), Multi-Provider Support, Economic Efficiency (cheaper models for routine tasks, reserve Claude for complex work), Customization, and Accessibility (use Claude Code without an Anthropic account). Core idea: decouple Claude Code's interface from Anthropic's models so the user controls model selection and cost.

**How it works:** Runs a local Node.js HTTP server (default http://127.0.0.1:3456) that speaks the Anthropic Messages API. Claude Code is pointed at this server via the ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL environment variable (plus ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN and NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1), so all of Claude Code's traffic flows through the proxy. The proxy reads ~/.claude-code-router/config.json, which has two top-level objects: (1) "Providers" — a list of upstream endpoints, each with name, api_base_url, api_key (supports $VAR / ${VAR} env interpolation), models, and a "transformer" pipeline; and (2) "Router" — a map of task categories to "provider,model" pairs. Router categories (confirmed in README): default (general), background (cheap/background tasks), think (reasoning / Plan Mode), longContext (auto-triggered above longContextThreshold, default 60000 tokens), webSearch, and image (beta). Routing is decided per-request based on context length, task type, and optional custom rules. Built-in "transformers" (anthropic, deepseek, gemini, openrouter, groq, maxtoken, tooluse, reasoning, enhancetool, etc.) rewrite requests/responses to fit each provider's API quirks; you can also supply arbitrary JS via CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH (an async function module.exports = async (req, config) => "provider,model" | null) and custom transformer plugins, which execute locally on your machine. It is NOT a phased/spec-driven methodology: there are no /specify, /plan, /tasks phases and it does not scaffold spec artifacts. The only artifacts are config/logs/presets under ~/.claude-code-router/. Architecture is a TypeScript monorepo: cli (the ccr command), @CCR/server (routing core, depends on @musistudio/llms), @CCR/shared, and @CCR/ui (a React+Vite web config UI).

**Claude Code integration:** Integration is via CLI wrapper + local proxy + environment variables — NOT a native Claude Code plugin, NOT an MCP server, and NOT via Claude Code hooks or a Claude Code plugin marketplace entry (re-confirmed against the README and docs; v2.0.0 did not add a plugin-marketplace install path). Mechanism: `ccr code` launches Claude Code with ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL pointed at the local proxy (http://localhost:3456); `ccr activate` / `eval "$(ccr activate)"` exports those env vars (ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL + ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN) so the plain `claude` command or Agent SDK apps route through it. CCR-specific in-session controls (both confirmed in README): a `/model provider,model` slash command to switch models live, and subagent routing by prefixing a subagent prompt with `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>provider,model</CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>`. These are CCR conventions interpreted by the proxy, not generic Claude Code features. It also offers a GitHub Actions path (companion repo musistudio/claude-code-router-action) using NON_INTERACTIVE_MODE. Primarily Claude-Code-targeted: the docs and design are exclusively about Claude Code; there is no first-class Cursor/Copilot/Gemini-CLI/OpenCode integration (any tool that honors ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL/Anthropic API could in principle point at it, but that is not an advertised feature).

**Install:** Prereqs + install (global): `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code` then `npm install -g @musistudio/claude-code-router` (pnpm/yarn also supported). IMPORTANT: install the exact scoped name @musistudio/claude-code-router — there are forks and look-alikes (e.g., @tellerlin/claude-code-router, @rikaaa0928/..., @w154594742/...) and active malicious clones of Claude Code routing tools in the wild (see risks). Then create ~/.claude-code-router/config.json with Providers + Router. Run with `ccr code` (starts proxy + Claude Code together). Other commands: `ccr start` / `ccr stop` / `ccr restart` (manage the proxy service on port 3456), `ccr ui` (web config UI), `ccr model` (interactive model picker), `ccr activate` (export env vars), `ccr preset export|install|list|info|delete` (share/reuse configs). Verify actual routing in ~/.claude-code-router/logs/ (Claude Code's header still shows the default Anthropic model, so logs are the source of truth).

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained but solo-governed (NOT abandoned). Single maintainer/author: "musistudio" (me@jinhui.li) — a personal GitHub/npm account, not an organization or foundation (the @CCR/* package scopes are internal monorepo names, not a separate npm org). Re-verified via `npm view`: 75 published versions; first release 1.0.0 on 2025-06-10; recent cadence frequent (1.0.69 on 2025-11-19, 1.0.73 on 2025-12-18); latest is v2.0.0 published 2026-01-04 (registry time.modified 2026-01-04T06:03:46Z). GitHub API (read 2026-06-09) shows archived=false, disabled=false, last code push pushed_at=2026-03-04, and community issues opened as recently as June 2026 — so it is clearly still maintained, though there has been no new npm release in ~5 months (last publish 2026-01-04) and no code push since 2026-03-04, which is worth watching. The GitHub Releases page is genuinely empty ("There aren't any releases here", re-confirmed), so there are no tagged release notes/changelogs on GitHub — versioning is npm-only. License: MIT (confirmed on npm + repo). Core HTTP/transform logic lives in a separate dependency, @musistudio/llms, also by the same author (same bus-factor).

**Popularity:** Very popular and re-confirmed via the GitHub REST API (read 2026-06-09): 34,823 stars, 2,858 forks, 958 open issues. The profile's prior "~35k stars / ~2.9k forks" is accurate; the open-issue figure is higher than the previously cited "~800" and is now ~958. Star count has been broadly flat-to-slightly-down vs. some earlier snapshots (an April 2026 third-party snapshot reported ~33.2k), consistent with a mature, very widely starred project. Widely covered in third-party tutorials (DataCamp, dev.to, ClaudeLog, TokenMix, DeepWiki, multiple blogs).

**Best for:** Cost-sensitive or experimentation-driven developers who love Claude Code's interface but want to (a) cut API spend by routing routine/background work to cheaper models, (b) run fully local/offline via Ollama, (c) use Claude Code without an Anthropic account, or (d) mix providers (e.g., a reasoning model for Plan Mode, a long-context model for big repos). Good for hobbyists and individual power users comfortable managing a local proxy and JSON config, and comfortable verifying they installed the genuine @musistudio package. Less suitable for teams needing official support, strict reliability/compliance, or guaranteed Claude-grade agentic behavior — and it is the wrong tool entirely if you wanted a spec-driven/workflow methodology framework (it is purely a model router).

**Risks:** ["Supply-chain / impersonation (ELEVATED, time-sensitive): the legitimate @musistudio/claude-code-router is NOT known to be compromised, but the Claude-Code routing-proxy CATEGORY is being actively weaponized in 2026. Security writeups (SafeDep, Xygeni 'FauxCode', Trend Micro, Malwarebytes, Bitdefender, 7AI) document malicious npm clones/typosquats of Claude Code routing tools that install cleanly, work as expected, and silently reroute API traffic — e.g., FauxCode's 'Cluster A' drops a CA cert to ~/.ccr/ca-bundle.crt (note the .ccr path mimicking this project's ccr command) and opens a reverse tunnel; 'Cluster B' rewrites ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL to attacker infrastructure. Verify the exact scoped package name and source before installing; avoid look-alike forks unless you trust them.", "Trust/governance: single-maintainer personal project (musistudio, me@jinhui.li) with no org backing — typical supply-chain and abandonment/rug-pull exposure for a globally-installed npm package that also pulls @musistudio/llms by the same author. Pin versions and review updates.", "Security (inherent design): it is a man-in-the-middle proxy that sees ALL Claude Code traffic, including your source code and prompts; it can forward them to arbitrary third-party providers you configure. With LOG enabled, payloads are persisted to ~/.claude-code-router/logs.", "Custom transformers and CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH execute arbitrary local JavaScript — only run presets/configs you trust.", "Exposure risk: if you set APIKEY and bind beyond 127.0.0.1 you could expose the proxy on your network; default is localhost-only (confirmed).", "Provider/data-handling risk: routing code to DeepSeek/Volcengine/SiliconFlow/etc. sends proprietary code to those providers under their (sometimes weaker) data policies.", "Soft lock-in: low (it's MIT and you can revert to plain Claude Code by unsetting ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL), but workflows built around CCR presets/routing are CCR-specific.", "Complexity/reliability: an extra always-on service in the loop is one more thing that can fail or silently misroute (header doesn't reflect active model)."]

**Strengths:**
  - Keeps Claude Code's exact UX/agent loop while letting you swap in cheaper or alternative models (DeepSeek, Gemini, Qwen, Groq, OpenRouter, local Ollama, Volcengine, SiliconFlow, etc.) — main draw is large cost reduction
  - Task-aware routing: separate models for default/background/think/longContext/webSearch/image, plus an auto longContext threshold (default 60k tokens, confirmed in README) so big contexts go to long-context models automatically
  - Lets you use Claude Code without an Anthropic subscription/account (route entirely to third-party or local models)
  - Extensible: built-in transformers normalize provider API differences, and CUSTOM_ROUTER_PATH + custom transformers allow arbitrary JS routing/retry logic
  - Convenience tooling: web config UI (ccr ui), interactive model picker, shareable presets (with API keys sanitized to placeholders on export), and a GitHub Actions companion for CI
  - Per-subagent (<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>) and live (/model) model switching within a session
  - Local-only by default (binds 127.0.0.1 unless you set an APIKEY — confirmed README security note), MIT-licensed, very large community (~35k stars)

**Weaknesses:**
  - Tool/function-calling compatibility is the biggest practical issue — Claude Code is agentic and depends on tool calls for edits/git/terminal; non-Claude models often handle this poorly or hit 'No endpoints found that support...' errors
  - You get the routed model's quality, not Claude's — extended thinking, Claude-specific tool-use behavior, and multi-step reasoning can degrade or break
  - Cascading-error risk: if background work routes to a weak model and produces a mess, later steps build on it
  - Adds operational complexity: a separate proxy service must be running, config changes need `ccr restart`, and the Claude Code header lies about which model is active (you must read ~/.claude-code-router/logs to confirm routing)
  - Extra network hop adds latency
  - No GitHub release notes/changelog (npm-only versioning, Releases page empty) makes it harder to audit what changed between versions
  - Solo maintainer / bus-factor of one; no organizational governance or official support channel; ~958 open issues
  - Privacy tradeoff when LOG:true — full request payloads (your code) are written to disk
  - Naming/category is heavily impersonated by malware (typosquat clones, fake 'ccr'/routing tools), so installing the wrong package is a real and active hazard

**Fact-check confidence:** High

**Verification notes:** Independently re-verified the riskiest claims on 2026-06-09. (1) STARS/POPULARITY — pulled fresh GitHub REST API (api.github.com/repos/musistudio/claude-code-router): stargazers_count=34,823, forks_count=2,858, open_issues_count=958, archived=false, disabled=false, pushed_at=2026-03-04, updated_at=2026-06-09; corrected open issues from the profile's '~800' to ~958; stars/forks (~35k/~2.9k) confirmed accurate. (2) MAINTENANCE — ran `npm view`: version=2.0.0, time.created=2025-06-10, time.modified=2026-01-04, maintainers=musistudio <me@jinhui.li> (single), license=MIT; full version list confirmed 75 versions ending [..1.0.71,1.0.72,1.0.73,2.0.0], with 2.0.0 published 2026-01-04T06:03:46Z, 1.0.73 on 2025-12-18, 1.0.69 on 2025-11-19 — cadence matches the profile. NOT abandoned: repo unarchived, last code push 2026-03-04, issues opened June 2026; however flagged that no new npm release has shipped since 2026-01-04. GitHub Releases page re-confirmed genuinely EMPTY (npm-only versioning). (3) INTEGRATION MECHANISM — re-confirmed against the raw README and docs: env-var proxy (ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL, port 3456) + `ccr code`/`ccr activate`; NOT a Claude Code plugin, MCP server, or hook; `/model provider,model` and `<CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL>` subagent tags both present; Router categories (default/background/think/longContext/webSearch/image) and longContext default 60000 tokens confirmed; APIKEY/127.0.0.1 localhost-binding security note confirmed; no plugin-marketplace path added in v2.0.0. (4) SECURITY/TRUST — found NO evidence the legitimate @musistudio package is malicious, abandoned, or a rug-pull. BUT confirmed an active and significant 2026 threat landscape against the Claude-Code routing-proxy category: SafeDep (typosquat npm packages abusing Claude Code SessionStart hooks), Xygeni 'FauxCode' (malicious Claude Code CLI clones — claude-code-best, @tmecontinue/claude, heibai — that silently reroute API traffic; Cluster A even uses a ~/.ccr/ca-bundle.crt path echoing this project's `ccr` convention, Cluster B rewrites ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL), plus broader fake-Claude-Code malvertising/infostealer campaigns (Trend Micro, Malwarebytes, Bitdefender, 7AI). None implicate musistudio's repo, but they materially strengthen the supply-chain/MITM/impersonation warnings — I added an ELEVATED, time-sensitive supply-chain risk item and an install-the-exact-package caveat. NOTE on tooling: the npmjs.com web page returned HTTP 403 and one WebFetch of the registry JSON returned stale/garbled values (claimed latest 1.0.58) — I disregarded that and relied on the authoritative `npm view` CLI output instead. The 'phases/slash-commands/subagents/artifacts' methodology framing remains inapplicable: this is a model-routing proxy, not a phased agentic framework; reported what actually exists (CCR's /model command, CCR-SUBAGENT-MODEL markers, config/log/preset files).

**Sources:** https://github.com/musistudio/claude-code-router · https://api.github.com/repos/musistudio/claude-code-router · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/musistudio/claude-code-router/main/README.md · https://musistudio.github.io/claude-code-router/ · https://github.com/musistudio/claude-code-router/releases · https://github.com/musistudio/claude-code-router-action · https://www.npmjs.com/package/@musistudio/claude-code-router · https://registry.npmjs.org/@musistudio/claude-code-router · https://claudelog.com/claude-code-mcps/claude-code-router/ · https://tokenmix.ai/blog/claude-code-router-guide-2026 · https://deepwiki.com/musistudio/claude-code-router · https://www.datacamp.com/tutorial/claude-code-router
