### Claude Code Agent Farm
`https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/claude_code_agent_farm`

**Category:** Multi-agent orchestration / parallelization harness for the Claude Code CLI (external Python+tmux supervisor, not a Claude Code extension)

**One-liner:** A Python + tmux orchestration framework that launches and supervises 20+ (up to 50) parallel Claude Code CLI sessions to sweep a codebase for bug/lint/type fixes or best-practices improvements, with lock-based coordination and a live dashboard.

**Philosophy:** "Parallelism over a single smarter agent" - rather than improving one agent, fan out many disposable Claude Code agents against bounded problem sets and coordinate them with file locks and a shared work registry, with safety scaffolding (backups, locks, heartbeats, emergency cleanup) so unattended runs fail safe. Author Jeffrey Emanuel frames it within a broader 'Agentic Coding Flywheel' of ~14 multi-agent coordination/safety tools.

**How it works:** It is an external supervisor, not anything that runs inside Claude Code. A Python program (claude_code_agent_farm.py) spawns a tmux session with N panes, each pane running the Claude Code CLI. Phases: (1) Problem generation - it runs the project's own type-checker/linter/test commands (e.g. mypy/ruff, tsc, etc.) to produce a combined problems file; (2) Agent launch - starts agents in tmux panes with adaptive 'stagger' timing (halves on success, doubles to max 60s on failure); (3) Task distribution - each agent is given a prompt template and picks a random chunk of the problem list to work on; (4) Conflict prevention - agents mark completed problems so others don't repeat them; (5) Progress tracking - changes are committed with diff summaries. Two main workflows: 'Bug Fixing' (parallel type/lint fixes) and 'Best Practices Implementation' (drives a per-stack guide, tracking progress in @<STACK>_BEST_PRACTICES_IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRESS.md). An advanced 'Cooperating Agents' mode adds a /coordination/ directory with active_work_registry.json, planned_work_queue.json, completed_work_log.json and per-agent lock files in agent_locks/: before touching files an agent must check the registry, write a lock claiming specific files/features, register a scoped work plan, and update status; stale locks (>2h) are reclaimed. Each agent gets a unique id (agent_{timestamp}_{random4}). Artifacts it writes: .claude_agent_farm_state.json (monitor state/metrics), .heartbeats/agent*.heartbeat (liveness), .claude_agent_farm_backups/ (rotated settings backups, last ~10, 200MB cap), agent_farm_report_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.html (run report), and ~/.claude/.agent_farm_launch.lock (single-launch lock). Ships ~37 prompt templates, ~35 best-practices guides, and config files covering 34 tech stacks.

**Claude Code integration:** CLI-orchestration only - it drives the existing `claude` binary as a child process inside tmux panes; it is NOT a native Claude Code plugin, NOT a skill, NOT an MCP server, and NOT a hooks integration. CONFIRMED directly from the repo README/page. It requires you to define an alias `cc="ENABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1 claude --dangerously-skip-permissions"` (the README states this alias is mandatory and is auto-configured by setup.sh) and depends on that permission-bypass flag to run unattended. The only 'commands' are TUI key bindings the supervisor sends to the panes: Ctrl+R broadcasts the built-in `/clear` to every agent to free context, and Ctrl+C does graceful-then-force shutdown. It is Claude-Code-specific: the README/CHANGELOG reference only Claude / Claude Code and make NO mention of Cursor, Copilot, Gemini, OpenCode or any other tool. CONFIRMED DISCREPANCY: a third-party aggregator (cultofclaude.com, which states twice on the page that it is 'Not affiliated with Anthropic') labels this a 'Claude Code Skill' and lists an install command `claude install-skill https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/claude_code_agent_farm` - that is aggregator-generated, is NOT a real/official Claude Code command, and appears nowhere in the repo's own docs; do not rely on it.

**Install:** git clone https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/claude_code_agent_farm.git && cd claude_code_agent_farm && chmod +x setup.sh && ./setup.sh  (setup.sh creates a Python 3.13 venv via `uv`, installs deps, and configures the `cc` shell alias for bash/zsh). Prerequisites (CONFIRMED in README): Python 3.13+, uv, tmux, git, the Claude Code `claude` CLI installed and authenticated, plus the project's own toolchain (e.g. mypy/ruff for Python, bun for Next.js). direnv optional. Linux/macOS oriented (tmux-based); README mentions no Windows-native support and there is no npx/pip-package install path. Each agent uses ~500MB RAM (README figure).

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained as of this research (today 2026-06-09), though no recent commits in the last ~2.5 months. Solo project by Jeffrey Emanuel (GitHub @Dicklesworthstone, '2.7k followers', '179' public repos) - an individual, not an org or company, but a known/reputable figure in agentic-coding tooling (author of the widely-cited DeepSeek market essay; builds a 'Flywheel' of ~14 agent tools incl. the 'dcg' destructive-git-guard hook). Latest commit on main is dated 'Mar 26, 2026' ('fix: add ❯ Try ready indicator for agent prompt detection'); the CHANGELOG was (re)built 'Mar 22, 2026'. Changelog spans '2025-06-28 -- Initial release' to '2026-02-21/22 -- License and branding'. No tagged releases or GitHub Releases (page shows 'No releases published'; CHANGELOG explicitly states 'This project has no tagged releases or GitHub Releases'); versioning is by dated commits only. License changed 2026-02-22 from plain MIT to 'MIT + OpenAI/Anthropic Rider'. Repo shows '48 Commits'.

**Popularity:** 841 GitHub stars, 94 forks, 17 watchers (read directly off the live repo page during this research, 2026-06-09). Search-engine snapshots showed stale lower counts (704-752) and one third-party tracker showed 844; 841 is the current live figure from the repo page itself. Topics: python, tmux, automation, developer-tools, ai-agents.

**Best for:** Teams or power users on large, mature codebases who want to brute-force a high volume of well-scoped, mechanical improvements - e.g. clearing thousands of mypy/ruff/tsc/eslint errors, applying a consistent best-practices pass across a monorepo, or large lint/type debt cleanups. Requires comfort with the terminal/tmux, a Linux/macOS environment, willingness to run agents with permissions skipped inside a git repo, and budget for many concurrent Claude Code sessions. Not a fit for Windows-native users, for nuanced one-off feature work, or for anyone needing a supported/governed tool.

**Risks:** ["Security: requires --dangerously-skip-permissions for ALL agents, meaning unattended file edits and tool/command execution with no per-action approval. This is the dominant risk - run only in a sandboxed/throwaway checkout, ideally a container or VM, never on sensitive prod credentials. Note general 2026 reporting on Claude Code permission-bypass abuse (e.g. crafted CLAUDE.md files escalating subcommands to exfiltrate SSH/AWS/GitHub secrets) underscores why running with permissions skipped is dangerous - though those reports concern Claude Code itself, not this repo specifically.", "Trust/abandonment: single individual maintainer, no org/company, no releases, no recent commits at research time - typical solo-project abandonment risk; vet the code before running since you execute a setup.sh and grant broad agent permissions. Mitigating factor: the author is a known, non-anonymous figure with a public track record. No malware, rug-pull, or supply-chain reports were found against this specific repo.", "License: switched 2026-02-22 from plain MIT to a non-standard 'MIT + OpenAI/Anthropic Rider' (restricting use by OpenAI/Anthropic/affiliates without express written permission from the author) - a custom rider that may matter for some orgs' legal review and is not OSI-standard MIT.", "Lock-in: coupled specifically to the Claude Code CLI; no abstraction for other agents/tools, so it does not port to Cursor/Copilot/Gemini/OpenCode.", "Complexity/cost: many concurrent billed sessions, heavy RAM use (~500MB/agent), and best-effort coordination can still yield merge conflicts, duplicated work, or large hard-to-review diffs; always run on a dedicated branch and review before merging."]

**Strengths:**
  - Genuinely parallelizes Claude Code - can run 20-50 agents at once for large mechanical sweeps (lint/type/test fixes) far faster than a single session
  - Lock-based coordination (active work registry + per-agent lock files + completed-work log, with stale-lock reclaim) is a real attempt to avoid agents clobbering the same files / duplicating work
  - Batteries-included breadth: ~37 prompt templates + ~35 best-practices guides spanning 34 tech stacks (Next.js, Python, Rust, Go, Java, C++, Angular, Flutter, Terraform, Kubernetes, Solana, etc.)
  - Operational safety scaffolding: timestamped settings backups with rotation (last ~10, 200MB cap), single-launch file lock, stale-lock reclaim, heartbeat liveness tracking, emergency cleanup of tmux/lock/state on exit
  - Real-time tmux dashboard plus per-run HTML reports give observability into a normally opaque parallel-agent run
  - Self-correcting launch behavior (adaptive stagger that backs off on failures) and broadcast /clear to manage context across all panes
  - Maintainer is a known, public, reputable figure in agentic tooling (not an anonymous account), which lowers the typical solo-project trust risk somewhat

**Weaknesses:**
  - Hard-depends on `claude --dangerously-skip-permissions`, so all agents run with permission prompts disabled - high blast radius if a prompt or agent misbehaves
  - No tagged releases / semver - you pin to a moving main branch, so reproducibility and upgrade safety are weak
  - Linux/macOS + tmux only in practice; no Windows-native path, no npx/pip package - non-trivial setup (Python 3.13, uv, tmux, per-stack tooling)
  - Solo-maintainer bus factor: one author, no org backing, no formal support or governance; no commits in the ~2.5 months before this research
  - Heavy resource cost (~500MB RAM/agent; dozens of concurrent API-billed sessions) and coordination is best-effort - merge conflicts/overlap are mitigated, not guaranteed
  - Best suited to mechanical/bounded fixes; large parallel fleets on nuanced refactors risk inconsistent or conflicting changes and large noisy diffs
  - Quality of output is only as good as the bundled prompts/best-practice guides, which can drift from current framework/Claude versions

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-checked all four riskiest claim areas directly against live sources on 2026-06-09. POPULARITY (confirmed/upgraded to high): read 841 stars / 94 forks / 17 watchers directly off the live GitHub repo page - exactly matching the profile; stale search snapshots (704-752) and one tracker (844) are outdated, profile's 841 is correct. MAINTENANCE (confirmed): commits page shows latest commit 'Mar 26, 2026' ('fix: add ❯ Try ready indicator...'), CHANGELOG rebuilt 'Mar 22, 2026', changelog spans 2025-06-28 to 2026-02-22; repo page and CHANGELOG both confirm 'No releases published' / 'no tagged releases or GitHub Releases'; license change to 'MIT + OpenAI/Anthropic Rider' confirmed dated 2026-02-22. Adjusted wording to note no commits in ~2.5 months before research, so 'actively maintained' is qualified. INTEGRATION (confirmed): README and repo page confirm it is an external CLI/tmux orchestrator - NOT a plugin/skill/MCP/hook - requiring the `cc` alias with `--dangerously-skip-permissions`. Confirmed the cultofclaude.com aggregator (self-labeled 'Not affiliated with Anthropic', stated twice) is the ONLY source calling it a 'Skill' and listing the bogus `claude install-skill <repo-url>` command, which is not in the repo and is not a real Claude Code command. SECURITY/TRUST (confirmed, no specific issues): found NO malware, rug-pull, or supply-chain reports against this specific repo. General 2026 'Claude Code' security search hits were about Anthropic's product/unrelated malware lure campaigns, not this project; I added the CLAUDE.md permission-escalation context to the security risk because it directly illustrates why --dangerously-skip-permissions is dangerous, while flagging it is not about this repo. Verified the maintainer is the real, public Jeffrey Emanuel (2.7k followers, 179 repos, known DeepSeek-essay author / agentic-tooling builder), which I added as a mild trust-strengthening factor. Could not query the GitHub REST API for exact numeric metadata (no gh/API access), so star/fork/watcher figures were read off the rendered repo page. Template/guide counts (~37 templates, ~35 guides, 34 stacks) remain README-reported and not independently file-audited (the one item kept at medium certainty). Net: corrected nothing materially wrong - the original profile was accurate; tightened maintenance wording, expanded the security-context note, and added the maintainer-reputation nuance. Overall confidence: high.

**Sources:** https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/claude_code_agent_farm · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Dicklesworthstone/claude_code_agent_farm/main/README.md · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Dicklesworthstone/claude_code_agent_farm/main/CHANGELOG.md · https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone/claude_code_agent_farm/commits/main · https://github.com/Dicklesworthstone · https://cultofclaude.com/skills/claude-code-agent-farm/ · https://trendshift.io/repositories/23839 · https://www.jeffreyemanuel.com/

### Spec-Flow
`https://github.com/marcusgoll/Spec-Flow`

**Category:** Spec-Driven Development (SDD) workflow toolkit for AI coding agents (primarily Claude Code; optional Gemini CLI)

**One-liner:** A file-based, spec-driven workflow toolkit that copies a structured pipeline of slash commands, subagents, skills, and hooks into your repo to take an idea from spec to production with quality gates and auditable artifacts.

**Philosophy:** Spec-Driven Development: the specification is the primary artifact and code is a generated output derived from it, with human review at phase gates rather than during implementation. Core tenets: replace ad-hoc prompting with a repeatable pipeline; persist all decisions in auditable on-disk artifacts (NOTES.md, spec.md, plan.md, tasks.md); enforce TDD via quality gates; manage/compact context to control token cost (the repo description and README explicitly advertise "token budgets" and "auto-compaction"); and make patterns reusable across features. It vendors itself INTO your codebase (no package.json dependency) so the workflow is owned and editable by your repo.

**How it works:** Standard pipeline of six phases: spec -> plan -> tasks -> implement -> optimize -> ship. Entry points scale by size: /quick (small fixes), /feature (focused features), /epic (multi-sprint orchestration). Phase slash commands: /spec, /plan, /tasks, /implement (test-first), /optimize (parallel quality gates: performance, security, accessibility, code review, coverage; docs also cite lint/type-check/unit tests/coverage delta/dead code/gap detection), /ship (staging/production deploy with validation/rollback). Setup/meta: /init-project, /init-preferences, /roadmap (GitHub Issues), /help, /review, /debug. Each phase spawns isolated subagents via Task(): spec/clarify/plan/tasks agents, worker/quick-worker, backend/frontend/database specialists, git-workflow-enforcer, and a multi-agent code-review voting ensemble. Artifacts: .claude/ (commands, agents, skills, hooks), .spec-flow/ (scripts, config, templates), specs/NNN-feature/, epics/NNN-epic/, docs/project/, plus CLAUDE.md. README advertises "auto-compaction" and "token budgets" but publishes no thresholds or numeric quality-gate pass/fail criteria (unconfirmed specifics).

**Claude Code integration:** Claude-Code-native but NOT distributed as a native Claude Code plugin/marketplace entry, and NOT an MCP server (verified: README/package.json describe a file-copy installer only; spec-flow does not appear in Anthropic's official claude-plugins-official marketplace). Integration is by file-copying: an npx installer (`npx spec-flow init`) writes Claude Code primitives directly into the project - slash commands (.claude/commands/, ~30), subagents (.claude/agents/ loaded on demand via Task()), skills (.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md as progressive-disclosure docs), and hooks (.claude/hooks/ as event handlers, thinly documented). It therefore uses Claude Code's slash-command + subagent + skills + hooks mechanisms, not the /plugin marketplace flow and not MCP. Multi-tool: primarily Claude Code; also installable as a Gemini CLI extension via `npx spec-flow install-gemini-extension` (same slash commands; agents/skills auto-adapted). No documented support for Cursor, GitHub Copilot, or OpenCode. Requires Claude Code with slash-command support; a Claude/Anthropic subscription or API access is implied but no specific tier is stated.

**Install:** Installer/maintenance CLI is run with npx (npm package `spec-flow`, bin `spec-flow` -> bin/cli.js, requires Node >=16): `npx spec-flow init` (copies .claude/, .spec-flow/, CLAUDE.md into the project), `npx spec-flow status` / `npx spec-flow status --check`, `npx spec-flow update`, `npx spec-flow install-gemini-extension`. NOTE (supply chain): package.json defines `"postinstall": "node bin/postinstall.js"`, so a maintainer-controlled script runs automatically on npm install; the package also ships bash/PowerShell setup scripts and has 6 runtime deps (chalk, colorjs.io, commander, fs-extra, inquirer, ora). Prerequisites: Git 2.39+, Python 3.10+, yq 4.0+; Windows users should install Git for Windows for full compatibility. After install you drive it from inside Claude Code (or Gemini CLI) with slash commands (e.g. /feature, /plan, /ship).

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained but a solo project. Owner is a USER account (@marcusgoll), not an org. Contributor stats (GitHub API, June 2026): marcusgoll 394 of 397 contributions (~99.2%); the remaining 3 are a single 'claude' commit, github-actions[bot], and marcusgoll-odin-orchestrator[bot]. Created 2025-10-04. High early release velocity (113 releases tagged through v11.9.1) but cadence has clearly slowed: a ~4-month gap between v11.9.0 (2025-12-16) and the latest release v11.9.1 (2026-04-23). Code last pushed 2026-04-23; repo metadata updated 2026-06-07; npm package version 11.9.1. Major versions carry breaking changes (e.g. v11.0.0 changed command architecture). Not archived/disabled. MIT licensed.

**Popularity:** Small/niche and confirmed low: ~88 GitHub stars, 9 forks, 1 open issue, 1 watcher/subscriber (GitHub API, ~June 2026). npm downloads are also low - approximately 415 downloads in the last 30 days (May 4 - Jun 2, 2026), with several zero-download days and a single ~147 spike. Has a blog/tutorial footprint around "spec-driven development for Claude Code" generally, but limited independent community footprint specific to Spec-Flow. (The Show HN thread often cited is a DIFFERENT tool - see verification_notes.)

**Best for:** Solo developers and small teams who already use Claude Code (or Gemini CLI) and want a disciplined, repeatable, document-first workflow for non-trivial features and multi-step work - especially those who value an auditable spec/plan/tasks trail and phase-gate review. Best when the work is large enough to justify the process (mid-sized features, epics) and the team is comfortable owning vendored workflow files and managing the Python/yq/Git/Node prerequisites. A poor fit for tiny one-off changes, teams wanting a maintained plugin with strong governance, or shops standardized on Cursor/Copilot/OpenCode.

**Risks:** Trust/maintenance: solo maintainer on a personal user account with low stars (~88) and low npm usage (~415/month) - real abandonment/bus-factor risk, and release cadence recently slowed (4-month gap before v11.9.1). No evidence of any rug-pull, malware, CVE, or malicious behavior specific to spec-flow, but it is unaudited by a large community. Supply chain (confirmed concrete): installation via `npx spec-flow` runs a maintainer-controlled CLI AND a `postinstall` script (bin/postinstall.js) automatically on npm install, plus bash/PowerShell setup scripts that write into your .claude/ and .spec-flow/ dirs - review/pin the version and inspect what it copies before trusting it in sensitive repos. Lock-in: workflow is effectively Claude-Code-specific (plus optional Gemini), and because files are vendored into your repo, deep adoption couples your process to this tool's conventions; migrating away or upgrading across breaking major versions can be painful. Complexity: multi-phase pipeline, many subagents, and several system prerequisites raise the learning curve and failure surface. Unverified claims: token-budget/auto-compaction and cost/performance benefits are advertised but not benchmarked or specified - treat as marketing until validated. MIT license means no warranty.

**Strengths:**
  - Opinionated end-to-end pipeline (spec -> plan -> tasks -> implement -> optimize -> ship) that reduces ad-hoc prompting
  - Vendors into your repo with no package.json dependency - you own, edit, and audit the workflow files
  - Auditable on-disk artifacts (spec.md/plan.md/tasks.md/NOTES.md) create a paper trail and enable phase-gate human review
  - Scales effort by size with /quick, /feature, /epic entry points
  - Uses native Claude Code primitives (slash commands, subagents, skills, hooks) rather than a heavy external runtime
  - Built-in quality-gate phase (security/perf/accessibility/coverage/code review) and a multi-agent code-review voting ensemble
  - MIT licensed; optional Gemini CLI extension for some cross-tool use
  - Maintainer is engaged and responsive (only 1 open issue), with a large body of releases

**Weaknesses:**
  - Single-maintainer (~99.2% one person on a personal account), no org governance - high bus-factor risk
  - Documentation is thin on mechanism: 'auto-compaction', 'token budgets', and quality-gate pass/fail criteria are claimed/advertised but no thresholds or numbers are published
  - No empirical benchmarks for its performance/cost claims (a recurring criticism of SDD-for-Claude tools generally)
  - Heavy ceremony/process can be overkill for small tasks where Claude Code's plan mode already suffices
  - Multiple external prerequisites (Python 3.10+, yq, Git 2.39+, Node 16+) and shell/PowerShell scripts add cross-platform setup friction
  - Breaking changes across major versions (e.g. v11.0.0) can disrupt installs since files are vendored into your repo
  - Not a native Claude Code marketplace plugin or MCP server - updates require re-running the npx installer
  - Largely Claude-Code-locked (only Gemini CLI otherwise); no Cursor/Copilot/OpenCode support
  - Low popularity (~88 stars, ~415 npm downloads/month) means limited community vetting, examples, and support
  - Release cadence has slowed (4-month gap before the latest release), a mild early-warning sign on momentum

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Adversarially re-checked the riskiest claims against live sources (June 2026). CONFIRMED via GitHub API: stars=88, forks=9, watchers/subscribers=1, open_issues=1, owner @marcusgoll is type User, license MIT, created 2025-10-04, pushed 2026-04-23, updated 2026-06-07, not archived/disabled. CONFIRMED releases: latest is v11.9.1 (2026-04-23), preceded by v11.9.0 (2025-12-16) - the ~4-month gap is real. CONFIRMED contributors: marcusgoll 394/397 (~99.2%); others are 1 'claude' commit + github-actions[bot] + marcusgoll-odin-orchestrator[bot]. CONFIRMED package.json: name spec-flow, version 11.9.1, bin spec-flow->bin/cli.js, engines node>=16, deps chalk/colorjs.io/commander/fs-extra/inquirer/ora, and a postinstall script `node bin/postinstall.js` (concrete supply-chain detail - now verified, previously only asserted). CONFIRMED integration mechanism from README: npx file-copy installer into .claude/ and .spec-flow/, uses slash commands/subagents/skills/hooks, NOT a /plugin marketplace entry (absent from anthropics/claude-plugins-official) and NOT an MCP server; Gemini CLI extension via `npx spec-flow install-gemini-extension` confirmed; prerequisites Git 2.39+/Python 3.10+/yq 4.0+ confirmed. NEW (gap filled): npm download stats ~415 in last 30 days (2026-05-04 to 2026-06-02) - the prior 'could not confirm downloads' gap is now closed and corroborates low/niche adoption. CONFIRMED caveat: Hacker News id=48231575 is 'Show HN: SDDW' by sermakarevich (github.com/sermakarevich/sddw, related tool 'fleet'), a DIFFERENT project - its critiques are about SDD-for-Claude generally and were NOT attributed to Spec-Flow. SECURITY: searches surfaced no malware/CVE/rug-pull/security advisory specific to spec-flow (only generic 2025-2026 npm supply-chain news unrelated to this package). STILL UNVERIFIED: any third-party security audit, real-world adoption beyond the repo/npm numbers, and the specifics of 'auto-compaction'/token-budget thresholds and quality-gate pass/fail rules (advertised but not documented). Net: the profile was accurate; corrections are additive (postinstall script confirmed; npm downloads added; description now explicitly says 'token budgets'; release-cadence slowdown noted). Confidence: high on externally verifiable facts; medium/low remains only on undocumented internal mechanisms.

### awesome-claude-code (hesreallyhim)
`https://github.com/hesreallyhim/awesome-claude-code`

**Category:** Curated "awesome list" / resource directory for Claude Code (NOT an installable framework, orchestrator, marketplace, or agentic-coding tool)

**One-liner:** A hand-curated, judgment-driven directory of third-party Claude Code resources (skills, subagents, hooks, slash-commands, status lines, orchestrators, CLAUDE.md files, tooling) — it is a reading list / index, not software you run or install.

**Philosophy:** "A delightfully curated collection of the finest of resources for the most excellent of agents, Claude Code" — emphasizing code quality, security, and originality, and serving both beginners and veterans. The guiding principle is hand-curated judgment over exhaustive aggregation: include only resources that demonstrably work and tag them by real-world purpose, with submitters required to provide verifiable claims and test prompts.

**How it works:** It is a GitHub "awesome list," so it has no runtime, phases, or commands of its own. The user-facing artifact is a README that links out to hundreds of community resources organized into roughly 10 top-level categories. Per the most complete reference available (a Track Awesome List mirror), those categories are: "Agent Skills"; "Workflows & Knowledge Guides" (incl. Teams, Ralph Wiggum); "Tooling" (IDE Integrations, Usage Monitors, Orchestrators, Config Managers); "Status Lines"; "Hooks"; "Slash-Commands" (Version Control, Code Analysis, Context Loading, Documentation, CI/Deployment, Project Management); "CLAUDE.md Files" (Language-Specific, Domain-Specific, Project Scaffolding); "Alternative Clients"; "Official Documentation"; and "Contributing". IMPORTANT/VERIFIED June 2026: the LIVE README's table of contents is currently a placeholder reading "I. TODO" followed by a note that "The previous Table of Contents was no longer fit for purpose, so a new organizational system is being prepared" — so the category list above is a recent-historical snapshot, not the current live structure. Internally the repo runs a CSV-first automation pipeline: THE_RESOURCES_TABLE.csv is the single source of truth, and the /scripts tree (now organized into subdirectories: readme/, validation/, resources/, badges/) contains generate_readme.py (builds README styles + live GitHub star stats from the CSV via templates), validate_links.py / validate_single_resource.py (URL + submission validation), sort_resources.py, parse_issue_form.py and create_resource_pr.py (turn a submission GitHub Issue into a PR), plus badge_notification.py (notifies accepted repos) — all seven scripts and the CSV-first design confirmed live. A github-actions[bot] account is the top committer (778 commits) reflecting this automation. As of June 2026 the repo is mid-migration to a "v3" structure (tag v2-pre-v3-cutover exists).

**Claude Code integration:** There is NO native technical integration. It is Claude-Code-focused in CONTENT but is not itself a plugin, MCP server, skill, hook, CLI, or npx/pip package, and (verified on the live README) it is NOT a plugin marketplace — there is no .claude-plugin/marketplace.json and no /plugin marketplace add flow. You do not "install" or "connect" it to Claude Code. It is a curated index whose LINKED entries integrate with Claude Code (those individual third-party items may be plugins, MCP servers, skills, hooks, or slash-commands, each with their own install method). The list is Claude-Code-only in scope (unlike cross-platform lists such as VoltAgent/awesome-agent-skills which also target Cursor/Copilot/etc.). Note: this repo is distinct from the several similarly named plugin-marketplace repos (e.g. jmanhype/awesome-claude-code, ccplugins/awesome-claude-code-plugins) that DO expose a .claude-plugin/marketplace.json — hesreallyhim's repo is not a marketplace.

**Install:** No install — it is a README/webpage you browse, not a package. Use it by opening the repo and following each linked resource's own install steps; contribute via the "[Resource]:" GitHub issue-form. No npm/npx/pip/plugin command exists for the list itself (verified against the live README, which contains no install instructions).

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained as of 2026 (verified). GitHub API: created 2025-04-19, last push to default branch 2026-04-27 (~6 weeks before this report on 2026-06-09); updated_at 2026-06-09. Not archived, not a fork. Governance is effectively SOLO: one human maintainer, hesreallyhim (276 human commits), assisted heavily by github-actions[bot] (778 commits); all other contributors have only 1-2 commits each (bus-factor ~1). No formal GitHub Releases exist (releases API returns an empty array — confirmed), but date-based tags are used (tags API returns: 2026.04.05, 2026.04.05.2, and a v2-pre-v3-cutover tag marking an in-progress reorganization). ~425 open issues — largely the resource-submission queue, consistent with the issue-form workflow rather than neglect.

**Popularity:** ~46,000 GitHub stars (46,054 via live GitHub REST API on 2026-06-09; the live repo page shows 46.1k), 4,009 forks, 308 watchers/subscribers, 425 open issues. It is one of the most-starred Claude Code resource lists and is widely cited as "the canonical hand-curated list." Caution CONFIRMED: secondary sources report wildly inconsistent figures — a June-2026 claudefa.st blog says 36.8k, a discussions snapshot says ~35.9k, and a stale Ecosyste.ms mirror still shows 1,156 stars from a 2025-06-25 sync (~12 months stale). The live GitHub API value of ~46k is authoritative; the lower figures are stale snapshots, not contradictions of fact.

**Best for:** Developers and teams already using (or evaluating) Claude Code who want a vetted starting point to discover skills, subagents, hooks, slash-commands, orchestrators, and CLAUDE.md patterns. Ideal as a discovery/bookmark layer rather than a dependency. Best for people comfortable evaluating and installing individual third-party tools themselves. Not appropriate for anyone expecting a turnkey installable framework, plugin bundle, marketplace, or orchestration engine.

**Risks:** ["Trust/endorsement risk: this is a pointer list; the actual security and supply-chain risk lives in each linked third-party repo (hooks, MCP servers, and plugins can execute arbitrary code in your environment) — vet each before installing, do NOT treat listing as a security audit. No malware, rug-pull, or supply-chain compromise has been reported against hesreallyhim's repo itself (verified: 2026 Claude Code security incidents — the source-code leak, fake-installer SEO-poisoning malware, and the Flatt 'one GitHub issue to break the supply chain' prompt-injection research — all concern Claude Code / anthropics/claude-code-action, NOT this list).", "Maintainer-side attack-surface note (NEW, inference not an incident): this repo's own model — accept untrusted contributor GitHub Issues and auto-generate PRs via GitHub Actions (parse_issue_form.py / create_resource_pr.py) — is the same general pattern shown to be exploitable via prompt-injection/poisoned issues in 2026 supply-chain research (GMO Flatt Security). No exploit of THIS repo is documented, but the pattern warrants the maintainer pinning actions and limiting workflow permissions.", "Bus-factor / abandonment risk: solo human maintainer; if hesreallyhim steps away, curation stops, though existing links remain.", "Name-collision risk: many similarly named repos exist (jmanhype, ccplugins, GiladShoham, Chat2AnyLLM, hekmon8, jqueryscript) — confirm you are on hesreallyhim's repo, and note that several look-alikes ARE plugin marketplaces while this one is not; do not 'add' a marketplace expecting it to be this list.", "Lock-in: essentially none for the list itself (it's just links); lock-in risk is per-resource.", "Complexity: none for consumers (just a webpage); the maintainer-side automation is moderately complex but invisible to users.", "License risk: CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 restricts commercial reuse and derivative redistribution of the curated list content (confirmed verbatim in LICENSE)."]

**Strengths:**
  - Editorial curation with judgment: items that don't work get cut and working items get tagged by what they actually do, unlike huge un-curated aggregator lists
  - Broad, well-categorized coverage of the whole Claude Code extension surface (skills, subagents, hooks, slash-commands, status lines, orchestrators, CLAUDE.md files, alternative clients)
  - Strong, transparent contribution + QA pipeline: structured issue-form submission, link validation (validate_links.py / validate_single_resource.py), claim/task verification checklist, and CSV-driven auto-generated README with live star counts — all seven pipeline scripts and the THE_RESOURCES_TABLE.csv source-of-truth confirmed in the live repo
  - Single best starting bookmark for discovering Claude-Code-specific tooling; tighter and more focused than cross-platform agent-skill lists
  - Genuinely active: bot-assisted maintenance, recent commits (push 2026-04-27), and an ongoing structural overhaul (v3)

**Weaknesses:**
  - It is only a directory — provides zero runtime capability, no install, no automation you can run; all real functionality lives in the third-party links
  - README is currently mid-reorganization: VERIFIED that the live main README table of contents is a placeholder literally reading 'I. TODO' with a note that a new organizational system is being prepared, so navigation/structure is in flux and the cleanest category map currently comes from a third-party mirror, not the live README
  - Curation and quality bar depend on a single human maintainer's bandwidth and taste (bus-factor of ~1; 276 human commits vs everyone else at 1-2)
  - Restrictive license for the list itself: CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 (no commercial use, no derivatives) — confirmed verbatim in the LICENSE file; limits reuse/forking/repackaging of the curated content
  - No semantic versioning or formal releases (releases API empty); only ad-hoc date tags (2026.04.05, 2026.04.05.2) plus a v2-pre-v3-cutover tag
  - Linked resources are third-party and vary in quality, maintenance, and security posture; inclusion is an endorsement signal, not a security guarantee

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** All riskiest claims re-checked live on 2026-06-09 against the GitHub REST API and live pages. CONFIRMED: stars 46,054 (API) / 46.1k (live page); forks 4,009; watchers/subscribers 308 (the earlier report's transient '46,054 watchers' artifact was a fetch error — the correct watcher count is 308); open issues 425; created 2025-04-19; pushed 2026-04-27; updated 2026-06-09; archived=false; fork=false; license NOASSERTION = CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 (verified verbatim in the LICENSE file). RELEASES: /releases API returns an empty array (no formal releases) — confirmed. TAGS: /tags API returns 2026.04.05, 2026.04.05.2, and v2-pre-v3-cutover — confirmed (note: the prior report's 'latest 2026.04.05' is slightly off; 2026.04.05.2 is the later same-day tag). CONTRIBUTORS: github-actions[bot] 778, hesreallyhim 276, all others 1-2 — solo+bot governance confirmed. INTEGRATION MECHANISM: live main README confirmed to (a) literally show 'I. TODO' as its table of contents with a 'new organizational system is being prepared' note, and (b) contain NO marketplace.json / no /plugin install / no npm/npx — it is a plain curated list, NOT a marketplace or installable tool. PIPELINE: all seven scripts (generate_readme.py, validate_links.py, validate_single_resource.py, sort_resources.py, parse_issue_form.py, create_resource_pr.py, badge_notification.py) and THE_RESOURCES_TABLE.csv source-of-truth confirmed live in /scripts (now nested in readme/, validation/, resources/, badges/ subdirs — a minor structural refinement vs the prior flat-'/scripts' description, consistent with the v3 reorg). SECURITY/TRUST: a dedicated search for malware/rug-pull/supply-chain compromise of hesreallyhim's repo found NONE; the 2026 Claude Code security stories (source leak, fake-installer credential-stealing malware via SEO poisoning, and GMO Flatt's 'Poisoning Claude Code: One GitHub Issue' research) all concern Claude Code itself or anthropics/claude-code-action, not this list — explicitly confirmed by reading the Flatt research, which never names awesome-claude-code. Added a maintainer-side attack-surface note (inference, not an incident) because this repo's untrusted-issue → auto-PR-via-Actions pattern resembles the class of vuln in that research. Stale/contradictory star numbers cross-checked and discarded: claudefa.st blog 36.8k, discussions ~35.9k, Ecosyste.ms mirror 1,156 (last synced 2025-06-25, ~12 months stale) — the live API ~46k is authoritative. Confidence raised to HIGH overall: identity, popularity, maintenance, integration mechanism (incl. live 'I. TODO' README state), license, pipeline, and absence of repo-specific security incidents are all directly verified; the only residual uncertainty is the exact current category layout, which is intentionally in flux due to the v3 migration.

### awesome-claude-code-toolkit (rohitg00)
`https://github.com/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit`

**Category:** Curated catalog / "awesome list" + Claude Code plugin marketplace aggregating community agents, skills, slash commands, hooks, MCP configs and plugins. NOT a single opinionated agentic-coding framework/workflow with a fixed phase pipeline.

**One-liner:** A large, actively-maintained "awesome list"/toolkit aggregating 135 agents, 35 curated skills (+400,000 via SkillKit), 42 commands, 176+ plugins, 20 hooks, 15 rules and 14-15 MCP configs for Claude Code — part original markdown content, mostly pointers to third-party community repos — packaged as a Claude Code plugin marketplace with monthly tagged releases.

**Philosophy:** Modular, role-based, community-aggregated. The stated design is to make Claude Code act as many specialized personas by offering a large menu of composable agents/skills/commands/hooks/MCP configs that users mix and match, plus to function as a central index/marketplace of the broader Claude Code ecosystem (including third-party projects). It optimizes for breadth and discoverability over a single prescriptive workflow.

**How it works:** It is primarily a repository of markdown definition files plus a marketplace manifest, not a runtime engine. Components: (1) agents/ — ~135 persona/subagent definition files in ~10 categories (core-development, language-experts, infrastructure, QA, data-&-AI, devex, specialized-domains, business-&-product, orchestration, research-&-analysis), each a markdown file describing persona + instructions + tool access; (2) commands/ — 42 slash-command-style markdown files grouped by topic (git, testing, architecture, documentation, security, refactoring, devops, workflow); sampled files (commands/workflow/wrap-up.md, commands/documentation/doc-gen.md) lacked strict YAML frontmatter, reading as prompt templates more than rigorously-formatted slash commands; (3) hooks/ — 20 lifecycle scripts (Bash/Python) for SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, PostToolUse, SessionEnd, etc.; (4) mcp-configs/ — 14-15 MCP server configuration references; (5) skills/, rules/ (15), templates/ (7), contexts/, examples/, plus 26 'companion apps' and ~52-53 ecosystem entries. A .claude-plugin/marketplace.json lists ~120 plugins (name/source/description/version) so the repo can be added as a Claude Code plugin marketplace. There is NO documented multi-phase pipeline (no fixed research->plan->implement->review phases) and no central orchestrator the toolkit itself runs — orchestration is delegated to individual third-party plugins/agents it catalogs (e.g. /fractal:run, /plt, /feat belong to listed external plugins, not the toolkit core). The repo IS cut into monthly tagged releases (v0.1.0 through v0.5.0) that bundle batches of merged community PRs.

**Claude Code integration:** Claude-Code-first, built around Claude Code's native extension points. Integrates as: (a) a native plugin MARKETPLACE — `/plugin marketplace add rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit` then `/plugin install <name>@<marketplace>`; (b) raw files copied into ~/.claude/ (commands, hooks/hooks.json, rules, CLAUDE.md templates, mcp-configs) via setup/install.sh; (c) agents referenced from your CLAUDE.md; (d) hooks wired into Claude Code lifecycle events; (e) MCP server configs. The README advertises multi-tool reach (Cursor, Copilot, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Windsurf, Cline, Aider, Continue, Amp), but that is mostly because many cataloged community plugins/MCP servers are tool-agnostic — the toolkit's own packaging (plugin marketplace, /plugin commands, ~/.claude layout, hooks.json) is specifically Claude Code's format. Treat it as Claude-Code-native with secondary, uneven multi-tool reach via individual entries. Integration is plugin/marketplace + raw-file + MCP + hooks — NOT a standalone CLI binary of its own.

**Install:** Plugin marketplace (recommended, lowest risk): in Claude Code run `/plugin marketplace add rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit` then `/plugin install <plugin>@awesome-claude-code-toolkit` — install only what you need rather than everything. Manual clone: `git clone https://github.com/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit.git ~/.claude/plugins/claude-code-toolkit` (you can also pin to a release tag, e.g. v0.5.0). One-liner (HIGHER RISK — pipes a remote script to a shell): `curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit/main/setup/install.sh | bash`. Per prior inspection the install.sh itself is benign (detects OS, prompts y/n before each step, copies markdown/JSON into ~/.claude/ subdirs for commands/hooks/rules/templates/mcp-configs; no sudo, no remote code exec, no repo clone, does not rewrite settings.json) — but curl|bash still means trusting whatever is at that URL at run time, so prefer the marketplace method or a tagged-release clone.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained and NOT abandoned. GitHub API (which responded this run) reports archived=false, disabled=false, created 2026-02-04, last push 2026-05-12T07:18:34Z (~4 weeks before the 2026-06-09 research date). Latest commits observed May 11-12, 2026 (merges of PR #387 llm-prices MCP, #388 Claude Skins, #389 html-anything). CORRECTION vs prior profile: there ARE formal versioned/tagged releases — five non-prerelease GitHub releases on a roughly monthly 'Community Batch' cadence: v0.1.0 (2026-02-04, Initial Release), v0.2.0 (2026-03-16, Ecosystem Refresh), v0.3.0 (2026-03-30), v0.4.0 (2026-04-09), v0.5.0 (2026-05-11, closes the open submission queue, 43 PRs). The flat '1.0.0' values in marketplace.json are per-plugin manifest versions, separate from the repo's git tags. Solo-owned but community-fed: owner is Rohit Ghumare (rohitg00), a personal account (not an org). Commits come from rohitg00, an automated 'rohit's agent'/'claude' bot, and many external contributors via PRs. Governance is benevolent-dictator/solo-maintainer with heavy reliance on community PRs. API open_issues_count is 107 (GitHub counts open issues + open PRs together) — consistent with the previously noted ~12 issues + ~95 PRs backlog, indicating curation can lag a fast-growing list.

**Popularity:** 2,003 GitHub stars, 642 forks, 17 watchers/subscribers as of 2026-06-09 (API-EXACT this run — the GitHub REST API responded, unlike the prior research environment where it was 403/rate-limited). open_issues_count=107 (issues + PRs combined). It was reported as the #1 trending GitHub project in February 2026. The maintainer is a well-known DevRel figure (2x Docker Captain, 3x CNCF Ambassador / CNCF Marketing Chair, 2x Google Developer Expert for Cloud & AI), which drives visibility. Note many headline 'star' numbers in the README (e.g. 9,900+, 21,000+, obra/superpowers 94,000+) belong to third-party plugins it links, not to this repo itself.

**Best for:** Claude Code users who want a curated DISCOVERY hub to find specific agents, skills, hooks, slash commands or MCP configs and cherry-pick one or two into their own setup. Good for tinkerers, DevRel/educators, and developers exploring what is possible with Claude Code's extension points. Poorly suited to teams wanting a single, supported, end-to-end agentic workflow with a guaranteed support chain — for that, a focused single-purpose framework or first-party tooling is safer (though, unlike the prior profile assumed, this repo DOES have stable monthly versioned releases).

**Risks:** ["Supply-chain/trust is the biggest risk: the catalog aggregates many third-party repos; installing those plugins/MCP servers runs other people's code/prompts with your Claude Code permissions. Vet each external entry independently before install — do not bulk-install.", "curl|bash install vector: the README's one-liner pipes a remote script to a shell; the URL content can change. Prefer the /plugin marketplace path or a tagged-release clone, or read install.sh first.", "Hooks execute Bash/Python on lifecycle events (PreToolUse/PostToolUse/SessionStart): a malicious or buggy hook can run arbitrary commands or exfiltrate data — review hook scripts before enabling.", "MCP configs may point at external services that receive your code/context — audit endpoints.", "Maintainer/continuity risk: solo personal-account ownership; if abandoned the many external links can rot. No evidence of a rug-pull, abandonment, or malware report specific to this repo as of June 2026 — repo is active (last push May 12, 2026), not archived, not disabled.", "Lock-in is LOW (plain markdown/JSON, easy to copy out; releases are tagged and pinnable) but COMPLEXITY/over-adoption is real: enabling many overlapping agents/hooks/commands can degrade and confuse agent behavior.", "Metric/marketing inflation (400,000 SkillKit skills, borrowed third-party star counts) can lead to over-trust; verify each component's real maintenance status yourself.", "General ecosystem context: AI-coding-agent supply chains saw real 2026 incidents (e.g. the axios npm compromise, Claude Code GitHub Action flaws) — relevant background, but none attributed to this toolkit."]

**Strengths:**
  - Breadth: one place to discover a huge range of Claude Code extensions (agents, skills, slash commands, hooks, MCP configs, plugins) across many domains and languages
  - Native Claude Code packaging: works via the official /plugin marketplace mechanism and the standard ~/.claude/ layout, so it slots in without bespoke tooling
  - Low-commitment adoption: install a single plugin from the marketplace instead of the whole thing; components are plain markdown/JSON, easy to read and audit before use
  - Actively maintained with a steady stream of community PRs, monthly tagged releases (v0.1.0-v0.5.0), and a credible, public maintainer (Rohit Ghumare) with strong open-source standing
  - Good for discovery/learning: agent persona files, hook examples and CLAUDE.md templates are useful references even if you do not adopt the whole catalog
  - Inspectable install path: setup/install.sh prompts before each step, needs no sudo, and does not rewrite global config; you can also pin a clone to a release tag
  - Apache-2.0 licensed and permissively reusable

**Weaknesses:**
  - It is a catalog/aggregation, not a cohesive framework: no single opinionated workflow, no fixed phase pipeline, no central orchestrator — you assemble your own setup from pieces
  - Much of the headline count (176+ plugins, many agents) is POINTERS to third-party repos by other authors, so quality, security and maintenance vary wildly per entry and are outside the maintainer's control
  - Inconsistent component quality/format: sampled command files (wrap-up.md, doc-gen.md) lack YAML frontmatter and are really prompt templates, so 'slash commands' is looser than it sounds; behavior depends on Claude interpreting prose
  - Inflated/round marketing metrics ('+400,000 skills via SkillKit', star numbers borrowed from third-party plugins) can overstate what the repo itself provides
  - Large PR backlog (~95 open PRs; API open_issues_count 107) suggests curation lag; some entries may be stale or unreviewed
  - Solo-owner / personal-account governance creates bus-factor and continuity risk if the maintainer steps away
  - Adopting many components at once adds context bloat and unpredictable agent behavior (competing personas, overlapping hooks)
  - Internal count inconsistencies in the README itself (e.g. 14 vs 15 MCP configs, 52 vs 53 ecosystem entries)

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-verified the riskiest claims against live sources on 2026-06-09. CORRECTIONS: (1) Popularity is now API-EXACT — the GitHub REST API responded this run (unlike the prior environment's 403): stargazers_count=2003, forks_count=642, subscribers_count=17, open_issues_count=107 (issues+PRs). Prior '~2,000 approximate' upgraded to exact 2,003. (2) RELEASES — prior profile said 'No formal versioned releases were confirmed... uncertain'; this was WRONG. API /releases and /tags both return five non-prerelease, non-draft tagged releases: v0.1.0 (2026-02-04), v0.2.0 (2026-03-16), v0.3.0 (2026-03-30), v0.4.0 (2026-04-09), v0.5.0 (2026-05-11), a clear monthly 'Community Batch' cadence. The marketplace.json '1.0.0' values are per-plugin manifest versions, distinct from repo git tags. CONFIRMED: archived=false, disabled=false, created 2026-02-04, last push 2026-05-12 (~4 weeks before research date) → actively maintained, not abandoned. Latest commits May 11-12, 2026 from rohitg00 + a 'rohit's agent'/'claude' bot + external contributors (basicScandal, benbencodes, Elon). Maintainer identity (Rohit Ghumare: 2x Docker Captain, 3x CNCF Ambassador/Marketing Chair, 2x Google Developer Expert) cross-confirmed via Docker, CNCF and LinkedIn. Integration mechanism confirmed from README: /plugin marketplace add (primary), git clone into ~/.claude/, and curl|bash install.sh one-liner; it is a catalog/toolkit, not a single framework. Headline counts confirmed from README (135 agents, 35 skills +400k via SkillKit, 42 commands, 176+ plugins, 20 hooks, 15 rules, 7 templates, 14-15 MCP configs, 26 companion apps, ~52-53 ecosystem entries). SECURITY: no malware, rug-pull, or supply-chain incident reports specific to THIS repo were found; search surfaced only general 2026 AI-coding-agent supply-chain coverage (axios npm compromise, Claude Code GitHub Action flaws) unrelated to this toolkit. Apache-2.0 license confirmed via API. Confidence raised to HIGH because the GitHub API was reachable this run, giving exact stats and definitive release/archive status.

**Sources:** https://github.com/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit · https://api.github.com/repos/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit · https://api.github.com/repos/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit/releases · https://api.github.com/repos/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit/tags · https://github.com/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit/releases · https://github.com/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit/commits/main · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rohitg00/awesome-claude-code-toolkit/main/README.md · https://github.com/rohitg00 · https://www.docker.com/captains/rohit-ghumare/ · https://www.linkedin.com/in/rohit-ghumare/ · https://www.cncf.io/people/ambassadors/ · https://claudefa.st/blog/tools/resources/awesome-claude-code

### Kiro
`https://kiro.dev (product). Public hub at https://github.com/kirodotdev/Kiro is an ISSUE TRACKER / docs + GitHub-automation repo, NOT the IDE/CLI source code (top level is .github/, docs/, scripts/, assets/, .kiro/specs/, README; the 95.6% TypeScript figure reflects automation scripts, not the product). Closed-source, by AWS/Amazon.`

**Category:** Standalone agentic AI IDE + terminal CLI (spec-driven development). NOT a Claude Code plugin/framework/skill.

**One-liner:** A closed-source, AWS-built standalone agentic IDE (VS Code / Code OSS fork) and CLI that enforces a spec-driven workflow (requirements to design to tasks), powered by Claude models via Amazon Bedrock. It is a peer/competitor to Claude Code, not an add-on for it.

**Philosophy:** "Specs as the unit of work": bring engineering rigor and structure to agentic coding so the developer steers the agent to author/review code via explicit, reviewable artifacts (requirements then design then tasks), rather than prompting code blindly. The aim is to move agentic AI from prototype 'vibe coding' to production-grade, team-aligned, auditable development.

**How it works:** Kiro centers on "spec-driven development." Instead of vibe-coding straight to code, you give it a feature request and it produces structured artifacts through a three-phase workflow:
1. Requirements - expands your prompt into user stories with acceptance criteria (EARS-style), written to requirements.md (or bugfix.md for bug specs).
2. Design - analyzes your codebase and writes a technical design with architecture/sequence diagrams to design.md, which you review/approve up front.
3. Tasks - breaks the design into discrete, trackable, executable tasks in tasks.md; it can run tasks one-at-a-time (stopping for review with clickable diffs) or run them concurrently. As of IDE v0.12 (May 2026) it explicitly supports "Parallel Task Execution."

Supporting concepts:
- Steering files: persistent project context as markdown in .kiro/steering/ (workspace) or ~/.kiro/steering/ (global). Three defaults: product.md, tech.md, structure.md. Inclusion modes via YAML front matter: Always, Conditional (fileMatch glob), Manual (#file reference in chat), and Auto (matched by description). Workspace overrides global.
- Agent Hooks: event-driven automations triggered by IDE events (on save, file create/delete, etc.) that run agent actions in the background, e.g. auto-update tests on save, run linters/security scans. v0.9 (Feb 2026) added Pre/Post Tool-Use hooks (validate/log around tool calls, filterable by read/write/shell/web).
- Custom Subagents + Agent Skills: as of IDE v0.9 (Feb 2026) you can define specialized subagents (markdown files with their own prompt/model/tool/MCP config, auto- or explicitly invoked) and use the open Agent Skills standard (portable instruction packages). This narrows the earlier gap vs Claude Code's subagents/skills.
- MCP: native MCP client support (local and remote). v0.11 (Mar 2026) added MCP Registry Governance / model governance for admins.
- Models (CURRENT, mid-2026): Claude Opus 4.8 (latest, May 2026), Opus 4.7, Opus 4.6 (1M context), Claude Sonnet 4.6, plus an "Auto" agent for cost/result optimization; free tier also exposes open-weight models (Qwen3 Coder Next, DeepSeek 3.2, MiniMax M2.5, GLM-5). Served via Amazon Bedrock. (At GA in Nov 2025 the named models were Sonnet 4.5 / Haiku 4.5 + Auto; these have since been superseded.)
- Artifacts all live in a .kiro/ project folder (specs/, steering/, hooks/, settings).

**Claude Code integration:** CRITICAL CLARIFICATION (RE-VERIFIED, still TRUE): Kiro does NOT integrate into Claude Code, and the premise that it is a "Claude Code framework/plugin" is incorrect. Kiro is a standalone product that COMPETES with Claude Code. Both run Claude under the hood (Kiro via Bedrock), but:
- There is NO native Claude Code plugin, NO Claude Code skill, NO npx package, NO Kiro-provided MCP server you install into Claude Code, and NO Kiro slash commands inside Claude Code.
- Kiro is its own IDE (Code OSS / VS Code fork) plus its own terminal CLI (kiro-cli). Its slash commands, specs, steering, hooks, subagents, and skills live inside Kiro itself, not Claude Code.
- The ONLY real interoperability path is the open Agent Client Protocol (ACP, JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio), which Kiro CLI added in v1.25 (released Feb 4, 2026 - NOT at GA). Running `kiro-cli acp` exposes Kiro as an ACP peer agent; Kiro extends ACP with _kiro.dev/-prefixed methods for its slash commands, MCP servers, and context compaction. Third-party ACP clients/orchestrators (Zed, JetBrains 2025.3.2+, Openclaw, Hermes) can drive both Kiro and Claude Code (via @agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp) as sibling ACP agents coordinated by an external tool, not one embedded in the other. The ACP Agent Registry (launched Jan 2026) lists both Kiro and Claude Code among 28+ agents as separate peers.
- A common community pattern is offloading coding to Kiro CLI via ACP (e.g. "cut Claude API token usage 60-80%" by billing work as Kiro Credits) while another agent routes - again, peer-to-peer, not a plugin.
- It is multi-tool only in the sense of ACP/MCP standards. It is NOT Claude-Code-specific in any direction.

**Install:** IDE: download installer from https://kiro.dev/downloads (macOS/Windows/Linux; a desktop app / VS Code fork, not an npm/CLI install). Current IDE version is 0.12 (May 6, 2026); the product is still on a 0.x version line. Sign in with social login, AWS Builder ID, or IAM Identity Center.
CLI: `curl -fsSL https://cli.kiro.dev/install | bash` (macOS, Linux, Windows). Current CLI version line is 2.x (v2.5, May 29, 2026). Then `kiro-cli` for interactive use, or `kiro-cli acp` (added v1.25, Feb 2026) to run as an ACP agent.
SECURITY NOTE on install version: older IDE builds carry known RCE/command-injection CVEs (see risks) - CVE-2026-0830 is fixed in 0.6.18+ and CVE-2026-10591 in 0.11+, so install/upgrade to a current build.
Note: There is no `npx kiro` and no Claude Code install path; you cannot `claude mcp add` Kiro itself (you add other AWS MCP servers TO Claude Code, which is unrelated to Kiro the product).

**Maintenance:** ACTIVELY maintained by AWS/Amazon (corporate org, not solo) - NOT abandoned. Timeline: public preview July 2025; General Availability November 17, 2025 (added the terminal CLI, IAM Identity Center / team features / admin console, checkpointing, property-based testing, multi-root workspaces, custom agents); re:Invent 2025 brought further updates. Release cadence is frequent and ongoing into mid-2026: IDE shipped v0.9 (Feb 5, 2026, custom subagents + agent skills + hook triggers), v0.10 (Feb 18), v0.11 (Mar 11, MCP/model governance), v0.12 (May 6, 2026, parallel task execution, quick plan); CLI shipped v1.25 (Feb 4, 2026, ACP integration), v2.1 (Apr 24), v2.5 (May 29, 2026). The kirodotdev/Kiro GitHub repo is a public issue tracker / docs + GitHub-issue-automation hub only (no product source), ~67 commits, ~252 forks, 36 watchers, large open-issue backlog (~2.9k open at fetch), ©2026 Amazon. The product is closed-source so there is no fork history; the related open repo is the demo app "spirit-of-kiro." Heritage: Kiro CLI is positioned as an evolution of the former Amazon Q Developer CLI.

**Popularity:** The kirodotdev/Kiro GitHub repo (issues/docs/automation only, not source) showed ~3.8k stars, ~2.9k open issues, ~252 forks, 36 watchers as of the June 2026 fetch. This star count reflects the feedback/issue repo, not a source-code repo (Kiro is closed-source), so it is a WEAK proxy for adoption. Real usage signal comes from AWS GA momentum, broad press coverage (InfoQ, SiliconANGLE, The Register, Caylent), a paid subscriber base, AWS Startups Pro+ promotions, and inclusion in the ACP Agent Registry - not from stars. Live numbers drift; treat as point-in-time.

**Best for:** Teams and developers building net-new features (especially on AWS) who want enforced engineering rigor, reviewable up-front specs, and shared team conventions/hooks - i.e. organizations trying to tame 'vibe coding' with structure. Strongest fit for AWS-centric shops and enterprises that can use IAM Identity Center (which also gets them out of telemetry/training-data collection and adds MCP/model governance). Poor fit for cloud-agnostic users, anyone wanting an open-source/self-hosted tool, or those who specifically want to EXTEND Claude Code (use Claude Code itself for fast iteration, refactors, and maintenance; if you want both, wire them together as separate peers over ACP).

**Risks:** ["Security CVEs in the IDE: CVE-2026-0830 (command injection from opening a maliciously crafted workspace, fixed 0.6.18, published Jan 9 2026) and CVE-2026-10591 (insufficient file-write restrictions to execution-sensitive paths like .vscode/tasks.json enabling arbitrary code execution, fixed 0.11, published Jun 2 2026, disclosed by Cymulate). Mitigation: stay on the latest build; both are patched in current versions.", "Supply-chain exposure (Kiro as a target, not the source): the June 2026 'Miasma'/Mini Shai-Hulud npm worm (compromised @redhat-cloud-services packages) was reported to inject hooks into AI developer tools including Kiro (alongside Claude, Codex, Gemini, Copilot, opencode). Kiro itself was not the malware; it is a victim/host environment - the real risk is malicious dependencies in your own project hooking the agent.", "Trust/privacy: by default, free and individual (social/Builder-ID) tiers send telemetry and may use code content for 'service improvement'/training; mitigated only by manually opting out or using enterprise IAM Identity Center.", "Vendor lock-in: closed-source, AWS Bedrock-backed, AWS identity and AWS-specific MCP 'Powers'; migrating away means leaving the IDE and its specs/steering conventions.", "Cost unpredictability: credit-based pricing with documented complaints and a real billing bug at launch; spec-heavy/parallel workflows can burn credits fast.", "Third-party/unofficial tooling: community proxies (e.g. kiro-gateway, AGPL) that route Kiro's free Claude access exist but are unofficial and carry their own ToS/security risk; do not confuse them with the official product.", "Reputational/governance context (DISPUTED, name-collision caution): early-2026 press (Financial Times) framed a 'Kiro' agent as having deleted a live AWS production environment / caused an AWS Cost Explorer interruption. Amazon publicly disputes the framing, attributing the limited single-region Cost Explorer interruption to user error (misconfigured access controls) that 'could occur with any developer tool, AI-powered or not,' and says no major services were affected. The more sensational write-ups appear speculative; treat as cautionary context, not a confirmed Kiro-product defect.", "Maturity risk: GA is recent (Nov 2025) and the IDE is still 0.x with a large open-issue backlog; behaviors and pricing have already shifted.", "Abandonment/rug-pull risk is LOW (backed by AWS, frequent releases, not a solo maintainer), but the flip side is full corporate control - features, pricing, and model access can change at AWS's discretion with no open-source escape hatch.", "Complexity: full IDE + spec ceremony + hooks + steering + subagents is more to learn and govern than a terminal agent for small tasks."]

**Strengths:**
  - Enforced structure: spec-driven workflow (requirements/design/tasks) front-loads planning and gives reviewable artifacts before code lands, reducing runaway 'vibe coding'
  - Persistent project memory via steering files (product.md/tech.md/structure.md) with flexible Always/Conditional/Manual/Auto inclusion
  - Event-driven Agent Hooks (including v0.9 Pre/Post Tool-Use hooks) automate tests/lint/security/validation on save or tool calls without manual prompting
  - Now has first-class Custom Subagents and the open Agent Skills standard (added IDE v0.9, Feb 2026), plus Parallel Task Execution (v0.12, May 2026) - closing the earlier gap vs Claude Code subagents
  - Familiar UX: full VS Code / Code OSS fork, so existing settings and Open VSX-compatible extensions carry over
  - Native MCP support (local + remote) plus an ACP server, so it interoperates as a peer agent and connects to external tools/data
  - Deep AWS ecosystem integration (Bedrock, IAM Identity Center, AWS-specific MCP servers / 'Powers'), strong for AWS shops
  - Team/enterprise features at GA: centralized admin, shared credits across IDE+CLI, consolidated billing, MCP/model governance (v0.11); paid/enterprise tiers auto opted-out of telemetry and content-for-training
  - Both IDE and terminal CLI share steering files and MCP config
  - Runs current frontier Claude models (Opus 4.8/4.7/4.6 1M-context, Sonnet 4.6) plus an Auto cost optimizer; free tier exposes open-weight models (Qwen3 Coder Next, DeepSeek 3.2, MiniMax M2.5, GLM-5)

**Weaknesses:**
  - Not a Claude Code add-on at all - if your goal is to extend Claude Code, Kiro does not do that; it is a separate competing IDE/CLI
  - Closed-source and proprietary (licensed as 'AWS Content'); you cannot inspect, self-host, or fork the engine
  - AWS lock-in: model serving via Bedrock, AWS auth/identity, AWS-centric MCP integrations; portability is limited to ACP/MCP standards
  - Documented IDE security vulnerabilities: CVE-2026-0830 (command injection via crafted workspace, fixed in 0.6.18) and CVE-2026-10591 (file-write tool RCE via .vscode/tasks.json, fixed in 0.11, disclosed by Cymulate) - users on old builds are exposed
  - Credit/pricing model has been contentious - spec requests historically cost more than vibe requests, tasks reported to silently consume multiple requests, and a confirmed billing bug over-consumed credits at launch
  - Free tier is thin (50 credits) - inadequate for sustained real work
  - Free-tier and individual social/Builder-ID subscribers are subject to telemetry AND content collection for 'service improvement' (potential training use) by default; you must manually opt out (enterprise IAM Identity Center is auto opted-out)
  - Spec/design/tasks ceremony is overkill for quick fixes/small tasks where Claude Code-style fast iteration is better
  - Early-product maturity: still on a 0.x IDE version line (0.12), with a large open-issue backlog and rapidly shifting features/pricing
  - Heavyweight: a full desktop IDE (or a separate CLI) rather than a lightweight terminal tool embedded where you already work

**Fact-check confidence:** High on core facts (standalone closed-source AWS IDE/CLI; spec/steering/hooks workflow; GA Nov 17 2025; Claude-via-Bedrock; ACP-based interop with Claude Code rather than being a plugin/skill/MCP-in-CC; ACP added in CLI v1.25 Feb 2026; actively maintained with frequent 2026 releases; ~3.8k stars / ~2.9k issues point-in-time; two real patched IDE CVEs). Medium on live-drifting numbers (exact stars/open-issues, current credit counts/pricing) and on the disputed FT 'AWS outage' story (Amazon's denial is documented but the full incident detail is contested). The premise that Kiro is a 'Claude Code framework/plugin' is confidently FALSE - it is a competing product.

**Verification notes:** Re-checked the four riskiest areas as instructed.
(1) STARS/POPULARITY - CONFIRMED & dated: direct GitHub fetch (June 2026) shows ~3.8k stars, ~2.9k open issues, ~252 forks, 36 watchers, 67 commits, TypeScript 95.6%. CORRECTED the repo characterization: the detailed file tree (.github/, docs/, scripts/, assets/, .kiro/specs/, README, CONTRIBUTING) confirms it is an ISSUE/DOCS/AUTOMATION hub, NOT product source code - one fetch loosely called it a 'source-code repo,' which is wrong; the 95.6% TypeScript is automation, not the IDE. Stars are a weak adoption proxy.
(2) MAINTENANCE - CONFIRMED active, NOT abandoned, and added concrete recent releases: IDE v0.12 (May 6 2026), v0.11 (Mar 11), v0.10 (Feb 18), v0.9 (Feb 5); CLI v2.5 (May 29 2026), v2.1 (Apr 24), v1.25 (Feb 4 2026). Noted the version schemes differ (IDE 0.x, CLI 1.x/2.x). Dated the ACP feature precisely to CLI v1.25 (Feb 4 2026), correcting the implication it might predate that.
(3) CLAUDE CODE INTEGRATION - RE-VERIFIED and unchanged: still ACP-only peer interop (kiro.dev/docs/cli/acp, dev.to ACP article, AWS Builder Center Openclaw article, Hermes issue, ACP Agent Registry Jan 2026). No plugin/skill/npx/MCP-into-CC. Premise that it's a 'Claude Code framework' remains FALSE.
(4) SECURITY/TRUST - SUBSTANTIAL NEW FINDINGS added: two real Kiro IDE CVEs verified at authoritative AWS security bulletins - CVE-2026-0830 (command injection, fixed 0.6.18, pub Jan 9 2026) and CVE-2026-10591 (file-write-tool RCE via .vscode/tasks.json, fixed 0.11, pub Jun 2 2026, credited Cymulate). Also added the June 1 2026 Miasma/Mini Shai-Hulud npm supply-chain worm that injects hooks into AI dev tools including Kiro (Kiro is a victim/host, NOT itself malware). Investigated the FT 'Kiro took down AWS' story: Amazon (aboutamazon.com) disputes it, calling it user error (misconfigured access controls), single service (Cost Explorer), one of 39 regions; the dramatized digitalbiztalk piece reads speculative/future-dated and is unreliable - logged as DISPUTED context, not a confirmed defect. No evidence of abandonment or rug-pull; AWS-backed.
OTHER CORRECTIONS: (a) MODELS were stale - profile said 'Sonnet 4.5 / Haiku 4.5'; those were the GA (Nov 2025) models. Current per kiro.dev/changelog/models: Claude Opus 4.8/4.7/4.6 (1M ctx), Sonnet 4.6, plus open-weight Qwen3 Coder Next, DeepSeek 3.2, MiniMax M2.5, GLM-5. (b) SUBAGENTS weakness was stale - IDE v0.9 (Feb 2026) added Custom Subagents and Agent Skills, and v0.12 added Parallel Task Execution, so the 'weaker subagent story' claim was downgraded/updated.

**Sources:** https://kiro.dev/ · https://kiro.dev/blog/general-availability/ · https://kiro.dev/changelog/ · https://kiro.dev/changelog/ide/ · https://kiro.dev/changelog/ide/0-9/ · https://kiro.dev/changelog/cli/ · https://kiro.dev/changelog/cli/1-25/ · https://kiro.dev/changelog/models/ · https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/acp/ · https://kiro.dev/pricing/ · https://github.com/kirodotdev/Kiro · https://github.com/kirodotdev/Kiro/tree/main
