### Agent OS (buildermethods/agent-os)
`https://github.com/buildermethods/agent-os`

**Category:** Spec-driven development / coding-standards framework for AI coding agents (markdown command files + bash installer; NOT an MCP server, daemon, or CLI package)

**One-liner:** A lightweight, file-based system that captures your team's coding standards and injects them into AI coding agents (optimized for Claude Code), plus a spec-shaping workflow that augments Claude Code's Plan Mode.

**Philosophy:** Core thesis: "Every time you prompt an AI coding agent, you're re-teaching it context that should already be known." Agent OS persists that context (standards, product mission, specs) as plain markdown so agents "build the way you would." v3 sharpens this into deliberate minimalism: do the things models are bad at (remembering your conventions, asking the right shaping questions) and step out of the way for things frontier models now do well (writing code, scaffolding, orchestration). Tool-agnostic by design via plain files; non-replacement (complements Claude Code/Cursor/etc. rather than being an IDE, MCP server, or agent runtime).

**How it works:** Agent OS is fundamentally a set of markdown "command" files plus standards/product/spec markdown documents, installed by bash scripts. There is no compiled binary, daemon, or server (independently confirmed: the repo is shell + markdown only, and the README/official docs describe it as a "lightweight system" where "all outputs are markdown files").

Three-layer context model: (1) Standards = how you build (coding conventions, patterns, tech-stack rules); (2) Product = what you're building (mission, roadmap); (3) Specs = detailed feature requirements.

v3.0 (Jan 2026) commands — confirmed by reading the repo's commands/agent-os/ directory directly (GitHub contents API): /discover-standards (mine patterns from an existing codebase into documented standards), /index-standards (build index.yml so standards are discoverable), /inject-standards (surface relevant standards into the current context — conversation, a Claude Skill, a custom command, or a spec/plan; can inject inline content or @-path references), /plan-product (define product mission/roadmap), /shape-spec (ask targeted, standards-aware questions to strengthen a spec — explicitly designed to augment Claude Code's Plan Mode rather than replace it). The retired commands (write-spec, create-tasks, implement-tasks, orchestrate-tasks) are verified ABSENT from the v3 directory.

Major v3 philosophy shift (confirmed in CHANGELOG and Discussion #310): the older heavy build phases were RETIRED. Agent OS deliberately defers spec authoring to Plan Mode and defers implementation/orchestration/scaffolding to the frontier model, narrowing its own scope to 'establish standards + inject standards + shape specs.' The stated rationale: "Claude Code's plan mode, extended thinking, and improved models now handle much of the scaffolding that earlier versions provided."

Artifacts/files created: base install ~/agent-os/ (profiles/, scripts/, commands/, config.yml). Project install creates an agent-os/ folder in the project (standards/, product/, specs/, index.yml) and, for Claude Code, .claude/commands/agent-os/*.md (the slash commands) and optionally Claude Code Skills generated from your standards. NOTE: the top-level v3 config.yml read from the repo is minimal (version: 3.0, default_profile, commented profile-inheritance examples); the older v2 toggle flags (claude_code_commands, standards_as_claude_code_skills, use_claude_code_subagents) no longer live in the root config and appear to be handled at the profile level in v3 — exact placement not hands-on verified.

**Claude Code integration:** Claude Code is the first-class, "optimized" target, but Agent OS is intentionally tool-agnostic at the file level. Integration mechanisms (confirmed against repo + official docs):
- Slash commands: project install copies command markdown into .claude/commands/agent-os/, exposing /discover-standards, /index-standards, /inject-standards, /plan-product, /shape-spec natively in Claude Code.
- Skills: since v2.1 (released 2025-10-21, default-on in 2.1+), standards can be converted into Claude Code Skills so Claude auto-applies them when relevant instead of injecting into every prompt. An /improve-skills helper optimizes skill descriptions.
- Subagents: Agent OS no longer SHIPS its own subagents (the v1.x test-runner/context-fetcher/git-workflow/file-creator/project-manager agents were removed in the v3 refocus). It can delegate to Claude Code subagents, and /inject-standards can bake standards into subagents/skills/custom commands/any prompt.
- Plan Mode: /shape-spec is explicitly built to enhance Claude Code Plan Mode.
- Hooks: NONE. No evidence Agent OS installs or relies on Claude Code hooks (confirmed: not in CHANGELOG, config.yml, or docs).
- MCP server: NONE. This is a CONFIRMED, widespread misconception. The repo is 100% shell + markdown with no MCP component, and the official docs/README describe it as a file-based system, not a server. At least one third-party article (Tim Wang, Medium) explicitly and verbatim calls Agent OS 'A model context protocol (MCP) server that enables communication and orchestration among agents' — this is FALSE, and worse, AI-generated search summaries are now amplifying the same error (repeatedly describing Agent OS as 'a framework and server' for multi-agent orchestration). Treat any 'Agent OS = MCP server / orchestration server' claim as incorrect.
- npx/CLI package: NONE. Not distributed via npm/npx; install is git clone (or ZIP download) + bash scripts.

Multi-tool: explicitly NOT Claude-Code-only. Docs position it to work with "any AI coding tool that can read file references" (Claude Code, Cursor, etc.). For non-Claude tools, users @-reference the same command/standards files instead of using native slash commands. Claude Code's advantage is convenience (native slash commands + Skills), not exclusive capability.

**Install:** No npx/npm. Bash-based (Windows requires WSL or Git Bash — explicitly stated in the official installation docs).
Base install (run once, in home dir):
  cd ~
  git clone https://github.com/buildermethods/agent-os.git && rm -rf ~/agent-os/.git
  (alternative: download the GitHub ZIP and place it in ~/agent-os)
Project install (run inside each project):
  ~/agent-os/scripts/project-install.sh
Variants:
  ~/agent-os/scripts/project-install.sh --profile rails   (use a named standards profile)
  ~/agent-os/scripts/project-install.sh --commands-only    (refresh commands, preserve existing standards)
Config lives in config.yml. The v3 root config (read from the repo) is minimal: version: 3.0, default_profile: default, with commented optional profile-inheritance examples. Older v2 flags (claude_code_commands, use_claude_code_subagents, agent_os_commands, standards_as_claude_code_skills) are referenced in v2 docs/CHANGELOG; in v3 they no longer appear in the root config and are believed to be profile-scoped. NOTE: there are open community reports (Discussion #311 'Fresh install triggers no actual install', #184 Windows/Git Bash failures) of install friction, so the installer is not always smooth on fresh or Windows environments.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained, but solo-dominated. Created 2025-07-16; last code push 2026-05-05 (API-confirmed); not archived; not disabled. Latest release is Agent OS v3.0.0, published 2026-01-20 — NOTHING has been released since (so as of 2026-06-09 the most recent tagged release is ~5 months old, though commits continued to May 2026). Release cadence: frequent through v1.x (Jul–Aug 2025) and v2.x (v2.0.0 Oct 7 2025 → v2.1.1 Oct 28/29 2025), then a ~3-month gap to the v3.0 major (Jan 2026). Governance is effectively SOLO: contributor stats (GitHub API) show CasJam (Brian Casel) with 42 commits (~79% of all contributions) and 16 other contributors making mostly one-off PRs (11 commits total). The GitHub account 'buildermethods' is an Organization-type account, but it functions as a personal/educational brand ('Builder Methods'), not a multi-maintainer org or foundation — direction depends on one person. MIT licensed. There is a paid 'Builder Methods Pro' community plus paid courses (Claude Code Course, etc.); the framework itself is free and open source. Key-person risk is real. Open issues = 0, but this is MISLEADING: the project routes support to GitHub Discussions (where there are open install/bug threads), so 0 open issues reflects an unused issue tracker, not a defect-free project.

**Popularity:** ~4,800 GitHub stars (4,802 confirmed via GitHub API on 2026-06-09), 760 forks, 106 watchers (per the GitHub web UI; the API's subscribers_count field returned 4,802, which is the known GitHub-API quirk of echoing the star count — the true watcher count is 106), 0 open issues, 17 total contributors. Mid-tier popularity and clearly growing (an independent comparison snapshot showed only ~3.4k stars earlier in 2026, vs 4.8k now). Well-known in the spec-driven-development niche and frequently compared against GitHub Spec Kit, OpenSpec, BMAD, and Kiro, but a smaller community than the leaders — notably OpenSpec (~52k stars as of June 2026 per one comparison) and GitHub Spec Kit.

**Best for:** Teams and multi-project organizations that already have (or want to codify) coding standards and conventions and want them consistently enforced across AI agents — especially Claude Code users who want standards auto-applied as Skills and better spec shaping in Plan Mode. Also strong for modernizing legacy/pre-AI codebases by extracting tribal knowledge into documented standards. Less suited to solo devs wanting a zero-config minimal tool (OpenSpec is lighter), native-Windows devs unwilling to use WSL/Git Bash, or those wanting full task-breakdown/orchestration baked in (v3 deliberately dropped that — Spec Kit or BMAD fit that better).

**Risks:** Security: relatively low surface — no server/daemon, no MCP, no network service, no hooks installed; it's local markdown + bash scripts. The main caution is standard supply-chain hygiene: the installer is git clone (or ZIP) + bash run against your home dir and project, and it writes into .claude/, so review the scripts before running (true of any 'clone and run a shell script' tool). No malware, CVEs, or rug-pull reports were found specific to this repo as of mid-2026 — the only negative signal is routine install friction (Windows/Git Bash, fresh-install no-op) reported in Discussions, not a security issue. Trust/abandonment: single-maintainer-dominated project — risk of stalled maintenance or unilateral direction changes (and the release cadence has already slowed: no tagged release since v3.0.0 in Jan 2026, though commits continued into May 2026). Mitigated by the MIT license and the fact that artifacts are plain markdown you fully own (you can keep using them even if the project dies). Lock-in: minimal — everything is portable markdown; the main 'lock-in' is conceptual (your standards/specs structured Agent OS's way) plus Claude-Code-specific conveniences (Skills/slash commands) that other tools access via @-references. Complexity / mental-model risk: moderate — the biggest practical pitfall is mismatched expectations from outdated docs/blogs describing retired v1/v2 features (shipped subagents, full orchestration) OR the actively-propagating false claim that Agent OS is an MCP/orchestration server, which it is not.

**Strengths:**
  - Standards-first: turns tribal knowledge / existing codebase patterns into reusable, version-controlled markdown standards (good for legacy modernization and team consistency)
  - Plain-markdown, file-based design = highly portable across tools and trivially diffable/reviewable in git; minimal lock-in surface and minimal attack surface (no server/daemon/MCP/network service)
  - First-class Claude Code integration: native slash commands plus auto-applied Skills generated from your standards (context-efficient vs. stuffing every prompt)
  - v3 is refreshingly humble in scope — defers spec authoring to Plan Mode and implementation to the model, reducing bloat and token waste vs. heavier frameworks like BMAD (which can run hundreds of millions of tokens/week)
  - MIT-licensed, free, with a clear public CHANGELOG and disciplined major-version releases
  - Profiles + inheritance let multi-project orgs share and specialize standards

**Weaknesses:**
  - Solo-dominated governance (Brian Casel / CasJam = ~79% of commits) — real bus-factor and continuity risk despite the org-named GitHub account
  - Frequent breaking changes across majors: v2 retired the 'Roles' system; v3 retired the entire write-spec/create-tasks/implement/orchestrate workflow AND the shipped subagents — v2 muscle memory, setups, and most blog tutorials don't carry forward
  - Higher setup complexity than peers: two-step (base + project) install, plus concepts (profiles, standards index, config flags) to learn; an independent comparison rated its setup complexity 'Higher' vs OpenSpec 'Minimal' / Spec Kit 'Medium'
  - Bash-only installer; Windows needs WSL or Git Bash, and there are real open community reports of install failures (fresh-install no-op #311; Windows/Git Bash #184)
  - Smaller community/ecosystem than OpenSpec and GitHub Spec Kit; fewer third-party integrations
  - Requires upfront investment to define standards before you get value — opinionated and not zero-config
  - Online sources are inconsistent/outdated/wrong — many describe retired v1/v2 subagents & orchestration, and several (including AI-generated summaries) wrongly call it an MCP/orchestration server, making it very easy to form a wrong mental model
  - No releases since v3.0.0 (Jan 2026) as of mid-2026 — slower release cadence than the v1/v2 era; '0 open issues' is misleading because support runs through Discussions

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Independently re-verified the riskiest claims on 2026-06-09. GitHub API (api.github.com/repos/buildermethods/agent-os): 4,802 stars, 760 forks, 0 open issues, archived=false, disabled=false, created 2025-07-16, pushed 2026-05-05, license MIT, owner type=Organization (CORRECTION vs original profile's implication of a personal account — though governance is still effectively solo). WATCHERS CLARIFIED: API subscribers_count returned 4,802 (the known GitHub-API quirk where it echoes stargazers), but the GitHub web UI shows Watchers: 106 — so the original profile's '106 watchers' is correct; I corrected the methodology note. CONTRIBUTORS (API): CasJam=42 commits (~79%), 16 others mostly one-off — confirms and slightly refines 'effectively solo' (it's solo-DOMINATED, not literally one person). Releases page + CHANGELOG: latest tagged release v3.0.0 on 2026-01-20, NOTHING newer (added this as a cadence/staleness nuance). v3 command set read directly from the repo contents API (discover-standards, index-standards, inject-standards, plan-product, shape-spec present; write-spec/create-tasks/implement-tasks/orchestrate-tasks confirmed ABSENT). config.yml read from raw.githubusercontent: minimal v3 file (version 3.0, default_profile), NO MCP/hooks, and the older v2 toggle flags are NOT in the root config (corrected: they're v2-era / likely profile-scoped in v3). MCP-SERVER MISCONCEPTION CONFIRMED AS FALSE: Tim Wang's Medium article verbatim calls Agent OS 'A model context protocol (MCP) server that enables communication and orchestration among agents' — directly contradicted by the repo (shell+markdown, no MCP) and official docs; additionally found that AI-generated search summaries are now amplifying this same error, so I strengthened the warning. softwarethug comparison: rates Agent OS setup 'Higher' vs OpenSpec 'Minimal'/Spec Kit 'Medium', describes it (correctly) as a file-based system, lists ~3.4k stars (older snapshot, consistent with growth to 4.8k). Maintainer confirmed: Brian Casel / Builder Methods (official site footer '© 2026 Builder Methods. Built by Brian Casel'). SECURITY: searched for malware/CVE/rug-pull/supply-chain reports specific to agent-os — found NONE; only generic AI-supply-chain articles and routine install-friction Discussions (#311 fresh-install no-op, #184 Windows/Git Bash). Added a correction that '0 open issues' is misleading because support is routed to GitHub Discussions. Confidence HIGH on identity, license, stars/forks/watchers/contributors (API-verified), v3 command set and config (read from repo), maintainer, install method, integration model (slash commands + Skills, no MCP, no hooks), and the MCP misconception. Medium only on exact runtime behavior of Skills generation/subagent delegation and the precise v3 location of legacy config flags (not hands-on tested).

### BMAD-METHOD (Breakthrough Method for Agile AI-Driven Development)
`https://github.com/bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD`

**Category:** Agentic spec-driven development framework / methodology (multi-agent SDLC orchestration layer for AI coding assistants)

**One-liner:** An open-source, role-based multi-agent methodology that overlays a full agile SDLC onto AI coding assistants like Claude Code: specialized agents (analyst, PM, architect, scrum master, dev, QA/test, UX) run structured workflows that produce planning artifacts (PRD, architecture, epics, stories) the coding agent then implements against.

**Philosophy:** "Spec-driven development, not vibe coding": keep a human in the loop and ground the AI in durable planning documents produced by role-specialized agents that mirror a real product team. Adaptive depth (scale-domain-adaptive) so heavyweight process only applies where complexity warrants. AI as expert facilitator/collaborator and guide, not a replacement for human thinking or architectural judgment.

**How it works:** BMAD is fundamentally a methodology + a library of agent definitions, workflows, and templates, distributed as files, not a runtime daemon. Design philosophy: "spec-driven development" over "vibe coding" — keep a human in the loop, ground the AI in persistent planning documents, and mirror a real product team with role-specialized agents. It is "scale-domain-adaptive": planning depth scales with project complexity (a lightweight "Quick Flow" path for small work vs. the full method for larger projects).

v6 architecture is organized into MODULES. The OFFICIAL v6 module set (verified against docs.bmad-method.org/reference/modules and DeepWiki, June 2026) is: bmm (BMad Method core SDLC, ~9 agents with standardized menus: pm/John, analyst/Mary, architect/Winston, dev/Amelia, ux-designer/Sally, tech-writer, sm/scrum-master, tea, quick-flow-solo-dev; plus a built-in QA agent Quinn — note marketing also says "12+ domain experts"); bmb (BMad Builder, a meta-module for authoring your own agents/workflows/modules); tea (Test Architect — a SEPARATE module providing the Murat agent and ~9 risk-based test-strategy/traceability workflows that SUPPLEMENT the built-in Quinn QA agent — it is NOT folded inside bmm); cis (Creative Intelligence Suite, ~6 agents: innovation-strategist, design-thinking-coach, creative-problem-solver, brainstorming-coach, presentation-master, storyteller); and bmgd (Game Dev Studio — game-dev workflows for Unity/Unreal/Godot, mirroring the four-phase method with game-specific artifacts like GDDs). There is also internal "core" shared tasks / a BMad Master orchestrator, but "core" is a shared internal layer rather than a user-selected module. Each module contains agents/, workflows/, and tasks/.

FOUR-PHASE LIFECYCLE: (1) Analysis/Discovery — analyst + CIS brainstorming produce a product brief; (2) Planning — PM agent produces a PRD (output PRD.md), or Quick Flow produces a lightweight tech-spec and jumps straight to implementation; (3) Solutioning — Architect produces system architecture and ADRs (in v6, epic/story breakdown happens AFTER architecture so work breakdown is technically informed); (4) Implementation — Scrum Master breaks the spec into epics and individual story files, the Developer agent implements story-by-story referencing the spec/story files (not chat history), and a QA/test agent validates against acceptance criteria. Artifacts are persistent markdown, segregated in v6 into planning_artifacts and implementation_artifacts directories (PRD/architecture vs. stories), plus an adversarial code-review workflow. "Party Mode" loads multiple agents into one chat to debate trade-offs. A bmad-help / bmad-spec skill gives just-in-time guidance (v6.8.0 added a bmad-spec skill distilling intent into a five-field spec kernel). Config lives in TOML — _bmad/config with a user-level config.user.toml.

Note: agent counts vary by source (9, 12+, 19+) because they count different module combinations and versions — treat exact counts as approximate.

**Claude Code integration:** BMAD is multi-tool, NOT Claude-Code-only — it targets a broad set of assistants (README cites "Claude Code, Cursor, etc."; DeepWiki cites "over 30 AI IDE platforms") plus web bundles for Google Gemini Gems and ChatGPT Custom GPTs (for planning, shipped as web-bundles-v1.0.0 on 2026-05-25). It is tool-agnostic by design: agents/workflows are plain markdown the host AI reads.

OFFICIAL Claude Code mechanism (CORRECTED — verified against docs.bmad-method.org/reference/commands, June 2026): the official installer writes NATIVE Claude Code Skills — a directory-per-skill layout where each skill is a subdirectory containing a SKILL.md file, written into an IDE-specific directory INSIDE YOUR PROJECT at .claude/skills/ (e.g. .claude/skills/bmad-help/SKILL.md, .claude/skills/bmad-prd/SKILL.md, .claude/skills/bmad-agent-dev/SKILL.md). The directory name IS the skill name; all skills use a bmad- prefix. Skills are generated at install time from module manifests; re-run the installer if modules change. The official docs explicitly do NOT describe MCP servers OR slash commands OR hooks for the Claude Code integration — invocation is via the native Skills mechanism. (Earlier v6 installs used .claude/commands/ slash-command files, which hit Claude Code's nested-subdirectory discovery limitation — official issue #773 — and the project migrated to the native Skills format.)

DISAMBIGUATION (vindicated and important): web searches heavily surface THIRD-PARTY ports whose specifics get mis-attributed to the official tool. Specifically, claims of "15 slash commands" (e.g. /workflow-init, /prd, /dev-story), a global "~/.claude/" install layout, install-v6.sh/install-v6.ps1 git-clone installers, "Memory integration," and "Hooks" belong to aj-geddes/claude-code-bmad-skills, NOT the official bmad-code-org distribution. Other community projects: PabloLION/bmad-plugin (a Claude Code /plugin-marketplace plugin advertising "30+ skills") and 24601/BMAD-AT-CLAUDE. These are unofficial; their feature claims (hooks, ~/.claude/skills global layout, specific command lists, "70-85% token savings") must NOT be attributed to the official tool. Install official BMAD via npx bmad-method install and let it generate Claude Code config; treat third-party plugins separately and vet them independently.

**Install:** Official install (interactive): npx bmad-method install
Prerelease channel: npx bmad-method@next install
Non-interactive / CI, scoped to Claude Code + the core method module: npx bmad-method install --directory /path/to/project --modules bmm --tools claude-code --yes
The npm package is "bmad-method" (bin: bmad / bmad-method → tools/installer/bmad-cli.js), latest published v6.8.0, MIT, no deprecation notice.
Prerequisites (per README): Node.js v20.12+, plus Python 3.10+ and the uv package manager (used by parts of v6). After install, the installer writes native Claude Code Skills into .claude/skills/ (subdir-per-skill SKILL.md); open the project in Claude Code (restart if needed) and invoke the bmad- skills. (Community alternatives exist — a Claude Code /plugin marketplace plugin from PabloLION and git-clone skills ports from aj-geddes and 24601 — but these are unofficial and should not be confused with the npx installer.)

**Maintenance:** Very actively maintained — CONFIRMED. Latest stable release v6.8.0 published 2026-05-25 (release notes: replaces single UX skill with DESIGN.md + EXPERIENCE.md, adds web bundles for Gemini Gems/ChatGPT, adds a bmad-spec skill and 19 new elicitation techniques, strengthens activation guardrails across 23+ skills, deprecates bmad-distillator); web-bundles-v1.0.0 same day; v6.7.1 on 2026-05-18 (patch fixing installer module-resolution after the experimental "baut"→"automator" rename) following v6.7.0 (2026-05-17). Repo pushed_at 2026-06-09 (the research date), so commits land essentially daily; not archived, not disabled. Uses semantic-release (semantic-release-bot is a top-3 committer). Governance — CONFIRMED: founder-led under a real legal entity, BMad Code, LLC, created by Brian "BMad" Madison (25+ yrs eng — Northrop Grumman/NASA & military sims, Siemens IoT; currently Sr Eng Manager leading AI SDLC transformation at Extend). Contribution is NOT solo: top contributor is "alexeyv" (402 commits, ~47% of human commits), founder bmadcode (156, ~18%) second, then semantic-release-bot (91, automated), muratkeremozcan (61), dracic (21), and a long tail — so a small active maintainer group with moderately concentrated human contribution (top 2 humans ≈ 65% of human commits) and a real key-person/bus-factor dependency. Docs at docs.bmad-method.org, active Discord/YouTube (@BMadCode). License: MIT (Copyright 2025 BMad Code, LLC) plus a trademark clause: "BMad and BMAD-METHOD are trademarks of BMad Code, LLC" (code is MIT-permissive; the names/marks are not). GitHub API reports the SPDX license as NOASSERTION because of the added trademark text, but npm and the LICENSE file declare MIT.

**Popularity:** Very high — CONFIRMED via GitHub API (June 2026): 48,839 stars, 5,670 forks, 59 open issues, created 2025-04-13 — i.e. ~49k stars in roughly 14 months, one of the most-starred agentic-coding methodologies. (Watcher/subscriber count: the API's subscribers_count field returned a value equal to the star count, a known GitHub quirk; the profile's earlier "~398 watchers" figure is unverified and should be treated as approximate/uncertain.) Large media/blog/podcast footprint (Medium, DEV, Tech Lead Journal, Nearform) and an active Discord. Frequently appears in 2026 "best spec-driven dev tools" roundups alongside Kiro and GSD.

**Best for:** Teams and solo developers doing greenfield, non-trivial projects who want disciplined, spec-driven, auditable AI development and are willing to invest in upfront planning (PRD + architecture + stories). Best when you value predictability and documentation over raw speed. A good fit for engineers who already think in agile/PM terms and want to keep a human firmly in the loop, and increasingly for game dev (via the BMGD module) and test-strategy-heavy work (via TEA). Poor fit for quick scripts, tiny features, one-off changes, or pure non-technical 'no-code' users — the process overhead dominates and the quality gates assume technical judgment.

**Risks:** ["Lock-in: LOW on tooling (tool-agnostic, MIT, plain-markdown artifacts you keep) but MEDIUM on process/mental-model — adopting it deeply reshapes your workflow and folder structure (_bmad config, .claude/skills, planning_artifacts/implementation_artifacts) and v6 was a disruptive rework, so version churn can force migrations.", "Key-person / governance risk: MEDIUM — founder-led under BMad Code, LLC with a small maintainer group; human commit activity concentrated (top contributor alexeyv ~47%, founder bmadcode ~18%). Not a faceless/anonymous project (low rug-pull risk — real identity, real entity, public LinkedIn/podcasts), but bus-factor is real and there is no broad multi-org governance. No evidence of abandonment — actively shipping (v6.8.0 May 2026, near-daily commits).", "Security/trust: Installs via npx (executes maintainer JS on your machine) and writes skill files the AI will execute as instructions — standard supply-chain exposure for an npm package; pin versions and review what it writes. The official package shows NO published GitHub security advisories and a formal security policy (covers prompt-injection, path-traversal, supply-chain). HIGHER caution for UNOFFICIAL third-party Claude Code ports/plugins (aj-geddes, PabloLION, 24601) — vet those independently before granting skills/hooks/plugin access; some request a global ~/.claude install and hooks the official tool does not use.", "Quality risk: the dev-loop and adversarial-review weaknesses (issues #2003, #1332) mean outputs still require careful human review; do not treat agent sign-off as real QA.", "Trademark: code is MIT but BMad / BMAD-METHOD names are trademarked by BMad Code, LLC — fine to use the tool, but don't rebrand a fork as 'BMad'. (GitHub reports license as NOASSERTION due to the trademark clause, though it is MIT-licensed code.)", "Verification caveats: exact agent counts (9 vs 12+ vs 19+), token-savings percentages, and the '30+ IDEs' figure come from docs/blogs of mixed reliability; several specific feature claims circulating online actually describe third-party ports, not the official tool."]

**Strengths:**
  - Imposes real structure on AI coding: persistent markdown PRD/architecture/story artifacts give the agent something durable to anchor to instead of fragile chat context, improving predictability and reducing rework (corroborated by independent practitioners)
  - Genuinely multi-tool/portable — Claude Code, Cursor, and many other IDEs, plus Gemini Gems / ChatGPT Custom GPTs web bundles — so you are not locked to one assistant; agents are plain markdown
  - Role-specialized agents (analyst, PM, architect, scrum master, dev, QA/test, UX) mirror a real team and force separation of planning from implementation; v6 sequences story breakdown AFTER architecture
  - Scale-adaptive: a lightweight 'Quick Flow' path exists for small tasks vs. the heavy full method for large projects
  - v6 integrates with Claude Code via the NATIVE Skills format (subdir-per-skill SKILL.md in .claude/skills/) rather than bespoke hacks — more future-proof and lower-friction than the old nested-slash-command approach (which hit Claude Code's discovery limitation, issue #773)
  - Extensible by design: the BMB 'Builder' module lets you author your own agents/workflows/modules; official extension modules now include Test Architect (TEA) and Game Dev Studio (BMGD)
  - Mature, very actively developed (v6.8.0 May 2026, near-daily commits), backed by a real legal entity (BMad Code, LLC) and identifiable founder, with semantic-release, real docs site, and a large community
  - Clean security posture: no published GitHub security advisories; formal security policy explicitly covering prompt-injection, path-traversal, and supply-chain scope

**Weaknesses:**
  - High process overhead: an independent reviewer (official issue #2003) estimated the full method can take 10-15x longer than just using Claude directly, making it impractical/overkill for small projects and minor changes (practitioners agree the full flow 'feels excessive for minor changes')
  - Steep learning curve and conceptual sprawl: many modules (bmm/bmb/tea/cis/bmgd), agents, phases and skills; counts and naming differ across versions/sources, and v6 was a large breaking rework from v4/v5
  - Target-audience paradox (issue #2003): pitched at both non-technical and technical users, but reviewers argue non-technical users can't audit AI code while technical users can't reliably make architecture decisions over large AI-generated volumes — quality gates assume judgment users may lack
  - Weak built-in safeguards in the dev loop: no mechanism forcing the dev agent to truly understand a problem before 'fixing' it; reviewers reported superficial fixes (renamed commands, meaningless test assertions, empty stubs marked resolved)
  - Adversarial code-review workflow is flawed: the AI is instructed to find problems so it manufactures false positives, and a forced minimum of ~3 issues per review (issue #1332) caused nitpicking and endless review cycles
  - Context-window pressure and agent-handoff misalignment on larger projects; users resort to manual handoff checklists
  - Brownfield/existing-codebase usage is weaker than greenfield (feedback issue #446 asked for better feature-driven approach and agent coordination)
  - Confusing ecosystem: multiple UNOFFICIAL Claude Code ports/plugins (aj-geddes, PabloLION, 24601) with overlapping names and inflated/mis-attributed feature claims (hooks, '15 commands', global ~/.claude install, token-savings %) make it easy to install the wrong/unmaintained thing
  - Real bus-factor: human contribution is concentrated (top contributor alexeyv ~47%, founder ~18%); no broad multi-org governance

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Independently re-verified the riskiest claims against current sources (June 2026), defaulting to skepticism.

CONFIRMED (via live GitHub API): 48,839 stars, 5,670 forks, 59 open issues, created 2025-04-13, pushed_at 2026-06-09, archived=false, disabled=false, description "Breakthrough Method for Agile Ai Driven Development." So stars/popularity and active-maintenance claims are accurate. NOTE: the original profile's "~398 watchers" could NOT be confirmed — the API subscribers_count returned a value equal to the star count (known quirk); flagged as uncertain.

CONFIRMED (via GitHub Releases API + release notes): latest stable v6.8.0 published 2026-05-25 (NOT a prerelease); web-bundles-v1.0.0 same day; v6.7.1 2026-05-18 (installer patch); near-daily commit cadence. NOT abandoned.

CONFIRMED (via npm registry): package "bmad-method" latest v6.8.0, license MIT, bin bmad/bmad-method → tools/installer/bmad-cli.js, NO deprecation notice. CONFIRMED (via GitHub Security tab): NO published security advisories; formal private-reporting security policy covering prompt-injection/path-traversal/supply-chain. No malware or rug-pull evidence found; the only npm-supply-chain hits (Shai-Hulud Sept 2025) are unrelated background and did NOT involve this package.

CONFIRMED (via LinkedIn/podcasts/README): founder Brian "BMad" Madison, BMad Code, LLC; Sr Eng Manager leading AI SDLC transformation at Extend; MIT + trademark clause for BMad / BMAD-METHOD marks. Contributors API: alexeyv 402, bmadcode 156, semantic-release-bot 91, muratkeremozcan 61, dracic 21 — confirming a small concentrated maintainer group and real bus-factor.

CORRECTED — Claude Code integration: official docs.bmad-method.org now state the installer writes NATIVE Claude Code Skills (subdir-per-skill SKILL.md) into the PROJECT's .claude/skills/ with a bmad- prefix, and explicitly do NOT mention MCP, slash commands, or hooks for Claude Code. The earlier profile's ".agents/skills"/"_bmad" install target for skills and namespaced "/bmad-bmm-create-prd" slash-command convention were overstated/conflated; current official mechanism is native Skills at .claude/skills/. Older v6 used .claude/commands/ (issue #773). Confirmed NO official MCP/hooks.

CORRECTED — modules: official v6 module set is bmm, bmb, tea, cis, AND bmgd (Game Dev Studio, previously omitted). TEA (Test Architect, agent Murat) is a SEPARATE module that supplements the built-in Quinn QA agent — it is NOT inside bmm as the earlier profile implied. "core" is an internal shared layer, not a user-selected module.

Also separated PabloLION/bmad-plugin (Claude Code plugin, "30+ skills") and 24601/BMAD-AT-CLAUDE. These community claims (hooks, MCP, token-savings %) are explicitly NOT attributed to the official distribution.

Remaining MEDIUM/uncertain: exact agent counts, the watcher count, and the precise "30+ IDEs" figure.

### Claude Task Master (Taskmaster AI)
`https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master`

**Category:** AI task-management / spec-driven workflow orchestration layer for agentic coding (npm package: task-master-ai)

**One-liner:** An AI-powered, PRD-driven task-management system that parses requirements documents into a structured, dependency-aware task graph and feeds tasks one at a time to coding agents — integrates with Claude Code (native plugin + MCP) and ~15 other AI editors.

**Philosophy:** Core thesis: agentic coding fails on large work because the agent loses the plan and context mid-stream. Task Master counters this by making the *plan* a first-class, persistent, version-controllable artifact rather than ephemeral chat context. You write a Product Requirements Document (PRD), Task Master parses it into discrete tasks with explicit dependencies, complexity scores, subtasks, and per-task test strategies, then drives the agent through them sequentially ("next task" -> implement -> mark done -> next). It is deliberately tool-agnostic and CLI-first/MCP-first so the same task graph works across Cursor, Claude Code, Windsurf, etc. The data (tasks.json + markdown task files + PRD) lives in the repo so it is git-trackable and human-auditable. The open-source CLI is also the on-ramp to the founders' commercial product, Hamster Studio (team sync + hosted inference).

**How it works:** Phases: (1) Initialize — `task-master init` (or `/tm:init`) scaffolds a `.taskmaster/` directory. (2) Plan — write a PRD at `.taskmaster/docs/prd.txt`; `parse-prd` uses an LLM to generate the initial task list. (3) Analyze/expand — `analyze-complexity` scores tasks and writes a complexity report; `expand-task`/`expand-all` breaks tasks into subtasks. (4) Execute — `next` surfaces the next unblocked task (respecting the dependency graph), `show <id>` displays details, and `set-status`/`to-in-progress`/`to-done` track state through pending -> in-progress -> review -> done (also deferred/cancelled). (5) Maintain — `update-task`, `add/remove-dependency`, `validate-dependencies`, `fix-dependencies`, plus a built-in `research` command that pulls fresh info (e.g. via Perplexity) into task updates. Tag-based "workstreams" let you keep parallel task contexts (e.g. per feature branch). Artifacts created: `.taskmaster/` containing `config.json` (model + settings), `state.json` (current tag), `tasks/tasks.json` (the central task DB, organized by tag), generated per-task markdown files, `docs/prd.txt`, and a complexity report JSON. The Claude Code plugin ships ~52 `/tm:`-prefixed slash commands (the repo's commands/ directory currently has 52 .md files; docs/marketing have cited "49") and exactly 3 subagents — task-orchestrator (analyzes the queue/dependencies and deploys executors, including in parallel), task-executor (implements an individual task), and task-checker (verifies/QA against the task's test strategy).

**Claude Code integration:** Multiple integration surfaces, and it is explicitly NOT Claude-Code-only. (1) Native Claude Code plugin via a bundled marketplace manifest (.claude-plugin/marketplace.json, owner "Hamster"/ralph@tryhamster.com): install with `/plugin marketplace add eyaltoledano/claude-task-master` then `/plugin install taskmaster@taskmaster`. The plugin package (packages/claude-code-plugin) bundles ~52 slash-command .md files (e.g. `/tm:init`, `/tm:parse-prd`, `/tm:next`, `/tm:expand-task`, `/tm:auto-implement-tasks`, `/tm:smart-workflow`), exactly 3 subagents (task-orchestrator/task-executor/task-checker), and auto-wires the MCP server via mcp.json — VERIFIED by direct GitHub contents listing. The plugin contains commands/ + agents/ + mcp.json directories/files but NO hooks/ directory and NO skills/ directory — so orchestration is done via subagents + commands + MCP, not Claude Code lifecycle hooks and not the newer Skills mechanism. (2) MCP server (stdio, `npx -y task-master-ai`) — add via `claude mcp add taskmaster-ai -- npx -y task-master-ai`; exposes ~36 tools (tunable to 15 'standard' or 7 'core'/'lean' via TASK_MASTER_TOOLS env var to cut token cost). (3) Claude Code as the *model provider* with no API key: set provider `claude-code` and models `claude-code/opus` or `claude-code/sonnet` in config.json, with knobs like maxTurns, permissionMode (default/acceptEdits/plan/bypassPermissions), allowedTools/disallowedTools. The 'loop'-style automated execution targets a Docker sandbox rather than Claude Code hooks. Multi-tool support: Cursor 1.0+, Windsurf, VS Code, Amazon Q Developer CLI, plus rules/slash-command generators for Codex, Gemini, OpenCode, Roo, Kiro (`task-master rules add <provider>`).

**Install:** Claude Code plugin (recommended for CC users): `/plugin marketplace add eyaltoledano/claude-task-master` then `/plugin install taskmaster@taskmaster`, then `/tm:init`. | MCP server in Claude Code: `claude mcp add taskmaster-ai -- npx -y task-master-ai`. | CLI: `npm install -g task-master-ai` then `task-master init` (or `npx task-master init` locally). | Claude Desktop one-click: `taskmaster.mcpb` MCPB bundle. Requires Node.js; for AI features either a provider API key (Anthropic/OpenAI/Google/Perplexity/xAI/OpenRouter/Mistral/Groq/Azure/Ollama) OR the Claude Code / Codex CLI OAuth (no key needed). Note: npm latest dist-tag is 0.43.1; a 1.0 line is in pre-release (dist-tag rc = 1.0.0-rc.1) — install of `latest` gives the stable 0.43.x.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained and company-backed (not a solo hobby/abandoned project). Created 2025-03-04; latest stable release task-master-ai@0.43.1 published ~2026-03-31; a 1.0 pre-release exists on npm (dist-tag `rc` = 1.0.0-rc.1), signalling continued forward work. Most recent repo push 2026-04-28 (verified via GitHub API on 2026-06-09); Snyk reports last commit ~1 month ago and rates maintenance "Healthy" (health score 83/100, "positive version release cadence"). Release cadence is roughly every few weeks (0.40.1 Dec 2025, 0.41 / 0.42 Jan 2026, 0.43.0 Feb 2026, 0.43.1 Mar 2026). 196 open issues per GitHub API (Snyk separately shows 155 open issues + 41 open PRs); not archived, not disabled. npm publishing is automated through GitHub Actions OIDC with provenance attestation (a positive supply-chain signal). Governance: backed by the startup Hamster (tryhamster.com, founded 2024); primary maintainers Eyal Toledano (@eyaltoledano) and Ralph (@Crunchyman-ralph / RalphEcom); co-creators cited include Jason Zhou. The OSS CLI is positioned as the free on-ramp to the paid Hamster Studio product. Caveat: could not confirm funding amount or team size from public sources.

**Popularity:** ~27.4k GitHub stars and ~2.56k forks (27,359 stars / 2,562 forks per GitHub API on 2026-06-09; ~60-72 contributors, 90+ releases). npm downloads: ~64k in the last month and ~13k/week (npm + Snyk); cumulative lifetime downloads are ~1.34M per the npm downloads API (some marketing materials round this up to "1.5M+", which is slightly inflated vs. the measured figure). One of the most-starred agentic-coding workflow tools; broadly discussed in Claude Code / Cursor community blogs.

**Best for:** Solo developers and small teams doing larger, multi-step features in an agentic editor (especially Claude Code or Cursor) who are willing to write a PRD up front and want the agent to execute a structured, reviewable plan rather than vibe-coding. Strongest fit for greenfield or well-specified work where breaking down requirements into dependency-ordered tasks pays off. Overkill for quick one-off edits or tiny scripts.

**Risks:** ["Trust/supply-chain: it is an npm package (task-master-ai) run via `npx -y` as an MCP server with filesystem access and your provider API keys passed as env vars — standard MCP/npm supply-chain exposure; pin versions and review the marketplace source. Mitigants: backed by an active company (Hamster) not an anonymous maintainer, source is open, and npm releases are published via GitHub Actions OIDC WITH provenance attestation. As of 2026-06-09 there are NO GitHub/GHSA security advisories, NO CVEs, and NO malware reports for this package (verified against the GitHub advisory DB and Snyk).", "Permission risk: Claude Code provider config supports permissionMode 'bypassPermissions' and bypassing tool allowlists — misconfiguration could let the agent act with reduced guardrails. (Note: 2026 Claude Code CVEs around hooks/MCP/env-var trust — e.g. CVE-2025-59536 / CVE-2026-21852 — are vulnerabilities in Claude Code itself, not in Task Master, but they underline the general risk of trusting project-config/MCP entries; keep Claude Code updated.)", "Lock-in: low at the editor level (multi-tool, plain-file artifacts) but moderate at the product level — the OSS tool funnels toward the paid Hamster Studio (hosted AI/team sync), so future best features may be gated.", "Licensing: Commons Clause restricts selling/hosting Task Master itself — a legal risk for anyone planning to commercialize or resell it.", "Complexity/cost risk: large token footprint and many moving parts can increase API spend and onboarding friction; not abandonment/rug-pull, but the project's long-term OSS investment is tied to the commercial company's incentives."]

**Strengths:**
  - Solves a real failure mode: persistent, dependency-aware task graph keeps long multi-step agent work on-plan instead of relying on fragile chat context
  - Genuinely tool-agnostic — same task DB drives Claude Code, Cursor, Windsurf, VS Code, Q CLI, Codex, Gemini, OpenCode; low lock-in to any single editor
  - First-class Claude Code support: native plugin (~52 slash commands + orchestrator/executor/checker subagents) AND MCP server AND zero-API-key provider mode using your Claude Code session
  - Artifacts are plain files in-repo (tasks.json, markdown, prd.txt) — git-trackable, diffable, human-auditable, team-shareable
  - Tunable token footprint (TASK_MASTER_TOOLS core/standard/all; large reduction in core mode) and multi-provider/fallback model config
  - Built-in research command injects fresh, post-cutoff info with project context
  - Company-backed with frequent releases (1.0 RC in flight), automated npm publishing with provenance attestation, and broad community traction
  - No known security vulnerabilities: zero GitHub/GHSA advisories, no CVEs, no malware reports; Snyk health score 83/100

**Weaknesses:**
  - High surface area / complexity: ~52 slash commands, ~36 MCP tools, subagents, tags, and config knobs are a lot to learn; easy to over-engineer simple tasks
  - Token / cost overhead — loading all MCP tools is ~21k tokens of context before any work; large projects can hit rate limits and slowdowns (users are steered to 'core' mode)
  - Quality is bounded by the PRD and the underlying LLM — weak/ambiguous PRDs yield poor task graphs, and the agent can still drift on complex tasks (the docs admit it is 'not foolproof')
  - License is MIT + Commons Clause, NOT pure MIT (GitHub reports it as 'Other'/NOASSERTION; npm metadata says 'MIT WITH Commons-Clause') — you may not sell Task Master itself or offer it as a hosted service; a real restriction for some commercial/redistribution use
  - The plugin ships no Claude Code lifecycle hooks and no Skills — deterministic enforcement is weaker than a hook-based system (verified: no hooks/ or skills/ dir in the plugin package)
  - Open-source tool is an explicit funnel to the paid Hamster Studio product — possible future feature gating / commercial pressure
  - Some doc/marketing figures are inconsistent with measured data (e.g. '49 commands' vs 52 in-repo; '1.5M+ downloads' vs ~1.34M measured) — treat secondary blog 'facts' cautiously
  - Sizeable open-issue backlog (196 open issues) typical of a fast-moving project

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-verified the riskiest claims on 2026-06-09 against authoritative sources. (1) POPULARITY — CONFIRMED via live GitHub API: 27,359 stars, 2,562 forks, 196 open issues, created 2025-03-04, last push 2026-04-28, archived=false, disabled=false. A secondary blog still cited a stale '25,300 stars' (Jan 2026) — overridden by the live API value. Cumulative npm downloads CORRECTED: npm downloads API sums to ~1.34M lifetime (not the marketed '1.5M+'); ~64k last month, ~13k/week. (2) MAINTENANCE — CONFIRMED active: npm `latest` = 0.43.1; GitHub releases page shows 0.43.1 dated 'March 31' (consistent with 2026-03-31), preceded by 0.43.0 (Feb 4), 0.42.0 (Jan 15), 0.41.0 (Jan 11), 0.40.1 (Dec 28). NEW FINDING: an npm `rc` dist-tag = 1.0.0-rc.1 exists, showing a 1.0 line in active development (Snyk's '1.1.0-rc.0' phrasing refers to the same pre-release stream). Snyk rates maintenance 'Healthy', health 83/100, 'positive version release cadence'. (3) CLAUDE CODE INTEGRATION MECHANISM — VERIFIED by direct GitHub contents listing of packages/claude-code-plugin: present = commands/ (52 .md files), agents/ (exactly 3: task-orchestrator, task-executor, task-checker), mcp.json, package.json, CHANGELOG.md; ABSENT = hooks/ and skills/ directories. This both confirms the 'no Claude Code hooks' claim (now near-certain, not just 'likely') and confirms it is NOT a Skills-based plugin — it is commands + subagents + MCP. CORRECTION: in-repo command count is 52, not the '49' cited in docs/marketing. Marketplace manifest owner = Hamster / ralph@tryhamster.com (confirmed). (4) SECURITY/TRUST — NO issues found: zero GitHub/GHSA security advisories for task-master-ai or claude-task-master, no CVEs, no malware/rug-pull/abandonment reports in web searches across 2026 supply-chain coverage; Snyk shows 'No direct vulnerabilities.' POSITIVE NEW FINDING: npm publishes via GitHub Actions OIDC with provenance attestation. Note: 2026 RCE/exfiltration CVEs (CVE-2025-59536, CVE-2026-21852) are in Claude Code itself, not Task Master, but are relevant context for trusting any MCP/project-config tool. License CONFIRMED as MIT WITH Commons-Clause (GitHub API: NOASSERTION/'Other'; npm metadata: 'MIT WITH Commons-Clause'). Confidence raised to HIGH overall: all four riskiest claim-areas verified against primary sources (GitHub API, npm registry/downloads API, GitHub contents listing, GHSA advisory DB, Snyk).

### Claude-Flow (now "Ruflo")
`https://github.com/ruvnet/ruflo (formerly github.com/ruvnet/claude-flow; that URL now redirects to /ruflo). npm: both `ruflo` and legacy `claude-flow` (both at v3.10.40, published 2026-06-09; neither formally deprecated). Website: https://claude-flow.ruv.io / https://flo.ruv.io. SourceForge mirror exists (ruflo.mirror).`

**Category:** Multi-agent orchestration "meta-harness" / agent-swarm framework layered on top of Claude Code (and, per marketing, Codex). Wraps the coding-agent CLI with a swarm/hive-mind coordinator, persistent SQLite + vector memory, a hooks system, and an MCP server. Marketed as "the leading agent meta-harness for Claude."

**One-liner:** An ambitious, solo-governed framework that turns Claude Code into a multi-agent "swarm" platform (Queen/worker hive-mind, 100+ agent roles, shared SQLite/vector memory, ~210-300+ MCP tools, hooks) - feature-dense and very popular (~58.7k stars, ~470k npm downloads/month) but carrying serious, only-partially-addressed security/trust concerns, self-reported (and implausibly high) benchmarks, and a credible independent audit alleging most "tools" are non-functional stubs.

**Philosophy:** Minimize user cognitive overhead ("after init, just use Claude Code normally - hooks auto-coordinate in the background") while maximizing autonomous, self-organizing multi-agent capability. Treat the coding agent as one node in a larger, memory-backed, self-learning swarm with consensus and federation. Model-agnostic branding (Claude Flow -> Ruflo, now advertising 5-provider failover) signals an intent to outlive any single LLM, though execution remains Claude-centric. Maximalist "kitchen-sink" feature philosophy - breadth, ambition, and aggressive marketing over minimalism and verifiable correctness; critics argue the breadth outruns the working implementation.

**How it works:** A coordination layer sits between you and Claude Code. You run `init`, which installs agent definitions, slash commands, skills, a hooks system, and an MCP server. Tasks are then routed to "swarms" of specialized agents (coding, testing, security, docs, architecture roles). Two coordination modes: (1) `swarm` - lightweight ad-hoc multi-agent coordination for a single task; (2) `hive-mind` - a persistent "Queen" coordinator directing worker agents across sessions, backed by SQLite session state. Consensus topologies (hierarchical/mesh/adaptive; Raft/Byzantine/Gossip/CRDT) are CLAIMED - but an independent audit (issue #1514) found consensus-type selection is cosmetic (all strategies use the same JSON majority-vote handler) and `verifySignature()` returned `true` unconditionally. Memory: `.swarm/memory.db` (SQLite), with v3 adding AgentDB + HNSW vector indexing for RAG/semantic recall; this is independently confirmed as one of the few genuinely working subsystems (real all-MiniLM-L6-v2 embeddings). Self-learning components (SONA neural patterns, ReasoningBank trajectory learning) claim to improve from past runs. A `ruflo-sparc` plugin provides a "guided 5-phase SPARC development methodology with quality gates" (5-phase framing confirmed in README; exact phase names unconfirmed). v3.5+ rewrote core components into Rust compiled to WASM. Install creates `.claude/`, `.swarm/`, `.hive-mind/`, `.claude-flow/`, and `CLAUDE.md`; reviewers report heavy artifact clutter and per-session token overhead unless tuned.

**Claude Code integration:** Designed Claude-Code-first, with three integration surfaces (all confirmed in the current README). (1) Native Claude Code PLUGINS via marketplace: `/plugin marketplace add ruvnet/ruflo` then `/plugin install ruflo-core@ruflo` (also ruflo-swarm, etc.; ~33 plugins). This "lite" path adds slash commands + agent definitions only - the README explicitly labels it "slash commands only," so MCP/memory/swarm/spawning are NOT wired up via this path alone. (2) MCP SERVER: `claude mcp add ruflo -- npx ruflo@latest mcp start` exposes MCP tools (e.g. `memory_store`, `swarm_init`, `agent_spawn`; the project claims ~210 tools in its web UI and 300+ across the stack). (3) Full CLI install (`npx ruflo@latest init` / `init wizard`, or the README's `curl ... install.sh | bash`) wires up everything: agents, 60+ slash commands, ~30 skills, the MCP server, HOOKS, and a background daemon. The hooks system is the key integration mechanism - after `init`, hooks auto-route tasks, capture learning, and coordinate agents in the background while you "use Claude Code normally." So it uses ALL of Claude Code's extension points: plugins, slash commands, skills, MCP, and hooks - this is accurate and verified. Multi-tool: README badges and docs now explicitly claim Codex integration AND broader multi-provider support - "5 providers with failover (Claude, GPT, Gemini, Cohere, Ollama)" and a web UI with "6 curated frontier models" via OpenRouter. This is more substantiated than the prior profile asserted (the earlier "thin/unconfirmed" framing was too skeptical), though real-world maturity of non-Claude paths is still unproven and the framework remains Claude-centric in practice.

**Install:** Full CLI: `npx ruflo@latest init wizard` (interactive) or `npx ruflo@latest init` (non-interactive); global: `npm install -g ruflo@latest`. README also offers a piped installer: `curl -fsSL https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/ruvnet/ruflo@main/scripts/install.sh | bash` (curl-pipe-bash is itself a trust hazard - prefer inspecting first). Legacy/back-compat name still works: `npx claude-flow@latest` (and historically `claude-flow@alpha`). MCP: `claude mcp add ruflo -- npx ruflo@latest mcp start`. Plugins: `/plugin marketplace add ruvnet/ruflo` then `/plugin install ruflo-core@ruflo`. Requires Node.js + Claude Code. NOTE: historical versions (v3.1.0-alpha.55 through ~v3.5.2/3.5.3) shipped an obfuscated npm preinstall script (see risks); the preinstall was later removed and a `ruflo verify` cryptographic-witness command added (v3.6.x). Prefer pinned, audited versions in an isolated environment.

**Maintenance:** VERY actively maintained but solo-GOVERNED. The GitHub account `ruvnet` (Reuven "rUv" Cohen) is a personal USER account, not an organization - effectively a one-maintainer-led project (the repo does show ~28 contributors total, so not literally one committer, but governance, releases, and direction are clearly dominated by the owner; bus-factor risk stands). Repo created 2025-06-02; last push 2026-06-09 (today). Extreme release velocity: latest v3.10.40 published 2026-06-09, with ~1,527 total releases (confirms the "1,500+ releases" claim) and multiple releases per day. Major arc: v2.0.0-alpha (mid/late 2025) introduced hive-mind; v3 ("V3 complete rebuild," tracked in issue #945) brought the Rust/WASM rewrite; v3.5.0 (Feb 2026) brought the rename from Claude Flow -> Ruflo (justified by trademark de-risking + multi-model positioning - "naming a multi-model tool after one model"); v3.6.x added security remediation (verify/witness system, preinstall removal, SECURITY.md). The project is still effectively in perpetual rapid-iteration/alpha-tagged state (the npm `alpha` and `latest` dist-tags both point at the same version), so "upgrade treadmill" and breaking-change risk are real. Heavily forked (~6.7k forks); many third-party forks are stale v2-alpha snapshots.

**Popularity:** ~58,700 GitHub stars (58,666 verified via GitHub API 2026-06-09), ~6,734 forks, ~396 watchers/subscribers, ~633 open issues, ~28 contributors. npm downloads are substantial and largely real: ruflo ~321,917 + claude-flow ~147,412 = ~469k downloads in the trailing month (2026-05-04 to 2026-06-02) - close to the "~500k" marketing figure (though raw downloads include CI/npx re-fetches and are NOT the same as the separately-claimed, unverified "~100k monthly active users across 80+ countries"). It is among the most-starred Claude Code multi-agent projects and is widely cited as a leading swarm orchestrator. CAUTION: older third-party write-ups (e.g. a dev.to piece) cite far lower star counts (~29.6k-31.1k) from before/around the rename, so any single secondary source's popularity figure may be stale - the live API number (~58.7k) is authoritative.

**Best for:** Individual developers and small teams building Claude-Code-centric workflows who want fast iteration, persistent agent memory, and broad/exploratory multi-agent research or scaffolding - and who are comfortable running it in an ISOLATED, non-sensitive, local sandbox with pinned, audited versions. Good for experimentation, prototyping, and "wide" research/analysis work, leaning on the memory/RAG subsystem (which is real) rather than the swarm/consensus/security tooling (much of which an audit flags as stub). Poor fit for: production systems, regulated/sensitive data, teams needing strong governance or mature observability, or multi-provider portability - and overkill for simple pipelines with a few predictable agents (where native Claude Code Agent Teams or a small custom script is simpler and safer).

**Risks:** HIGH security/trust risk - the most important caveat, and largely confirmed against the project's own tracker plus independent audits (issues #1375 closed/'completed', #1482 still OPEN, #1514 closed; discussion #1359; remediation tracking #1383/#1384/#1386). (1) SUPPLY-CHAIN (confirmed historical): versions ~v3.1.0-alpha.55 through ~v3.5.2/3.5.3 shipped an OBFUSCATED npm preinstall one-liner that silently, recursively deleted directories under ~/.npm/_npx/ and cache index files containing 'claude-flow'/'ruflo', suppressing errors - textbook supply-chain red flag. It was removed only after external disclosure; v3.6.x added an Ed25519 `ruflo verify` witness/manifest system in response. (2) PROMPT INJECTION (alleged, on-record): MCP tool descriptions reportedly contained hidden instructions telling Claude to add the repo owner as a contributor to users' repos without consent (issues #1375 and #1514); the maintainer disputes intent, and remediation #1384 reports 'searched tool descriptions for contributor injection (none found in current code)' - so treat as a real, documented allegation that the maintainer claims is no longer present; verify in the version you install. (3) CODE-LEVEL vulns reported (issue #1375 / discussion #1359, partially open): SQL injection (memory-initializer.ts), path-traversal via sanitizePath(), prototype pollution, command injection via execSync string interpolation, unauthenticated MCP endpoints, SSRF to cloud metadata, disabled WebSocket auth, full process.env exposure to child processes, and a `verifySignature()` that allegedly returns true unconditionally. (4) STUB/THEATER + FABRICATED METRICS (independent audit, issue #1514): a security scanner (`aidefence_scan`) that returned API keys as 'safe' until a v3.6.18 fix; ~97% of tools alleged non-functional; hardcoded performance numbers. (5) hive-mind reportedly spawns Claude CLI with safety checks disabled. (6) Persistent files/processes surviving uninstall. (7) A community security PR (#1292) was closed without full merge - the maintainer shipped a partial subset. NUANCE / correction vs prior profile: maintainer engagement is NOT 'largely absent' - there is documented remediation activity (SECURITY.md added, preinstall removed, verify/witness system, remediation issues #1383/#1384, v3.6 roadmap #1386, specific scanner fix in v3.6.18). However, issue #1482 remains open and the deeper 'most tools are stubs / fabricated benchmarks' criticisms remain contested and not independently resolved. RECOMMENDATION: do NOT use in production or with sensitive data; isolated experimentation only, pin and audit the installed version (run `ruflo verify`), avoid the curl|bash installer, and do not trust the benchmark/marketing claims at face value.

**Strengths:**
  - Deep, native use of every Claude Code extension point - plugins, slash commands, skills, MCP server, and hooks (all confirmed in the current README) - genuinely wires Claude Code into a multi-agent harness
  - Persistent cross-session memory (SQLite + HNSW vector/RAG) that independent auditing confirms is one of the few genuinely working subsystems (real all-MiniLM-L6-v2 embeddings + SQLite) - addresses a real gap in vanilla Claude Code
  - Strong on broad/exploratory research-style tasks: a third-party bake-off found it produced more thorough, multi-source output than Claude Code's native Agent Teams
  - Reported token efficiency in at least one bake-off (hit Claude Code throughput limits less often) - though note this conflicts with an audit alleging large per-session token OVERHEAD, so treat token claims as workload-dependent and contested
  - Flexible install spectrum: lightweight plugin-only path up to full MCP+daemon stack; adoptable incrementally
  - Very large advertised feature surface (swarm/hive-mind topologies, 100+ agent roles, SPARC methodology, federation, observability, cost tracking) with multi-provider/Codex positioning and an active, fast-moving roadmap
  - v3.6 added a cryptographic install-verification path (`ruflo verify`, Ed25519 witness/manifest) and a SECURITY.md - i.e. the maintainer has at least begun responding to supply-chain criticism

**Weaknesses:**
  - Credible independent audit (roman-rr, GitHub issue #1514) concludes ~290 of 300+ tools (~97%) are non-functional 'theater' stubs that return JSON state with no execution backend, with only ~10 (~3%) genuinely working (memory/embeddings/terminal/session) - if accurate, the headline feature counts massively overstate working functionality
  - Self-reported, implausible benchmarks: the '84.8% SWE-bench' and '75% API cost savings' come from the project's own wiki/marketing with no independent verification; 84.8% would EXCEED leading frontier models on SWE-bench Verified (Opus ~80.9%), which is a strong implausibility/red flag, and audits allege specific fabricated metrics (e.g. a '352x faster' figure benchmarked against a literal `sleep(352)`; hardcoded cache 'hit rates' and token baselines)
  - Massive complexity / over-engineering - even sympathetic reviewers call it potentially 'the most over-engineered tool in human history'; Byzantine consensus etc. is overkill (and per audit, cosmetic)
  - Workspace noise and persistence: many state/artifact files + background processes; reports of `.swarm` files reappearing after deletion and ~45k lines / 259 files persisting after `npm uninstall -g`
  - Documentation gaps for advanced use; ecosystem smaller and less battle-tested than LangChain/LangGraph
  - Limited debugging/tracing maturity vs dedicated orchestration frameworks
  - Solo-governed + perpetual rapid-iteration churn = upgrade treadmill and bus-factor risk
  - Genuine multi-provider/Codex maturity is unproven despite the de-branding and badges - Claude remains the practical center of gravity (soft ecosystem lock-in)

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** RE-CHECKED the riskiest claims on 2026-06-09. CONFIRMED via GitHub REST API: ruvnet/ruflo = 58,666 stars, 6,734 forks, 396 watchers, 633 open issues, ~28 contributors, created 2025-06-02, last push 2026-06-09, license MIT, archived=false, owner type=User (not Org). github.com/ruvnet/claude-flow redirects to /ruflo. ~1,527 total releases (confirms '1,500+'). CONFIRMED via npm registry: both `claude-flow` and `ruflo` at v3.10.40 (modified 2026-06-09); neither formally deprecated; both `latest` and `alpha` dist-tags point to 3.10.40. CONFIRMED npm downloads: ruflo ~321,917 + claude-flow ~147,412 = ~469k in trailing month - supports the '~500k downloads' marketing (but not the separate '100k MAU' claim, which stays unverified). MAINTENANCE: very active, solo-governed; corrected to note ~28 contributors exist but ruvnet (USER account) dominates. INTEGRATION: confirmed all three surfaces (plugins via marketplace, MCP via `claude mcp add`, full CLI `init` with hooks/skills/daemon) directly from README; CORRECTED the prior 'multi-tool support thin/unconfirmed' framing - the current README explicitly advertises Codex + '5 providers with failover (Claude/GPT/Gemini/Cohere/Ollama)' and OpenRouter models, so multi-provider is claimed/positioned (real-world maturity still unproven). SECURITY: confirmed issue #1375 (closed/completed), #1482 (still OPEN), #1514 (closed - the roman-rr '99% theater' audit), discussion #1359; obfuscated preinstall (v3.1.0-alpha.55 - ~v3.5.2/3.5.3) confirmed across multiple sources; prompt-injection-via-MCP-tool-descriptions allegation confirmed on-record (#1375/#1514), maintainer disputes intent. CORRECTED the prior 'maintainer responses largely absent' claim: remediation IS documented (SECURITY.md added, preinstall removed, Ed25519 `ruflo verify` witness system in v3.6.x, remediation issues #1383/#1384, v3.6 roadmap #1386, aidefence_scan fixed in v3.6.18) - so engagement is partial-but-present, not absent. roman-rr audit's specific figures captured: ~10/300+ real (~3%), ~290 stubs (~97%), '352x faster' = literal sleep(352), verifySignature() returns true unconditionally. BENCHMARKS: 84.8% SWE-bench / 75% cost savings remain SELF-REPORTED and are implausibly high (would exceed frontier models ~80.9%); the wiki SWE-Bench pages did not render eval methodology/instance counts via WebFetch, so subset/methodology still UNCONFIRMED and flagged. SPARC: '5-phase' confirmed in README, exact phase names still unconfirmed. CONFIDENCE: high on repo identity, rename/redirect, stars, forks, license, owner type, version, downloads, release velocity, integration surfaces, multi-provider positioning, and the existence/state of the security issues + remediation; medium on the audit's exact stub percentage and on benchmark methodology (sourced from third-party audit and self-reported wiki respectively, not independently reproduced).
