### Everything Claude Code (ECC)
`https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC (repo renamed from affaan-m/everything-claude-code; old URL redirects — verified: github.com/affaan-m/everything-claude-code resolves to affaan-m/ECC)`

**Category:** Agentic-coding "operator system" / harness config framework — a distributable bundle of subagents, skills, slash commands, hooks, rules, and MCP conventions for Claude Code and other AI coding harnesses. Distributed as a native Claude Code plugin (also installable via npm/shell scripts).

**One-liner:** A massive, opinionated "everything bundle" of agents, skills, hooks, and rules that turns Claude Code (and Cursor/Codex/OpenCode/etc.) into a research-first, test-driven, security-gated engineering harness — powerful but heavy, solo-maintained, and with an extraordinary star count (211K+) that outpaces its visible usage/discussion signals.

**Philosophy:** "The harness-native operator system for agentic work, built from real-world multi-harness engineering workflows." Practical/battle-tested over theoretical: encode an experienced operator's discipline (research-first, tests-first, security-gated, memory + continuous learning, compaction) as reusable, portable primitives that move ACROSS harnesses rather than living in one tool. Origin story: configs the author used to win an Anthropic-affiliated hackathon (built zenith.chat in Claude Code), hardened over ~10 months of daily use. (Note: sources variously place the hackathon as Anthropic x Forum Ventures Sept 2025; the companion AgentShield was built at a Cerebral Valley x Anthropic hackathon Feb 2026 — multiple hackathons are referenced.)

**How it works:** ECC packages reusable agent-harness primitives that you install into your config dir. Core components (per the live .claude-plugin/plugin.json, v2.0.0-rc.1, verified 2026-06-09): 64 specialized subagents (planner, architect, code-reviewer, security-reviewer, language-specific reviewers like go/python/rust/java, build-error resolvers), 261 skills organized by domain (tdd-workflow, verification-loop, backend/frontend patterns, security-review, continuous-learning, framework-specific), 84 legacy command shims (e.g. /plan, /code-review, /build-fix, /refactor-clean, /quality-gate, /learn), reusable hooks that fire on Claude Code tool events (SessionStart, PostToolUse, Stop), and rule sets (language-agnostic + per-language). Workflow philosophy is an explicit loop: Plan (planner agent) -> TDD/write-tests-first -> Code -> Review (code-reviewer, filters to >80% confidence) -> Security review (security-reviewer) -> Verify (verification-loop) -> Compact. Subagents run as delegated prompts with scoped tools. It also ships 'NanoClaw v2', a built-in orchestration engine (model routing, skill hot-load, session branch/search/export/compact/metrics) — confirmed in README. Artifacts it creates: files/dirs under ~/.claude/ (skills, commands, agents, hooks via hooks.json) and harness-specific dirs (.cursor/, .opencode/, .codex/, .gemini/, .zed/, .qwen/, .trae/, .kiro/, .github/copilot-instructions.md); rules must be MANUALLY copied to ~/.claude/rules/ecc/ because the plugin system cannot distribute them. The author also maintains a companion scanner, AgentShield (github.com/affaan-m/agentshield — verified separate repo, CLI/GitHub Action/ECC plugin/GitHub App, ~102 static-analysis rules) for auditing agent configs.

**Claude Code integration:** Claude-Code-FIRST but explicitly multi-harness (NOT Claude-Code-only). Claude Code is the primary/native target: the repo contains a real .claude-plugin/ with plugin.json + marketplace.json, so it installs as a native Claude Code plugin via the /plugin marketplace + /plugin install flow (verified). Integration surfaces: (1) native plugin packaging skills + commands + agents; (2) slash commands invoked as /ecc:plan (plugin namespace) or /plan (manual install); (3) hooks auto-loaded by Claude Code v2.1+ from hooks/hooks.json (README explicitly WARNS: do NOT add a 'hooks' field to plugin.json, since v2.1+ auto-loads them and an explicit declaration triggers a duplicate-detection error — verified verbatim); (4) skills (the primary surface, with legacy command shims for back-compat); (5) MCP — it ships MCP CONFIGS/conventions (mcp-configs/, .mcp.json) but the plugin.json declares NO bundled MCP servers (empty mcpServers — verified); (6) rules copied manually to ~/.claude/rules/ecc/ (README explicitly states the plugin system cannot distribute rules — verified verbatim). Beyond Claude Code it has first-class adapters for Cursor, OpenCode, Codex (CLI + macOS app), GitHub Copilot, Gemini, Zed, Qwen, Trae, Kiro — confirmed by dedicated top-level dirs for each harness and README. Minimum requirement: Claude Code CLI v2.1.0+ (verified).

**Install:** Plugin (native, recommended): /plugin marketplace add https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC then /plugin install ecc@ecc (verified verbatim in README). CLI: npx ecc-install --profile full (or ./install.sh --profile full on macOS/Linux, or .\install.ps1 --profile full on Windows PowerShell — all verified). Requires Claude Code v2.1.0+. IMPORTANT: if you installed via /plugin, do NOT also run ./install.sh; instead manually copy only the rules/ directories you want into ~/.claude/rules/ecc/ (start with rules/common plus one language/framework pack) because the plugin system cannot distribute rules.

**Maintenance:** Active and fast-moving, but solo-led. Verified via GitHub API on 2026-06-09: last push 2026-06-09 (most recent commit references PR #2201), exactly 2055 commits on main (confirmed via Link-header pagination), repo NOT archived/disabled. Release cadence ~2-3 weeks: latest stable v1.10.0 (2026-04-05, "Surface Refresh, Operator Workflows, and ECC 2.0 Alpha"); latest prerelease v2.0.0-rc.1 (2026-05-25). MIT-licensed. Solo maintainer dominance confirmed: affaan-m (Affaan Mustafa) has ~1,432 contributions, ~95% of all human contributions; next human contributor has 47. Named human contributors ~17; total contributors incl. anonymous email-only ~233 (per API Link header). Bus-factor and commercial-tier (ECC Pro) considerations apply.

**Popularity:** Authoritative (GitHub API, 2026-06-09, directly verified): 211,761 stars, 32,500 forks, ~1,070 subscribers/watchers (note: the API 'watchers_count' field mirrors stars at 211,761; the real watch/subscriber count is subscribers_count=1,070), 45 open issues. NOTE on conflicting figures: secondary sources are stale/inconsistent snapshots of a fast-growing repo — blogs cite 82K (Medium), 100K, 140K (project Discussion #1272), 163K (Augment, Apr 2026), and 182K stars at different timestamps, and older component counts (28/48 agents, 119/156/183 skills, 60 commands). Treat the live API numbers as current truth and the blog numbers as time-stamped snapshots. CAVEAT: critics argue the star count overstates real daily adoption (it spiked via a viral X thread; Discussions/issue activity looks small relative to 200K+ stars). This skepticism is partly editorial/inferential — at least one major tracking blog (Augment) treats the popularity as genuine, citing contributor count and 10+ months of production use. Bottom line: stars are real and not demonstrably botted, but are an unreliable proxy for stability or active usage; evaluate on the actual diff you install.

**Best for:** Power users and engineering teams who (a) live in Claude Code and/or juggle multiple harnesses (Cursor/Codex/OpenCode) and want one standardized, security-gated, test-first operating model, and (b) are comfortable auditing and pruning a large config. Best adopted MODULARLY — pull specific high-value pieces (code-reviewer agent, TDD/verification-loop skills, security rules) rather than a full day-one install. Poor fit for solo devs on simple/small projects, anyone wanting a minimal setup, or teams unwilling to vet third-party hooks/MCP configs — for them a hand-written CLAUDE.md plus a few hooks covers ~80% of the value.

**Risks:** ["Supply-chain / trust (general, NOT an active incident): it's a large, fast-moving, single-author bundle of executable hooks, agents, and installer scripts (npx ecc-install / install.sh / install.ps1 run Node + npm install and write into ~/.claude). Untrusted hooks and repo-controlled .claude/.mcp.json are exactly the vectors the project's own security guide warns about (verified-real CVE-2025-59536 / CVE-2026-21852, MCP auto-approval). No malware/backdoor/rug-pull has been reported against ECC itself (verified — the 'fake Claude Code malware' news stories are unrelated impersonation sites and a separate Claude Code source-code leak, not ECC). Still, review the diff before installing and consider scanning with a config scanner (e.g. the author's AgentShield).", "Abandonment / governance risk: solo-maintained by one individual (~95% of commits) who also monetizes a Pro tier; if he steps away, a 200K+-star repo could stagnate quickly. No org/foundation backing.", "Lock-in / switching cost: deep, multi-tool integration and bespoke workflow conventions mean unwinding ECC from your config dirs across several harnesses is non-trivial.", "Complexity risk: large surface increases the chance of misbehaving hooks, conflicting rules, or token/cost bloat; harder to reason about what the agent will do.", "Commercial-direction risk (NOT a rug-pull today): OSS is MIT and stated 'free forever', but a paid 'ECC Pro' GitHub App ($19/seat/mo, per README) exists, so future value may shift behind the paid tier. Pricing taken from README/marketing, not separately verified billing.", "Hype risk: extremely high stars are not a reliable proxy for stability or adoption here — evaluate on the actual diff you install, not the star badge."]

**Strengths:**
  - Genuinely native Claude Code plugin (real .claude-plugin/plugin.json + marketplace.json, verified) — not just a CLAUDE.md dump; installs via the official /plugin flow
  - Huge breadth of ready-made primitives (verified counts: 64 agents, 261 skills, 84 command shims, plus rule sets) covering TDD, security review, language-specific review, ML/MLOps, frameworks
  - True multi-harness: one repo emits configs for Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, OpenCode, Copilot, Gemini, Zed, Qwen, Trae, Kiro (verified dirs) — useful for teams standardizing behavior across tools
  - Strong, REAL security posture: ships a dedicated security guide referencing genuine, independently-verified CVEs (CVE-2025-59536 [CVSS 8.7] and CVE-2026-21852 — Check Point disclosures: pre-trust hook RCE + .mcp.json consent bypass, fixed in Claude Code 1.0.111), warns about MCP auto-approval / hook risks / --dangerously-skip-permissions, and the author ships a real companion scanner, AgentShield, for auditing configs
  - Opinionated, coherent workflow (Plan->TDD->Code->Review->Verify->Compact) with a code-reviewer that filters to >80%-confidence findings — high learning value even if you only borrow pieces
  - MIT-licensed with high commit velocity (2055 commits, ~2-3wk release cadence); selective/profile-based install lets you take a subset
  - Components are 'borrowable' — patterns are useful as reference even without installing the whole thing
  - No malware, backdoor, or rug-pull reports against ECC itself were found (verified); it is a legitimate, openly-developed project

**Weaknesses:**
  - Over-engineering is the #1 community complaint: most users 'just need a good CLAUDE.md, not an entire ecosystem'; 261 skills/64 agents is overwhelming for simple projects
  - Broken 'install once, get everything' promise: the plugin system CANNOT distribute rules, so users must manually copy rule packs into ~/.claude/rules/ecc/ (confirmed in README)
  - Star count vs. visible-usage mismatch — 211K+ stars but comparatively modest Discussions/issue activity; popularity is partly viral-thread-driven, so social proof is an unreliable adoption signal (though stars are genuine, not botted)
  - Solo maintainer / bus-factor risk: one person (~95% of commits) drives architecture, releases, and the commercial tier; sustainability hinges on continued personal activity
  - Very young project (created 2026-01-18) with frequent large releases and a churny surface (commands -> skills migration, 'legacy command shims'), implying API/behavior instability
  - Complexity and cross-harness adapters create switching/lock-in costs if the project stalls
  - Inconsistent public numbers across the project's own marketing and third-party blogs make it hard to know exactly what you're getting until you inspect the repo
  - Installer runs Node + npm install and writes broadly into ~/.claude and many harness dirs — non-trivial footprint to review/trust

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-checked the riskiest claims against PRIMARY sources on 2026-06-09 and the profile held up extremely well. CONFIRMED (directly verified): (1) Popularity — GitHub API returns stars=211761 (profile said 211760; off by 1, rounding noise — CORRECTED to 211761), forks=32500, subscribers=1070, open_issues=45; repo NOT archived/disabled. Clarified that API 'watchers_count' (211761) mirrors stars and the real watcher figure is subscribers_count=1070 (profile already used 1070). (2) Maintenance — last push 2026-06-09; exactly 2055 commits via Link-header pagination (matches profile); stable v1.10.0 (2026-04-05) and prerelease v2.0.0-rc.1 (2026-05-25) confirmed via releases API; ~2-3wk cadence. (3) Solo-maintainer/bus-factor — affaan-m ~1432 contributions (~95% of human commits), next human at 47; ~17 named contributors, ~233 incl. anonymous (reconciles blog 'contributors' figures). (4) Integration mechanism — plugin.json (v2.0.0-rc.1) confirms 64 agents / 261 skills / 84 legacy command shims, empty mcpServers, NO hooks field; README confirms /plugin marketplace add + /plugin install ecc@ecc, install.sh/.ps1/npx ecc-install --profile full, min Claude Code v2.1.0, the verbatim 'plugins cannot distribute rules — copy to ~/.claude/rules/ecc/' instruction, and the verbatim 'do not add hooks field to plugin.json (v2.1+ auto-loads hooks/hooks.json)' warning. Old everything-claude-code URL redirects to ECC (confirmed via API resolving both to affaan-m/ECC). (5) Security/trust — NO malware, backdoor, scam, or rug-pull reports against ECC itself (the 'fake Claude Code malware' news is unrelated impersonation sites + a separate Claude Code source-code leak). The CVEs cited in ECC's security guide are GENUINE and independently verified (CVE-2025-59536 CVSS 8.7 + CVE-2026-21852, Check Point/Hacker News/Dark Reading; pre-trust hook RCE + .mcp.json consent bypass; fixed in Claude Code 1.0.111) — this STRENGTHENS the security-posture claim. AgentShield is a real, separate repo by the same author. ECC Pro ($19/seat/mo) and NanoClaw v2 confirmed in README. CHANGES MADE: corrected stars to 211761; explicitly stated no malware/rug-pull against ECC; reframed the CVEs as verified-real (a credibility plus, not a weakness); added AgentShield-verified note; SOFTENED the 'stars overstate adoption' claim from near-fact to a sourced-but-partly-editorial caveat (Augment treats popularity as genuine; Medium supplies the over-engineering/divide critique); reconciled contributor-count discrepancy; flagged the hackathon-origin detail as having multiple variant references. COULD NOT independently confirm 'real' daily-active-usage numbers (no public DAU data — inherently unverifiable) and did not separately verify ECC Pro billing. Confidence: HIGH on metrics, integration mechanics, license, component counts, maintenance, CVE legitimacy, and absence-of-malware; MEDIUM only on the stars-vs-real-usage interpretation.

**Sources:** https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC · https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC/blob/main/README.md · https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC/releases · https://github.com/affaan-m/ECC/blob/main/.claude-plugin/plugin.json · https://api.github.com/repos/affaan-m/ECC (verified 2026-06-09: stars=211761, forks=32500, subscribers=1070, open_issues=45, created=2026-01-18, last push=2026-06-09, archived=false, MIT) · https://api.github.com/repos/affaan-m/ECC/commits?per_page=1 (Link header rel=last page=2055 => 2055 total commits) · https://api.github.com/repos/affaan-m/ECC/contributors (affaan-m ~1432 contributions / ~95%; ~17 named, ~233 incl. anonymous) · https://github.com/affaan-m/agentshield (companion security scanner by same author — verified separate repo) · https://research.checkpoint.com/2026/rce-and-api-token-exfiltration-through-claude-code-project-files-cve-2025-59536/ (verifies CVE-2025-59536 + CVE-2026-21852 are real) · https://thehackernews.com/2026/02/claude-code-flaws-allow-remote-code.html · https://www.augmentcode.com/learn/everything-claude-code-hits-163k-stars (163K snapshot, Apr 2026; treats popularity as genuine) · https://medium.com/@tentenco/everything-claude-code-inside-the-82k-star-agent-harness-thats-dividing-the-developer-community-4fe54feccbc1 (82K snapshot; over-engineering / community-divide critique)

### GSD (Get Shit Done) — now "GSD Core" (open-gsd/gsd-core)
`https://github.com/open-gsd/gsd-core (formerly open-gsd/get-shit-done-redux; original upstream gsd-build/get-shit-done — still public/unarchived but issues locked + redirected, npm pkg get-shit-done-cc deprecated)`

**Category:** Spec-driven development / context-engineering workflow framework (meta-prompt + CLI + agents + hooks) for AI coding agents, with Claude Code as the primary target

**One-liner:** A "context-engineering" workflow system that drives AI coding agents through a disciplined Discuss to Plan to Execute to Verify to Ship loop, isolating heavy work in fresh-context subagents to beat "context rot." Tagline: "Git. Ship. Done."

**Philosophy:** Context engineering over prompt cleverness: treat the LLM's finite, degrading context window as the primary constraint, and engineer around it with disciplined process. Keep the orchestrating session lean; push research/planning/execution into disposable fresh-context subagents; persist all real state to human-readable files (STATE.md, CONTEXT.md, plans) so knowledge survives compaction and session resets; and force a spec-first, verify-before-done, PR-per-phase rhythm so AI output stays reviewable and auditable. In short: 'Git. Ship. Done.' — process discipline as the antidote to AI flakiness.

**How it works:** GSD is fundamentally a large bundle of prompt/instruction Markdown files plus a Node.js CLI/SDK and hook scripts that orchestrate a structured software-delivery loop. The core problem it targets is "context rot" — the quality degradation of an LLM agent as its context window fills with tokens. The fix is structural: the main session stays lean while research, planning, and execution are delegated to specialized subagents, each spawned with a fresh ~200k-token context.

PHASE LOOP (per milestone): (1) Discuss/Spec — capture implementation decisions before any code; (2) Plan — research, decompose into atomic tasks, and verify each plan fits a fresh context; (3) Execute — run plans in parallel "waves," with independent tasks parallelized and dependent tasks auto-sequenced, each executor starting clean; (4) Verify — goal-backward walk-through of what was built, diagnose and fix before declaring done; (5) Ship — create the PR, archive the phase, repeat. Higher-level scaffolding adds new-project, new-milestone, and complete-milestone steps.

SLASH COMMANDS: The repo ships a large set of command definitions under commands/gsd/. I directly counted 101 .md command files in the live `next` branch on 2026-06-09 (e.g. new-project, new-milestone, discuss-phase, spec-phase, plan-phase, ultraplan-phase, execute-phase, verify-work, ship, complete-milestone, audit-milestone, code-review, secure-phase, ui-phase, map-codebase, debug, forensics, autonomous, resume-work, pause-work, health, stats, config, add-tests, capture, graphify, quick, surface). These surface in Claude Code as /gsd-* slash commands. NOTE: counts vary widely by version and by npx-install vs third-party-plugin packaging; marketing copy cites figures like '~50 markdown files' or '82 commands', the prior profile estimated '~70', but the actual live `next` count is 101 command files.

SUBAGENTS: 36 agent definitions under agents/ in the live `next` branch (e.g. gsd-planner, gsd-executor, gsd-verifier, gsd-code-reviewer/gsd-code-fixer, gsd-debugger, gsd-doc-writer, gsd-ui-auditor, gsd-phase-researcher, gsd-plan-checker, gsd-roadmapper, gsd-codebase-mapper). Each is a fresh-context worker for one job. (Prior profile said 33; live count is 36.)

ARTIFACTS / FILES: It writes durable, human-readable Markdown state that survives session boundaries — notably STATE.md and CONTEXT.md (templates/examples at repo root), plus per-phase plans (.plans/ dir), milestone/roadmap docs, and (in the plugin packaging) a HANDOFF.json for auto-resume. This 'state lives in files, not the chat' design is the heart of the approach.

HOOKS: hooks/hooks.json registers Claude Code hooks across the lifecycle (SessionStart, PreToolUse, PostToolUse, SubagentStop, Stop, PreCompact, FileChanged) — used for a context-usage statusline, read/prompt injection guards, commit validation, workflow drift detection, worktree path guards, and update banners.

**Claude Code integration:** Multiple integration surfaces, and it is multi-tool, NOT Claude-Code-only:

1) NATIVE PLUGIN MANIFEST: The repo contains .claude-plugin/plugin.json declaring name 'gsd-core' (version shown in `next` as 1.3.1-dev.0) and registering commands ('./commands/gsd/') and hooks ('./hooks/hooks.json'). So GSD Core is structured as a native Claude Code plugin. (Verified: plugin.json registers commands + hooks paths.)

2) PRIMARY INSTALL = npx INSTALLER (not the plugin marketplace): The documented quickstart is `npx @opengsd/gsd-core@latest`, an interactive installer that prompts for your runtime and deploys the correct command/agent files. The README warns the installer is required for cross-runtime compatibility — do not copy files from agents/ or commands/ directly. The package exposes bin commands gsd-core (install.js) and gsd-tools (gsd-tools.cjs). NOTE: the older redux package also exposed a get-shit-done-redux bin; in @opengsd/gsd-core the verified bins are gsd-core and gsd-tools (the prior profile's 'get-shit-done-redux / gsd-sdk / gsd-tools' bin list was for the renamed/older package and is partly stale).

3) HOOKS: Uses Claude Code's hook system extensively (7 event types). Includes Cursor-specific hook scripts too.

4) SKILLS: GSD predates/parallels Anthropic's Skills; it is built on commands + subagents + hooks rather than the Skills mechanism. (No /skills directory in core.)

5) MCP: Core itself depends on @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk (^0.2.84) and ws (8.20.1); MCP is NOT central to the official core. A separate third-party packaging, jnuyens/gsd-plugin (latest v3.4.3, 2026-06-09, ~55 releases, actively maintained, based on the redux/gsd-core lineage and cherry-picking from gsd-core ~1.4.1), repackages GSD as a native marketplace plugin and ADDS an MCP server (project state as queryable resources/mutation tools) plus context:fork sub-agent isolation. Installed via `/plugin marketplace add jnuyens/gsd-plugin`, `/plugin install gsd@gsd-plugin`, `/reload-plugins`. It claims ~92% per-turn token reduction vs the npx install but is maintained by a different person (Jasper Nuyens), uses an independent '+2 major offset' versioning (v3.x vs core v1.x), and lags upstream.

MULTI-TOOL SUPPORT: README lists Claude Code, OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Kimi CLI, Kilo, Codex, Copilot, Cursor, Windsurf 'and more.' Dedicated GEMINI.md/AGENTS.md/.clinerules/gemini-extension.json files confirm genuine multi-runtime support — though Claude Code is the lead/best-supported target.

**Install:** Official (recommended), runs an interactive installer that detects your runtime:
  npx @opengsd/gsd-core@latest
Then inside the agent:
  /gsd-new-project

Native Claude Code marketplace plugin (THIRD-PARTY repackaging by jnuyens, adds MCP + token optimization, independent versioning ~v3.4.3, lags upstream):
  /plugin marketplace add jnuyens/gsd-plugin
  /plugin install gsd@gsd-plugin
  /reload-plugins

Do NOT manually copy agents/ or commands/ — the README states the installer is required for cross-runtime compatibility. The original npm package get-shit-done-cc (latest 1.42.3) is DEPRECATED ('Package no longer supported') — do not use it. The interim package @opengsd/get-shit-done-redux is also deprecated and redirects to gsd-core (latest tag stalled at 1.1.0, next at 1.2.0-rc.5).

**Maintenance:** Actively and intensely maintained as of 2026-06-09. open-gsd/gsd-core latest release v1.4.3 published 2026-06-09T17:49Z (a patch fixing /gsd-update and orchestration bugs); the repo was pushed to ~10 minutes later (pushed_at 2026-06-09T17:50Z). Created 2026-05-22. ~137 contributors (verified via paginated API: 100 + 37 = 137; the '138' figure is accurate to within rounding/anon). npm @opengsd/gsd-core last published v1.4.3 on 2026-06-09 with ~3,957 downloads in the week of May 27–Jun 2 (last-week window). Not archived, not disabled.

GOVERNANCE / FORK HISTORY (important): This is a community fork. Original project was gsd-build/get-shit-done by 'TÂCHES' (Lex Christopherson / glittercowboy), now ~64,053 stars / ~5,457 forks. Per the maintainer's own discussion posts (#109) and secondary reporting, TÂCHES went silent on 2026-04-01, social accounts were deleted, and a $GSD crypto token tied to the project was publicly linked to a rug-pull. On 2026-05-22 a GSD collaborator, 'trek-e', forked the pre-incident MIT codebase (branches/tags/attribution preserved), stripped token/social references, and relaunched under the open-gsd org — first as get-shit-done-redux, then renamed to gsd-core. The original repo is NOT GitHub-archived (still public, default branch main, pushed 2026-05-31); 'locked' means its Issues were closed/locked, a top-of-README warning + New-Issue redirect were added, and an Actions workflow auto-closes new issues. Its npm package (get-shit-done-cc) IS deprecated. So: solo-led-by-trek-e under an org wrapper (top contributors verified: trek-e 1,866 commits, original author glittercowboy 945, then Tibsfox 127, jeremymcs 83, viktorplus 47, long tail). Default branch is 'next' — note rapid RC churn.

**Popularity:** open-gsd/gsd-core (verified live 2026-06-09 via GitHub API): 3,315 stars, 202 forks, 44 open issues, ~137 contributors, MIT, default branch 'next'. npm @opengsd/gsd-core: ~3,957 downloads in the week of 2026-05-27 to 2026-06-02. CONTEXT: the ORIGINAL gsd-build/get-shit-done sits at 64,053 stars / 5,457 forks (verified) — so the famous 'GSD has 59k+ / 64k stars' headlines refer to the ORIGINAL repo, NOT this community fork. The fork is still early in re-accumulating stars (~3.3k).

**Best for:** Solo developers and small teams using Claude Code (or another supported CLI agent) on non-trivial, multi-step features or greenfield projects where AI sessions otherwise 'lose the plot.' Best for people who want structure, reviewable PR-per-phase increments, and durable cross-session memory, and who are comfortable with command-line tooling and some ceremony. Overkill for one-off scripts, quick edits, or developers who want minimal/zero workflow scaffolding. Teams needing strict supply-chain/security guarantees should treat it cautiously (see risks) and pin/review versions.

**Risks:** ["SUPPLY-CHAIN / TRUST (highest): The project's own history is a cautionary tale — the original maintainer vanished (last contact 2026-04-01), socials were deleted, and an associated $GSD token was publicly linked to a RUG-PULL. The independent RokketSec review and secondary reporting both flag that the ORIGINAL npm package keys reportedly remain with the previous developer, and the deprecated legacy packages remain live on the registry — leaving a path to future malicious updates/takeover targeting the existing install base. Use ONLY the fork's @opengsd/gsd-core; never the deprecated get-shit-done-cc.", "PROMPT-INJECTION / AGENT BLAST RADIUS: Audits found injection-defense hooks are advisory and overridable, @-file (and @~/) references can pull secrets into context, a frontmatter path bypass can read/write outside the project, and subagents have broad shell + filesystem access. A malicious repo/file could plausibly steer the agent. Run in sandboxed/least-privilege environments.", "NO CONFIRMED ACTIVE BACKDOOR: Both the maintainer's audit (293/293 tests, no active exfiltration payloads, zero known dependency vulns) and the independent RokketSec review concluded the current tracked source has no active malicious payload/backdoor — but 'no confirmed payload' is not 'proven safe.' Pin versions and review diffs.", "GOVERNANCE / ABANDONMENT: Community fork led primarily by one person (trek-e); could stall again. Org wrapper (open-gsd) mitigates but does not eliminate this.", "LOCK-IN: Low at the vendor level (multi-tool, MIT, plain Markdown/files you can keep), but moderate at the WORKFLOW level — adopting GSD's phase/state conventions across a team is a process commitment that's non-trivial to unwind.", "COMPLEXITY/COST: token spend and cognitive overhead are real ongoing costs; the npx path's overhead is significant enough that a third-party plugin exists specifically to reduce it."]

**Strengths:**
  - Directly attacks a real, well-known failure mode (context rot) with a coherent structural answer: lean main session + fresh-context subagents + file-based durable state (STATE.md/CONTEXT.md) that survives compaction and new sessions
  - Genuinely comprehensive and opinionated: ~100 slash commands and 36 specialized subagents (verified counts in the live 'next' branch) covering spec, planning, parallel 'wave' execution, verification, security audit, UI review, debugging, and shipping
  - Native Claude Code plugin manifest (.claude-plugin/plugin.json) + deep hook integration across 7 lifecycle events (SessionStart/PreToolUse/PostToolUse/SubagentStop/Stop/PreCompact/FileChanged) for guards, statusline, and drift detection
  - Truly multi-runtime (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, Cursor, Copilot, OpenCode, Windsurf, etc.) — reduces vendor lock-in to a single agent vendor
  - Spec-driven, PR-per-phase workflow encourages reviewable, auditable increments rather than one giant AI dump
  - MIT licensed with full attribution; the fork transparently preserved upstream provenance/history
  - Unusually high security transparency for a vibe-coding tool: published its own audit (293/293 tests) plus invited an independent third-party (RokketSec) review and published its findings

**Weaknesses:**
  - Heavy and complex: ~100 commands, 36 agents, many hooks, and a whole vocabulary (phases, waves, milestones) — steep learning curve and real ceremony for small tasks
  - Token/cost overhead: spawning many fresh ~200k-context subagents and re-reading state files can be expensive; the third-party plugin explicitly exists to cut overhead (claims ~92% per-turn token reduction), implying the npx path is costly
  - Governance fragility: effectively led by one fork maintainer (trek-e) who by their own statement is 'not the original author' and has 'no inside information' about the original; bus-factor and continuity risk remain
  - Version/identity confusion: original repo (64k stars; deprecated npm get-shit-done-cc) vs interim redux (deprecated, redirects to gsd-core) vs current @opengsd/gsd-core (v1.4.3) vs third-party marketplace plugin (jnuyens/gsd-plugin, independent v3.x versioning). Easy to install the wrong/abandoned thing
  - Fast-moving on a 'next' default branch with frequent RC releases — stability/churn risk
  - Security model is advisory, not enforcing: independent audit found prompt-injection 'guard' hooks issue warnings but don't block, @-file references can inline secrets (SSH keys, AWS creds) into agent context, frontmatter path bypass allows reads/writes outside project bounds, and agents get broad shell/filesystem access
  - Multi-tool breadth means Claude-Code-specific polish may outpace other runtimes; non-Claude support quality is uneven/unverified here

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Independently re-verified the riskiest claims against live sources on 2026-06-09; defaulted to skepticism. CONFIRMED via GitHub REST API: gsd-core 3,315 stars / 202 forks / 44 open issues / MIT / default branch 'next' / created 2026-05-22 / pushed+released 2026-06-09 / NOT archived / NOT disabled; release v1.4.3 (not prerelease) published 2026-06-09T17:49Z. Original gsd-build/get-shit-done = 64,053 stars / 5,457 forks / MIT / default branch main / NOT archived/disabled / pushed 2026-05-31. Contributor counts verified by pagination (137 total; trek-e 1,866, glittercowboy 945, Tibsfox 127, jeremymcs 83, viktorplus 47). npm: @opengsd/gsd-core latest=1.4.3 pub 2026-06-09, deps @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk ^0.2.84 + ws 8.20.1, bins gsd-core + gsd-tools, no deprecation; ~3,957 downloads for week 2026-05-27..06-02. get-shit-done-cc latest 1.42.3 DEPRECATED. @opengsd/get-shit-done-redux latest=1.1.0/next=1.2.0-rc.5, DEPRECATED redirecting to gsd-core. plugin.json (next) name 'gsd-core' v1.3.1-dev.0 registers commands+hooks. Third-party jnuyens/gsd-plugin v3.4.3, ~55 releases, MCP server + context:fork, ~92% token-reduction claim (vendor-stated). Rug-pull/fork narrative corroborated by maintainer Discussions #109 (fork rationale, 2026-04-01 silence, token=rug-pull, original issues locked+redirected) and #119 / RokketSec review (293/293 tests, no active payload; @~/ secret exfil, frontmatter path bypass, advisory-only hooks, broad shell access; original npm keys reportedly retained by prior dev), plus an independent AI Weekly write-up.

CORRECTIONS made to the prior profile: (1) Command count is 101 .md files in live 'next' (not '~70'); agent count is 36 (not 33). (2) Original repo is NOT GitHub-archived/'locked/relocated' at the repo level — it's still public on branch main; 'locked' applies only to its Issues (closed/locked + redirect + auto-close workflow). Reworded to avoid implying the whole repo is archived. (3) npm bins for @opengsd/gsd-core are gsd-core + gsd-tools (the 'get-shit-done-redux'/'gsd-sdk' bin list was stale from the older package); removed/updated. (4) Clarified the ~3,957 downloads is the 2026-05-27..06-02 last-week window, not a perpetual rate. (5) Contributors ~137 (claimed 138 — confirmed accurate to within rounding). UNVERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY (medium): the third-party plugin's 92% token-savings is vendor-stated; the rug-pull and 'npm keys retained by original dev' claims rest on the maintainer's posts + RokketSec summary + AI Weekly aggregation (itself sourced from Reddit r/ClaudeAI), not on-chain/registry forensics I performed myself. Despite these caveats, every load-bearing factual claim resolved as accurate, so overall confidence is HIGH.

### GSD Core (Git. Ship. Done — Core)
`https://github.com/open-gsd/gsd-core (npm: @opengsd/gsd-core; homepage opengsd.net). Community continuation/fork of the original gsd-build/get-shit-done ("Get Shit Done") by TÂCHES. The code was first mirrored to open-gsd/get-shit-done-redux on 2026-05-22 and later renamed/continued as gsd-core under the open-gsd org.`

**Category:** Spec-driven / context-engineering development workflow framework for AI coding agents (meta-prompting + phase-loop orchestration). Multi-runtime, with first-class Claude Code support.

**One-liner:** A spec-driven, context-engineering workflow that drives an AI coding agent through a disciplined Discuss to Plan to Execute to Verify to Ship loop, pushing heavy work into fresh-context subagents and persisting state to disk to beat "context rot."

**Philosophy:** "Beat context rot." Keep the main agent context lean and clean; offload heavy research/planning/execution to disposable fresh-context subagents; persist all project knowledge as inspectable plain-text files so work is resumable and auditable; put deterministic logic in code (Bash/Node) not in prompts; gate every phase with explicit verification and atomic, revertable commits. The org tagline: "keep the context clean, the work verified, and the git history honest — ship more, worry less." ("Git. Ship. Done.")

**How it works:** Core idea (verified against the official README): GSD attacks "context rot — the quality degradation that accumulates as an AI fills its context window — by running all heavy research, planning, and execution work in fresh-context subagents while keeping your main session lean." State is persisted as plain-text files so work survives session boundaries and resumes cleanly.

Phase loop: each milestone/phase repeats the five-step loop Discuss -> Plan -> Execute -> Verify -> Ship. Discuss captures implementation decisions; Plan researches/decomposes and validates the plan fits a fresh context; Execute runs plans in parallel "waves," each executor on a clean context with each task an atomic commit; Verify walks through what was built and spawns debug/fix work before declaring done; Ship opens a PR and archives the phase. (The README states the five phase names but does not enumerate explicit per-phase slash commands beyond the entry point.)

Slash commands: gsd-core uses the /gsd- hyphen prefix; the documented entry point is /gsd-new-project. The original TÂCHES build used the /gsd: colon namespace, and the third-party jnuyens/gsd-plugin still uses /gsd: commands (e.g. /gsd:resume-work) — a real source of prefix confusion. Other phase-command spellings (e.g. /gsd-discuss-phase, /gsd-plan-phase, /gsd-execute-phase, /gsd-verify-work, /gsd-ship) are inferred from the phase loop and secondary docs, NOT confirmed verbatim in the official README. When installed as a native Claude Code plugin, commands are namespaced /gsd-core:<command>. Reported command counts vary widely across analyses (~29 for the original TÂCHES build vs ~67/82 for the redux/core tree) and should be treated as approximate.

Subagents/agents: specialized roles such as gsd-project-researcher, gsd-roadmapper, gsd-planner, gsd-executor, gsd-verifier. Agent-count estimates differ across sources (~12 for the original build vs ~33 for the redux/core tree); several "researcher" agents reuse one definition and specialize via prompt. Treat counts as approximate.

Artifacts/files: written under a local planning directory. The README and CHANGELOG reference .planning/ (e.g. the v1.4.0 config-hot-reload hook targets .planning/config.json); some older analyses mention .plans/. Named artifacts include PROJECT.md, REQUIREMENTS.md (scoped requirement IDs), ROADMAP.md, STATE.md, CONTEXT.md, config.json and a research/ subdir. Design principle: deterministic logic lives in Bash/Node helpers, not in prompts.

Architecture note: v1 was Markdown prompts relying on LLM cooperation; v2 was rewritten as a TypeScript/Node application that controls the agent session externally (clearing context, injecting files, managing git branches, tracking tokens/cost, detecting stuck loops, recovering from crashes), enabling harder guardrails like schema-drift and scope-reduction detection. A 2026-05 security audit confirmed the codebase uses safe argv-array subprocess calls and no eval(), consistent with the "deterministic logic in code" claim.

**Claude Code integration:** Multi-tool, NOT Claude-only — but Claude Code is the flagship target. The npx installer prompts for runtime: Claude Code, OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Kilo, Codex, Copilot, Cursor, Windsurf, "and more," plus a global-vs-local choice (verified in the official README). The README notes the installer is required for cross-runtime compatibility.

For Claude Code it leans on native primitives: skill/slash commands, custom subagent definitions, and hooks wired via settings.json (SessionStart, PostToolUse, plus a statusline showing live context usage). As of v1.4.0 (2026-06-08, confirmed in CHANGELOG) gsd-core ships a .claude-plugin/plugin.json manifest so it installs as a NATIVE Claude Code plugin via `claude plugin install`, and registers three additional hook events — SubagentStop, Stop, PreCompact — plus a FileChanged hook (matcher: config.json) wired to a gsd-config-reload.js helper that hot-reloads .planning/config.json. Commands then appear as /gsd-core:<command>. The OFFICIAL gsd-core package does NOT itself ship an MCP server.

A separate, well-known third-party packaging (jnuyens/gsd-plugin, by Jasper Nuyens) wraps GSD as a Claude Code marketplace plugin and DOES add an MCP server (exposing project state as queryable resources/mutation tools) plus extra hooks and a HANDOFF.json handoff file (a PreCompact hook writes HANDOFF.json; SessionStart auto-invokes /gsd:resume-work). It is explicitly third-party, based on open-gsd/get-shit-done-redux (treats redux as upstream from v2.43.6 onward), and still uses /gsd: colon-prefixed commands — not the official org package.

**Install:** Recommended (multi-runtime, official): run `npx @opengsd/gsd-core@latest`, answer the runtime + global/local prompts, then start with `/gsd-new-project` in your agent. Native Claude Code plugin (gsd-core v1.4.0+, 2026-06-08): install via `claude plugin install` using the bundled .claude-plugin/plugin.json (commands appear as /gsd-core:<command>). Third-party Claude Code marketplace packaging (jnuyens/gsd-plugin, optional, adds an MCP server): `/plugin marketplace add jnuyens/gsd-plugin` then `/plugin install gsd@gsd-plugin` then `/reload-plugins`. If migrating off the old TÂCHES build, uninstall the legacy packages first (e.g. the global get-shit-done-cc / get-shit-done install) and switch to @opengsd/* — the open-gsd team explicitly recommends this because the ORIGINAL npm package's publish keys remain under the control of the unreachable original maintainer. Caveat: exact phase-command spellings beyond /gsd-new-project are inferred, not confirmed verbatim from the README.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained as of 2026-06-09 (research date). open-gsd/gsd-core: created 2026-05-22, last push 2026-06-09 (same day as research), MIT, not archived, 44 open issues. npm @opengsd/gsd-core: first published 2026-05-30 (npm registry "created" timestamp; the GitHub repo predates the npm package by ~8 days), latest 1.4.3 published 2026-06-09. Eleven versions shipped between 2026-05-30 and 2026-06-09 (incl. 1.4.0 on 2026-06-08 and patches 1.4.1/1.4.2/1.4.3 on 2026-06-09) — very rapid iteration. Governance: this is a community FORK/continuation, NOT the original. Lineage: original = gsd-build/get-shit-done by TÂCHES (Lex Christopherson), ~64,053 stars. After the maintainer went unreachable (no contact since 2026-04-01, socials deleted) and the $GSD token was linked to a rug-pull, collaborator trek-e mirrored the MIT code bit-for-bit (per Discussion #109: 394 branches, 229 tags, 77 issues, 17 PRs) to open-gsd/get-shit-done-redux on 2026-05-22, later renamed/continued as gsd-core under the open-gsd org (org created 2026-05-22, ~197 followers, 10 repos: gsd-core, gsd-pi, gsd-browser, gsd-cursor, gsd-cloud-daemon, agent-inbox, marketplace-gsd-dev, gsd-test-runner, gsd-pi-config, gsd-pi-presets — none archived). So: actively (rapidly) maintained, but by a NEW, small community team rather than the original author, and itself a continuity fork born of an abandonment.

**Popularity:** open-gsd/gsd-core: ~3,315 GitHub stars, ~202 forks (verified via GitHub API on 2026-06-09; up ~1 star from an earlier 3,314 read — figures drift). npm downloads: the often-cited "~3,957/week" is a STALE/misleading figure — npm's trailing-week endpoint returned 3,957 for the window 2026-05-27 to 2026-06-02, which captures only the package's first ~4 days of life (zero downloads before 2026-05-30, then 115 on 5/30, ~1,023 on 5/31, ~1,398 on 6/1, ~1,421 on 6/2). Current daily volume is materially higher, so any single weekly snapshot understates real adoption; treat absolute download counts as a rough, fast-moving signal. The ORIGINAL TÂCHES repo it descends from has ~64,053 stars / ~5,457 forks, so the brand/ecosystem is very popular even though the new official package is small and young. Blog claims of "53.9K"/"58.9K"/"58K"/"48K" stars all refer to the original repo at various points in time, NOT gsd-core, and some third-party blogs conflate the two (and even misquote the version line, e.g. citing the original's "v1.38.5" alongside gsd-core).

**Best for:** Developers running long, multi-session, spec-driven builds with an AI coding agent on small-to-medium greenfield or feature-scoped projects — especially those who hit quality degradation on 50+ file codebases and want disciplined planning, durable resumable state, atomic commits, and an explicit verification gate. Less suited to one-off edits, tiny scripts, or teams wanting a zero-config lightweight helper. Good for people who deliberately want a Claude Code-native (or cross-CLI) workflow without a proprietary cloud runtime — provided they install from the official @opengsd/gsd-core source and review the hooks/permissions before granting autonomy.

**Risks:** ["TRUST/REPUTATION (most important): the ORIGINAL upstream by TÂCHES (Lex Christopherson) suffered a maintainer disappearance (unreachable since 2026-04-01, deleted socials) and the associated $GSD Solana token was publicly linked to a rug-pull (community estimates ~$500K extracted across hackathon rewards, contest prizes, token fees, and a final dump). gsd-core was created specifically to escape that — it stripped the $GSD token badges and @gsd_foundation references — but the episode is a real reputational stain on the 'GSD' name. Do NOT install random GSD/$GSD-token packages.", "SUPPLY-CHAIN (specific, confirmed): the ORIGINAL npm package's publish keys remain under the control of the unreachable original maintainer — a third-party (RokketSec) audit on 2026-05-23 called this the 'single highest-risk vector,' since that maintainer could push a malicious update to the legacy package name at any time. Mitigation: install ONLY from @opengsd/gsd-core (the fork team holds those keys), uninstall any legacy get-shit-done packages, and pin versions. No active malware/exfiltration payload was found in the current gsd-core source as of the 2026-05-22 audit (293 security tests passed; zero known dep vulns), but it ships Node/Bash helpers and registers hooks (SessionStart/PostToolUse/PreCompact/SubagentStop/Stop/FileChanged) that execute on tool calls and session events — review the manifest/hooks before granting autonomy.", "SECURITY ARCHITECTURE: per the third-party audit, the security hooks are advisory-only (warn but never block injections), @~/ file references can inline host secrets into prompts, resolveFrontmatterPath() can bypass project-root containment, and agents receive root-level access with no circuit breaker. The auditor's conclusion: a secure codebase today, but trust boundaries need hardening before production use. Markdown-link injection and base64-scanner edge cases were also flagged (tracked in issues #113-116).", "GOVERNANCE/BUS-FACTOR: the official maintainership is a small community org (~197 followers, ~10 repos, a handful of visible members) and a brand-new fork; long-term continuity is not guaranteed (it is itself a continuity fork born of an abandonment).", "COMPLEXITY/WORKFLOW LOCK-IN: heavy conventions and many artifacts create process lock-in (project state lives in GSD's .planning/ format); migrating away means unwinding that. Multi-runtime support mitigates tool lock-in but not workflow lock-in.", "VERSION/IDENTITY CONFUSION: multiple coexisting names (get-shit-done, get-shit-done-redux, gsd-core) and prefixes (/gsd: vs /gsd- vs /gsd-core:), plus a third-party plugin that adds its own MCP server, raise the risk of installing an outdated, abandoned, third-party, or impostor variant."]

**Strengths:**
  - Directly attacks context rot: heavy work runs in fresh-context subagents so the main session stays lean and quality holds over long sessions (README's central design goal; one comparison reported quality maintained into the 3rd hour on 50+ file projects)
  - Durable, inspectable state on disk (PROJECT/REQUIREMENTS/ROADMAP/STATE/CONTEXT.md under .planning/) — sessions resume cleanly and every intermediate step is auditable
  - Built-in quality gates: explicit Verify phase, atomic per-task commits that are independently revertable, plus schema-drift and scope-reduction (dropped-requirement) detection
  - Uses native Claude Code primitives (skills/slash commands, subagents, hooks, statusline) and now ships as a native CC plugin via .claude-plugin/plugin.json — no proprietary runtime to trust; the official package ships NO MCP server
  - Genuinely multi-runtime (Claude Code, OpenCode, Gemini CLI, Kilo, Codex, Copilot, Cursor, Windsurf, and more), reducing single-tool lock-in
  - MIT licensed; full git history, all 394 branches and 229 tags, and contributor attribution preserved through the fork
  - Deterministic logic pushed into Bash/Node helpers rather than prompts; a 2026-05 security audit found no eval(), no hardcoded secrets, and safe argv-array subprocess calls
  - Security/governance handled in the open: published audit transparency report, 293 passing security tests, and third-party (RokketSec) review with tracked follow-up issues

**Weaknesses:**
  - Heavyweight/opinionated: the full phase loop, dozens of slash commands, and many agent definitions impose ceremony that can be overkill for small tasks or quick fixes
  - v2 is a TypeScript application (not just Markdown prompts), adding integration complexity and friction when combining with other frameworks (e.g. Superpowers)
  - Best for small-to-medium projects; analyses note it 'might not go deep enough in some places and lack detail in others' and isn't a fit for every requirement
  - Brand-new official package: extremely young (first npm publish 2026-05-30) with low absolute download numbers, meaning limited battle-testing of the new package line
  - Documentation/naming fragmentation: prefix drift (original /gsd: vs gsd-core /gsd- vs plugin /gsd-core:), .planning/ vs .plans/ in older docs, and three parallel packagings (official npx, native CC plugin, third-party jnuyens/gsd-plugin with its own MCP server and /gsd: commands) make it easy to install the 'wrong' GSD
  - Parallel-wave execution + many subagents can increase token/compute spend versus a single-agent flow
  - Security hooks are advisory-only ('warn but never block') per the third-party audit; agents run with root-level access and no circuit breaker, and @~/ file references can inline host secrets (e.g. ~/.ssh/id_rsa) into prompts — architectural trust boundaries still need hardening

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-verified the riskiest claims on 2026-06-09 against authoritative APIs and primary sources. (1) STARS/POPULARITY: GitHub API gave gsd-core 3,315 stars / 202 forks (corrected from 3,314 — value drifts), created 2026-05-22, pushed 2026-06-09, MIT, not archived, 44 open issues, homepage opengsd.net. Original gsd-build/get-shit-done = 64,053 stars / 5,457 forks, created 2025-12-14, NOT archived, still attributed to TÂCHES. Corrected a misleading npm-downloads claim: the '~3,957/week' figure is real but stale — it covers the package's first ~4 days (zero before 2026-05-30), so it understates current adoption; flagged accordingly. Confirmed blog '48K/53.9K/58.9K stars' all refer to the ORIGINAL repo, not gsd-core. (2) MAINTENANCE: npm registry confirmed @opengsd/gsd-core latest=1.4.3, FIRST published 2026-05-30 (not 2026-05-22; that earlier date is the GitHub repo/org creation), 11 versions through 2026-06-09 — actively/rapidly maintained by a small community org (197 followers, 10 repos, none archived). (3) CLAUDE CODE INTEGRATION: CHANGELOG confirms native CC plugin + .claude-plugin/plugin.json + `claude plugin install` + SubagentStop/Stop/PreCompact/FileChanged hooks landed in v1.4.0 (2026-06-08). Clarified that the OFFICIAL package ships NO MCP server — the MCP server, HANDOFF.json, and /gsd: resume hooks belong to the THIRD-PARTY jnuyens/gsd-plugin (based on get-shit-done-redux). README confirms the multi-runtime installer and the /gsd-new-project entry point; other phase-command spellings remain inferred, not verbatim-confirmed. (4) SECURITY/TRUST: fork announcement (Discussion #109) and aiweekly corroborate the TÂCHES disappearance (unreachable since 2026-04-01), deleted socials, and the $GSD rug-pull (~$500K community estimate). MATERIALLY STRENGTHENED the security section using two primary audits (Discussion #119): the official audit (2026-05-22, 293 tests passed, no active exfiltration payload, zero known dep vulns, 4 tracked gaps) AND an independent RokketSec audit (2026-05-23) that names the retained ORIGINAL npm publish keys as the single highest-risk vector and flags advisory-only hooks, @~/ secret-inlining, root-level agent access with no circuit breaker, and a frontmatter path-containment bypass. No evidence of malware in CURRENT gsd-core source. Net changes: corrected star count to 3,315; corrected/contextualized npm first-publish date (2026-05-30) and the stale download window; separated official-package vs third-party-plugin MCP/hooks; added concrete audit-backed security findings. Confidence raised to HIGH on repo identity, stars/forks, dates, license, npm versions, CC plugin mechanism, the fork/abandonment/rug-pull narrative, and the supply-chain key-retention risk; residual uncertainty only on approximate command/agent COUNTS and the exact spelling of phase commands beyond /gsd-new-project.

### Ralph Wiggum (a.k.a. "Ralph Loop")
`https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/tree/main/plugins/ralph-wiggum (manifest name: "ralph-wiggum" v1.0.0; the marketplace/claude.com listing is branded "Ralph Loop" with the /ralph-loop command). Not a standalone repo — it is a subfolder of the anthropics/claude-code monorepo.`

**Category:** Agentic-coding autonomy / iterative auto-loop — official Anthropic Claude Code plugin (slash commands + Stop hook). marketplace.json category is literally "development". NOT an MCP server, not a CLI/npx tool, not a Skill.

**One-liner:** Official Anthropic Claude Code plugin that turns Claude into a "while-true" loop — it intercepts the session-exit (Stop) event and re-feeds the same prompt over and over until a completion phrase is emitted or a max-iteration cap is hit, letting work accumulate in files and git history rather than in the context window.

**Philosophy:** "Ralph is a Bash loop." Treat the LLM as a programmable computer and let an agent retry the same prompt indefinitely, accumulating durable progress in files + git history (not in the context window) to defeat context rot/compaction. Embrace eventual consistency: iteration beats first-shot perfection, failures are tuning data, and the operator's job is to 'watch the loop', shape the prompt/specs, and keep the system improving — like clay on a pottery wheel. Creator Geoff Huntley has used it to clone commercial products (e.g. tax software, an Atlassian product) and frames it as a step toward autonomous 'evolutionary software', while voicing genuine unease (per The Register, he questions whether making this possible "hexed the software industry") about how cheaply it can clone product features.

**How it works:** Design philosophy (from creator Geoffrey/Geoff Huntley, ghuntley.com/ralph and ghuntley.com/loop): "Ralph is a Bash loop." The canonical raw form is `while :; do cat PROMPT.md | claude-code ; done` (this exact line is quoted by The Register). The insight: progress should accumulate in DURABLE artifacts (files, git history) — NOT in the LLM context window — so each iteration can start fresh and sidestep "context rot" and compaction failures. Mantras: Iteration > Perfection; Failures Are Data; Operator Skill Matters; Persistence Wins ("eventual consistency").

The raw technique (not the plugin) uses external files as state/spec: PROMPT.md (the single-iteration instruction, re-fed each loop), fix_plan.md (prioritized TODO list updated each loop), a specs/ directory (one spec per file), and AGENT.md (build/run instructions + accumulated learnings). Huntley explicitly warns AGAINST premature multi-agent / agent-to-agent complexity.

THE OFFICIAL PLUGIN is a streamlined, in-session implementation (it does NOT shell out to an external bash `while` loop and does NOT itself create PROMPT.md/fix_plan.md/specs — those are author conventions you write yourself):
1. You run `/ralph-loop "<prompt>" --max-iterations <n> --completion-promise "<text>"`.
2. A setup script (under scripts/, added in the Jan 2026 hardening) writes a state file at `.claude/ralph-loop.local.md` — YAML frontmatter holding iteration, max_iterations, completion_promise, with the prompt text after a `---` delimiter.
3. Claude works the task.
4. When Claude tries to end its turn, the Stop hook (hooks/stop-hook.sh) fires. CONFIRMED behavior read from the live script: it reads the state file and the session transcript (JSONL), extracts the last assistant message, then decides:
   - No state file -> allow exit.
   - iteration >= max_iterations (max>0) -> stop and delete the state file.
   - If a --completion-promise was set, it uses a Perl -0777 multiline regex (`perl -0777 -pe 's/.*?<promise>(.*?)<\/promise>.*/$1/s'`) to extract text inside <promise>...</promise> tags, and on exact string match it stops.
   - Otherwise it increments the counter (NEXT_ITERATION=$((ITERATION+1)), written atomically via a `${RALPH_STATE_FILE}.tmp.$$` file + sed), re-extracts the prompt, and returns JSON {"decision":"block","reason":<prompt>}, forcing Claude to keep going.
5. /cancel-ralph removes the state file to stop immediately.

Artifacts the PLUGIN itself creates/uses: `.claude/ralph-loop.local.md` (loop state) and a transient `.claude/ralph-loop.local.md.tmp.$$`. Everything else (specs, plans) is operator-authored convention.

**Claude Code integration:** Claude-Code-NATIVE plugin, distributed through Anthropic's official marketplace ("claude-code-plugins", hosted in the anthropics/claude-code monorepo; also surfaced at claude.com/plugins/ralph-loop, publisher Anthropic). VERIFIED directory contents at plugins/ralph-wiggum/: .claude-plugin/ (manifest), README.md (~5.2 KB), commands/, hooks/, and scripts/. It uses two Claude Code extension mechanisms: (a) SLASH COMMANDS in commands/ and (b) a Stop HOOK (hooks/stop-hook.sh). CORRECTION: the commands/ directory contains THREE command files — ralph-loop.md, cancel-ralph.md, AND help.md — so there is also a /help command the original profile omitted. It is NOT an MCP server, NOT a Skill, and NOT an npx/CLI tool. The OFFICIAL plugin is Claude-Code-only. The broader Ralph TECHNIQUE is tool-agnostic (a bash loop around any agent CLI) and has independent ports — e.g. vercel-labs/ralph-loop-agent, snarktank/ralph, iannuttall/ralph, frankbria/ralph-claude-code, mikeyobrien/ralph-orchestrator. Windows caveat: the hook is a bash script (the README itself is bash-centric and does not document a Windows path), so on Windows you may need to point the cached plugin's hooks at Git Bash if `bash` resolves to WSL — this is community-observed, not stated in the official README.

**Install:** From inside Claude Code:
1. Add the marketplace:  /plugin marketplace add anthropics/claude-code
2. Install the plugin:    /plugin install ralph-wiggum@claude-code-plugins
Then run e.g.:  /ralph-loop "Build a REST API for todos. CRUD + validation + tests. Output <promise>COMPLETE</promise> when done." --completion-promise "COMPLETE" --max-iterations 50
Cancel with:    /cancel-ralph   (Help: /help)
(Verified marketplace name in marketplace.json is "claude-code-plugins" and the verified manifest plugin name is "ralph-wiggum". The official claude.com listing shows the example install/run command `/ralph-loop "your prompt here" --max-iterations 10 --completion-promise "DONE"`. Some third-party listings show alternative install targets — confirm the exact suffix with /plugin marketplace list after adding it. NOTE: the README itself does not spell out the two-step marketplace-add + install commands; those follow the standard Claude Code plugin install flow.)

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained by Anthropic (organization-owned, not a solo side project). VERIFIED via GitHub commits API for path plugins/ralph-wiggum — exactly three commits: first commit 2025-11-16 by Daisy S. Hollman ("feat: Add ralph-wiggum plugin for iterative self-referential development"); then 2026-01-05 Anthony Morris ("fix(ralph-wiggum): move multi-line bash from command to setup script") and 2026-01-06 Anthony Morris ("fix(ralph-wiggum): add :* to allowed-tools pattern to permit arguments"). Both Jan 2026 commits are bug/security hardening — the multi-line-bash move reduces command-injection surface and is what keeps the plugin working under the post-CVE-2025-54795 restriction (Claude Code v1.0.20+, Aug 2025, blocked $() substitution and multi-line bash). Importantly, the official plugin was first authored 2025-11-16 — AFTER that August patch — so it is built for the patched runtime and is current/functional; reports of "the ralph plugin is broken by the CVE patch" refer to older community implementations, NOT this official plugin. Manifest version 1.0.0 — no formal release/tag cadence; ships as part of the monorepo. Parent repo anthropics/claude-code is NOT archived and was pushed/updated 2026-06-09 (same day as this check). plugin.json author of record: Daisy Hollman (daisy@anthropic.com). Governance goes through Anthropic's normal PR process. Confidence: high (read directly from plugin.json, marketplace.json, stop-hook.sh, directory listing, and the commits API).

**Popularity:** The plugin: the official claude.com/plugins/ralph-loop listing reports 178,034 installs (publisher Anthropic); the claudepluginhub aggregator shows the same figure. It has NO standalone GitHub repo / star count of its own — it is a subfolder of anthropics/claude-code. CONFIRMED via GitHub repos API (2026-06-09): parent repo has 131,297 stars, 21,273 forks, 8,597 open issues, not archived. The underlying Ralph TECHNIQUE is very widely discussed (The Register, 2026-01-27; numerous blogs; many independent ports each with their own stars). Bottom line: high adoption, but "stars" is the wrong metric since it is not a standalone repo; install count is the relevant figure and comes from Anthropic's own listing (so reasonably trustworthy, though it is a snapshot, not a live API number).

**Best for:** Developers running well-scoped, machine-verifiable, iteration-heavy tasks where success can be checked automatically — e.g. greenfield prototypes with clear specs, large mechanical refactors/framework migrations, driving a test suite to green via TDD, or batch fix-ups (lint/type/test errors). Ideally an operator who understands prompt/context engineering, will write good specs and a completion promise, runs in a sandbox/container or disposable branch, and is comfortable babysitting (or budgeting for) a multi-iteration run. Poor fit for ambiguous/architectural/security-critical work, one-shot edits, or anyone wanting hands-off magic with no verification harness.

**Risks:** ["SECURITY (biggest): Autonomous loops are typically run with permission prompts disabled (auto-accept / --dangerously-skip-permissions 'YOLO mode'). That removes Claude Code's tool-approval boundary, so a sandbox/container becomes your ONLY guardrail; without one, the agent can read/exfiltrate or clobber SSH keys, tokens, cookies, configs. Run in an isolated container with only the project dir mounted. (The OFFICIAL plugin loops in-session via the Stop hook and does NOT itself force --dangerously-skip-permissions, but practical unattended Ralph use is strongly associated with it.)", "RUNAWAY COST: an unbounded or high --max-iterations loop can silently burn money — ~$10/hour of compute per The Register, $50-100+ per long run per community blogs; always set a conservative cap (community guidance ~20). The README itself emphasizes max-iterations as the primary safety mechanism.", "CORRECTNESS / sycophancy loop: the model may declare success, delete needed files, or invent syntax to 'satisfy' the prompt; without an independent verifier it can loop on garbage. Mitigate with strict tests/linters in the loop and human review of the diff.", "PLUGIN-MARKETPLACE SUPPLY-CHAIN CONTEXT (separate from this plugin's own trust): independent researchers (PromptArmor; GMO Flatt Security/RyotaK; reported by The Hacker News, eSecurity Planet, Feb 2026) demonstrated that the Claude Code plugin-marketplace and GitHub Actions integration could be abused (injected marketplace plugins; a checkWritePermissions flaw patched in claude-code GitHub Actions v1.0.94 allowing secret/OIDC exfiltration). These are CLAUDE-CODE-platform issues, not flaws in the ralph-wiggum plugin itself, but they reinforce: only install plugins from marketplaces you trust, and prefer this first-party plugin over the many third-party 'ralph' clones on aggregators.", "TRUST / LOCK-IN: LOW for the official plugin — first-party Anthropic, in their monorepo, only 3 verified commits (initial + 2 security fixes), and the loop is a tiny inspectable bash hook + state file. Lock-in is minimal since the underlying technique is just a loop, reproducible on any agent. HIGHER trust risk applies to the many third-party 'ralph' clones (solo-maintained, varying quality, possible abandonment — at least one referenced clone, dial481/ralph, now 404s) — prefer the official plugin or vet forks carefully.", "COMPLEXITY: low to set up, but high operational discipline required (good specs, completion criteria, sandboxing, monitoring); easy to misuse on tasks it is explicitly unsuited for."]

**Strengths:**
  - First-party and verified by Anthropic, distributed through the official marketplace and in Anthropic's own monorepo — low supply-chain risk vs. random third-party Ralph repos
  - Dead-simple, robust loop mechanism: a single Stop hook + a small state file (verified in stop-hook.sh); no external bash wrapper or background daemon, so it runs inside one normal Claude Code session
  - Two built-in safety/exit levers: --max-iterations (hard cap) and --completion-promise (explicit <promise>X</promise> success signal); README explicitly recommends max-iterations as the primary safeguard
  - Embodies the genuinely useful core insight — accumulate progress in files + git, not in the context window — which mitigates context rot and compaction failures on long tasks
  - Excellent for grindy, verifiable, repetitive work: TDD loops, large mechanical refactors/migrations, getting a test suite green, fixing lint/type errors
  - /cancel-ralph gives a clean immediate kill switch; state is one inspectable file (.claude/ralph-loop.local.md). A /help command is also bundled
  - Actively maintained by an org with a real review process; the only two follow-up commits (Jan 2026) were security/permission hardening, and the plugin is built for the post-CVE-2025-54795 patched runtime

**Weaknesses:**
  - completion-promise is EXACT string match on text inside <promise> tags only — the README itself states you cannot use it for multiple completion conditions; no OR/fuzzy match, so it is easy to mis-signal completion or never trigger it (README steers you to rely on --max-iterations instead)
  - No semantic verifier/judge of its own — it does NOT check that work is actually correct; if you don't bake verification (tests/linters) into the prompt, it will happily loop on broken or hallucinated work ('sycophancy loop' risk)
  - Cost is real and can run away. The Register cites roughly US $10/hour of compute for this style of work; community blogs cite $50-100+ for a 50-iteration run on a medium/large codebase (NOT from Anthropic). The iteration cap is as much a budget control as a safety control
  - The hook is a bash script — the README is bash-centric with no documented Windows path, so Windows users frequently must hand-edit the cached hooks config to force Git Bash instead of WSL
  - Explicitly unsuited to ambiguous requirements, architectural decisions, security-critical code, exploratory work, and anything needing human judgment
  - To run truly unattended (no per-tool approval prompts) you generally pair the technique with Claude Code's auto-accept / --dangerously-skip-permissions YOLO mode, which removes the permission safety net (see Risks)
  - The plugin only manages the loop; the high-leverage parts (good PROMPT.md, specs/, fix_plan.md, sandboxing) are still on the operator — 'operator skill matters'
  - Branding/naming is confusing: 'Ralph Loop' (marketplace + claude.com + /ralph-loop command) vs 'ralph-wiggum' (manifest/folder), plus many similarly named third-party clones on aggregators (michelabboud-ralph-wiggum, flora131-ralph, mariogiancini-ralph-loop-setup, etc.)

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** All riskiest claims independently re-checked against live primary sources on 2026-06-09.

CONFIRMED: (1) Integration mechanism = Claude Code plugin via slash commands + Stop hook (NOT MCP/CLI/Skill). Read stop-hook.sh directly: state file .claude/ralph-loop.local.md, Perl -0777 promise-tag regex, atomic .tmp.$$ + sed increment, {"decision":"block"} re-feed, completion via exact-match on <promise>. (2) plugin.json: name 'ralph-wiggum', v1.0.0, author Daisy Hollman (daisy@anthropic.com). (3) marketplace.json: marketplace name 'claude-code-plugins', plugin category 'development'. (4) Commit history via GitHub API: exactly 3 commits — 2025-11-16 Daisy S. Hollman (feat), 2026-01-05 + 2026-01-06 Anthony Morris (security/permission fixes). (5) Parent repo via GitHub API: 131,297 stars, 21,273 forks, 8,597 open issues, NOT archived, pushed 2026-06-09 (numbers slightly updated from the profile's ~131,300/~21,300). (6) Install count 178,034 confirmed on the official claude.com/plugins/ralph-loop page (publisher Anthropic) — upgraded from 'aggregator-only' to 'first-party listing', so trust raised. (7) Huntley as creator and the `while :; do cat PROMPT.md | claude-code ; done` canonical loop confirmed by The Register (2026-01-27).

CORRECTIONS/ADDITIONS made: (a) The commands/ directory has THREE files — ralph-loop.md, cancel-ralph.md, and help.md — so there is also a /help command the original profile missed (it claimed 'exactly two slash commands'). (b) plugin.json does NOT contain a category field; 'development' lives in marketplace.json (profile had implied category in the manifest). (c) The plugin folder also contains a scripts/ directory (the setup script from the Jan 2026 hardening), in addition to commands/ and hooks/. (d) Cost attribution corrected: The Register cites ~$10/hour, NOT the $50-100/run figure — that $50-100 is from community blogs, now labeled as such. (e) Added platform supply-chain context: CVE-2025-54795 (command injection, fixed Claude Code v1.0.20, Aug 2025) and the Feb 2026 marketplace/GitHub-Actions research (PromptArmor; GMO Flatt/RyotaK; checkWritePermissions flaw patched in GH Actions v1.0.94) — these are Claude Code platform issues, NOT flaws in ralph-wiggum, and importantly the official plugin was authored AFTER the Aug 2025 patch and is functional under it. (f) Noted a referenced third-party clone (dial481/ralph) now 404s — minor evidence of clone churn. Stars/forks were marginally stale (rounded), the 'two slash commands' count was wrong (it is three, incl. /help), the cost source was mis-attributed, and the plugin is confirmed actively maintained and functional (not abandoned, no malware in the plugin itself).
