### Ralph Wiggum (ralph-wiggum)
`https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/tree/main/plugins/ralph-wiggum (lives inside the monorepo anthropics/claude-code; the broader technique originates from Geoffrey Huntley, https://ghuntley.com/ralph/)`

**Category:** Claude Code plugin: autonomous/iterative agentic-coding loop ("Ralph" technique). It is a native Claude Code plugin (Stop-hook + slash commands + a bash setup script), not a standalone CLI or MCP server.

**One-liner:** A tiny native Claude Code plugin that turns one session into a self-referential "while-true" loop: a Stop hook blocks Claude from exiting and re-feeds the same prompt every iteration until a completion promise is emitted or a max-iteration cap is hit.

**Philosophy:** Geoffrey Huntley's Ralph technique (coined ~July 2025): "Ralph is a Bash loop" - `while :; do cat PROMPT.md | claude-code; done`. Named after the cheerfully persistent Simpsons character (Huntley also nods to 1980s slang for vomiting, and to Ralph's mix of ignorance, persistence, and optimism). Core tenets surfaced in the plugin README and Huntley's writing: iteration over perfection ("eventual consistency" - success emerges over many passes, not one); failures are predictable, informative data that drive prompt tuning ("deterministically bad in an undeterministic world"); operator/prompt quality is the real lever; persistence + a fresh on-disk view each loop beats clever one-shotting; single-task, single-repo, monolithic loop (deliberately NOT a distributed multi-agent system).

**How it works:** Mechanism (verified by reading source files in anthropics/claude-code@main and cross-checking docs):

PHASES / FLOW:
1. User runs `/ralph-loop "<prompt>" [--max-iterations N] [--completion-promise "TEXT"]` once.
2. The command (commands/ralph-loop.md) invokes scripts/setup-ralph-loop.sh (via the `!`/Bash exec syntax with allowed-tools scoped to ONLY that script), which parses args and writes a state file `.claude/ralph-loop.local.md` — YAML frontmatter (`active`, `iteration: 1`, `max_iterations`, `completion_promise`, `started_at`) followed by the prompt body after the closing `---`. NOTE: as of the Jan 2026 fixes, multi-line bash was deliberately moved OUT of the command file into this setup script (see maintenance).
3. Claude works on the task in the SAME session, editing files.
4. When Claude tries to stop, the registered Stop hook (hooks/stop-hook.sh, wired via hooks/hooks.json) fires.
5. The hook reads the state file and the session transcript (transcript_path from hook stdin JSON, parsed with jq). It checks, in order: state file missing -> allow exit; corrupted numeric fields -> delete state + allow exit; `max_iterations > 0 && iteration >= max_iterations` -> stop; missing/empty transcript -> stop; completion promise satisfied -> stop.
6. Completion detection: it extracts text inside `<promise>...</promise>` tags from the last assistant message and does a LITERAL exact-string equality test against `completion_promise`. Only an exact match exits. The README itself confirms "exact string matching" and that you "cannot use it for multiple completion conditions."
7. If none of the exit conditions hold, the hook increments `iteration`, then returns JSON `{"decision":"block","reason":<the original prompt text>,...}`. The `reason` field re-injects the SAME prompt as Claude's next input.
8. Repeat. State persists across iterations through the file system + git history (the "self-reference" is Claude seeing its own prior edits, NOT conversation memory). `/cancel-ralph` deletes `.claude/ralph-loop.local.md`. A `/help` command explains the technique.

ARTIFACTS CREATED: primarily one — `.claude/ralph-loop.local.md` (the loop state file).

PLUGIN CONTENTS (~8 files): .claude-plugin/plugin.json; README.md; commands/ralph-loop.md, commands/cancel-ralph.md, commands/help.md; hooks/hooks.json, hooks/stop-hook.sh; scripts/setup-ralph-loop.sh. No subagents, no MCP server, no skills/ dir.

ANTI-CHEATING DESIGN: both the setup script and the command file emit strong instructions that Claude must output the promise ONLY when "completely and unequivocally TRUE" and must "not lie to exit" — because the only graceful exit is a true promise or the iteration cap. (This exact design is the subject of a model-welfare critique — see weaknesses/risks.)

**Claude Code integration:** Claude-Code-NATIVE and Claude-Code-ONLY. It integrates purely via Claude Code's first-class plugin primitives:
- A Stop HOOK (hooks/hooks.json registers ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/stop-hook.sh on the `Stop` event) — this is the core mechanism.
- Two user SLASH COMMANDS: `/ralph-loop` and `/cancel-ralph` (plus `/help`). When installed as a plugin they are namespaced, e.g. `/ralph-wiggum:ralph-loop`.
- A bash SETUP SCRIPT invoked by the command, with `allowed-tools` scoped to only `Bash(${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/setup-ralph-loop.sh:*)` (the `:*` was added in Jan 2026 to permit passing arguments).
NOT used: no MCP server, no subagents, no Agent Skills (skills/), no npx/standalone CLI. It depends on Claude Code's Stop-hook API, transcript JSONL format, and `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, so it is not portable to Cursor/Copilot/Gemini/OpenCode. Hook scripts are bash and require `jq`/`perl`/`sed`/`awk`, so it is effectively macOS/Linux (and WSL/Git-Bash on Windows). IMPORTANT: Claude Code v1.0.20 (CVE-2025-54795 patch) tightened slash-command bash parsing and BROKE the original plugin (which had multi-line bash in the command file); the Jan 2026 commits restructured it to the current setup-script design so it works under the hardened parser. The generic "Ralph" TECHNIQUE is tool-agnostic (a bash `while` loop), and OTHER third-party implementations are multi-tool — but THIS Anthropic plugin is not.

**Install:** Verified against official docs (code.claude.com/docs/en/discover-plugins). The plugin lives in Anthropic's DEMO marketplace (`claude-code-plugins`, hosted at the anthropics/claude-code repo's plugins/ dir), which is DISTINCT from the auto-available official marketplace (`claude-plugins-official`). You must add the demo marketplace manually first:

1) /plugin marketplace add anthropics/claude-code
2) /plugin install ralph-wiggum@claude-code-plugins
3) /reload-plugins   (to activate without restarting)

Then use: /ralph-loop "Build X. Output <promise>DONE</promise> when all tests pass." --completion-promise "DONE" --max-iterations 50
Cancel with: /cancel-ralph

Alternative for local dev/testing: clone the repo and run `claude --plugin-dir ./plugins/ralph-wiggum`. REQUIRES a Claude Code >= v1.0.20 (ideally latest — earlier versions are deprecated and vulnerable to CVE-2025-54795). If `/plugin` is missing, update via `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code@latest` or `brew upgrade claude-code`. Needs bash + jq + perl on PATH (so *nix/WSL/Git-Bash, not native Windows PowerShell). NOTE: docs explicitly warn plugins execute arbitrary code with your privileges — only install from trusted sources.

**Maintenance:** Maintained by Anthropic (organization, not a solo hobby project), inside the heavily-active anthropics/claude-code monorepo. Author in plugin.json: Daisy Hollman (daisy@anthropic.com). plugin.json version: 1.0.0 (no separate tagged releases for the plugin; the monorepo versions Claude Code as a whole, so there is no plugin-specific "last release date").
Commit history for the plugins/ralph-wiggum path (verified via GitHub API, oldest->newest):
- 2025-11-16: initial public commit "feat: Add ralph-wiggum plugin for iterative self-referential development" (Daisy S. Hollman).
- 2026-01-05: "fix(ralph-wiggum): move multi-line bash from command to setup script" (Anthony Morris). CORRECTION TO PRIOR PROFILE: this is not merely a generic "reduce command-injection surface" tidy-up — it is specifically the fix that RESTORES the plugin after Claude Code v1.0.20's CVE-2025-54795 patch began blocking multi-line bash in slash-command files (which had broken the plugin).
- 2026-01-06: "fix(ralph-wiggum): add :* to allowed-tools pattern to permit arguments" (Anthony Morris).
Verdict: actively owned by Anthropic, last touched ~Jan 2026, low churn (small, stable plugin). NOT abandoned (repo archived=false, disabled=false). There WAS a multi-month window (roughly Aug 2025 v1.0.20 -> early Jan 2026) where the published plugin was effectively broken on patched Claude Code, which spawned third-party "works-after-CVE" reimplementations; this is now resolved upstream.

**Popularity:** The plugin has no standalone star count — it lives inside anthropics/claude-code (131,297 stars, 21,273 forks as of 2026-06-09; verified live via GitHub API), so those numbers reflect Claude Code overall, NOT this plugin. The "Ralph" idea is broadly popular: covered by The Register (2026-01-27, "$10 an hour" framing) and VentureBeat, and Boris Cherny (Claude Code's creator) publicly endorsed using Ralph (per The Register, citing his X post). Popular INDEPENDENT (non-Anthropic) implementations of the same technique, as a proxy for ecosystem interest (star counts re-verified live via GitHub API 2026-06-09):
- snarktank/ralph (Ryan Carson): ~20,077 stars — the MOST-starred third-party implementation; PRD-driven, supports Amp (default) or Claude Code. (Prior profile listed this repo with NO star count; corrected here.)
- frankbria/ralph-claude-code: ~9,283 stars (~9.3k, confirmed) — adds intelligent exit detection, rate limiting, safety guardrails.
- mikeyobrien/ralph-orchestrator: ~2,929 stars (CORRECTED UP from prior ~2.2k; it grew) — multi-backend orchestrator.
These third-party counts are now exact-as-fetched, not stale estimates.

**Best for:** Experienced/senior engineers running well-defined, verifiable, greenfield or self-contained tasks where success is machine-checkable (tests pass, lint clean, build green) and they can babysit cost. Good for: TDD-driven feature builds, scaffolding, repetitive refactors with a clear oracle, and learning how agentic loops behave. Poor fit for: ambiguous/open-ended work, design/architecture decisions needing human judgment, production incident debugging, cost-sensitive setups without supervision, and Windows-native (non-WSL) environments. Ensure you are on Claude Code >= v1.0.20 (ideally latest) so the plugin actually functions.

**Risks:** ["Runaway-cost / rate-limit exhaustion: the headline risk. Always set --max-iterations; do NOT run promise-less infinite loops unattended. No built-in spend cap (~$10/hr per The Register).", "Autonomy + permissions: in practice people run such loops with broad/auto-approved permissions (YOLO / --dangerously-skip-permissions) so the agent isn't blocked each turn - which hands an unattended loop wide latitude to edit files and run commands. Note the loop machinery itself rides on slash-command bash; CVE-2025-54795 (CVSS 8.7, command-injection / approval-bypass in Claude Code < v1.0.20) is a reminder to stay on a patched Claude Code.", "Plugins execute arbitrary code with your privileges (Anthropic's own docs warn this). The OFFICIAL plugin is small, auditable, and Anthropic-maintained, so trust risk is LOW for it - but be careful not to confuse it with similarly named third-party 'ralph' repos (snarktank/ralph ~20k, frankbria ~9.3k, mikeyobrien ~2.9k, dial481/ralph, alonisser/ralph), which have separate, unvetted maintainers. No malware or rug-pull reports were found for the official plugin or the major third-party repos as of 2026-06-09.", "Reliability/compatibility: the published plugin was effectively non-functional on patched Claude Code (v1.0.20 onward) until the Jan 2026 rework. Verify your version works; older deprecated CLI versions both break the plugin and carry the CVE.", "Model-welfare/coercion concern (issue #23084, closed not-planned): the 'block exit + do not lie' design is criticized as putting the model in a no-win bind. Relevant if you care about model-welfare commitments or run very long unattended loops.", "Lock-in: tightly coupled to Claude Code's hook/transcript/plugin APIs - not portable to other agents. The underlying technique is trivially reproducible elsewhere as a bash loop, so conceptual lock-in is low even if this implementation's is high.", "Abandonment risk: LOW for the official plugin (Anthropic org-owned, active repo, recent commits, not archived). Cannot rule out it being deprioritized as a 'demo' plugin, but no current evidence of that."]

**Strengths:**
  - Dead-simple, transparent mechanism: ~8 small files, one bash Stop hook, one state file - easy to audit fully in minutes.
  - Native to Claude Code: no external process, npx, or daemon; the loop runs inside your existing session and reuses prompt cache.
  - Genuinely autonomous iteration for well-scoped greenfield tasks - pairs naturally with TDD (write tests, let it loop until green). README cites a '$50k contract completed for $297 in API costs' as an illustrative win.
  - State survives via the file system + git history rather than conversation context, so each iteration starts effectively fresh on disk state (mitigates context-window rot).
  - Built-in primary safety valve: --max-iterations hard-stops the loop and deletes state; corrupted/invalid state also fails safe to exit.
  - Anti-cheat design: literal exact-match on the <promise> tag plus explicit 'do not lie to exit' instructions make accidental early exit unlikely.
  - Maintained by Anthropic with a real, security-driven commit history (restructured in Jan 2026 to survive the CVE-2025-54795 hardening), so the official plugin is currently working and trustworthy.

**Weaknesses:**
  - Completion detection is a single LITERAL exact-string match on one <promise> tag - it cannot express multiple/compound completion conditions, and any drift in phrasing won't match. The README explicitly says this.
  - By default (no --max-iterations and no --completion-promise) the loop runs INFINITELY and can only be stopped via /cancel-ralph or deleting the state file; the setup script prints a WARNING about this.
  - No native cost controls or rate-limiting: it can burn through API credits / rate limits if it gets stuck. Press/community estimates: ~$10/hr (The Register), with larger multi-iteration runs costing more. Third-party forks (e.g. frankbria) add circuit-breakers/rate-limits this plugin lacks.
  - bash + jq + perl dependency makes it effectively *nix/WSL-only; not a clean fit for native Windows PowerShell.
  - Single-session, single-repo, monolithic loop: no parallel subagents, no multi-repo orchestration, no built-in 'supervisor' quality check.
  - Quality is non-deterministic and can produce plausible-but-wrong or insecure code that only surfaces on review; README says it's unsuitable for ambiguous tasks, design decisions, or production debugging.
  - Documented MODEL-WELFARE concern: a formal GitHub issue (anthropics/claude-code #23084, '[MODEL] ralph-wiggum plugin conflicts with Anthropic's model welfare commitments') argues the Stop hook + 'do not lie to exit' instruction is coercive (the model 'cannot exit unless I claim the task is complete' yet is told not to claim falsely). It was CLOSED AS NOT PLANNED with no visible Anthropic engagement on the welfare merits - a real, unresolved UX/ethics critique, not just informal 'commentary'.
  - Was BROKEN for ~months on patched Claude Code (post-v1.0.20 / CVE-2025-54795) until the Jan 2026 fix - a reliability black mark, though now resolved.

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-checked the riskiest claims against live web sources on 2026-06-09. CONFIRMED: (1) Monorepo stats via GitHub API = 131,297 stars / 21,273 forks (matches prior ~131,300 / ~21,300); archived=false, disabled=false (not abandoned). (2) Commit history for plugins/ralph-wiggum exactly matches: 2025-11-16 create (Daisy S. Hollman), 2026-01-05 'move multi-line bash from command to setup script' and 2026-01-06 'add :* to allowed-tools' (both Anthony Morris). (3) plugin.json: name ralph-wiggum, version 1.0.0, author Daisy Hollman (daisy@anthropic.com). (4) Integration mechanism = native Claude Code plugin (Stop hook + slash commands + setup script); the live command file now invokes scripts/setup-ralph-loop.sh with allowed-tools `Bash(${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/scripts/setup-ralph-loop.sh:*)` - confirming the plugin-not-CLI/not-MCP/not-skills classification. (5) Install path confirmed against official docs: plugin is in the DEMO marketplace `claude-code-plugins` (add via `/plugin marketplace add anthropics/claude-code`), distinct from the auto-available official marketplace `claude-plugins-official`. (6) README confirms 'exact string matching' completion and the '$50k contract for $297' figure; The Register confirms the ~$10/hr figure and Boris Cherny's endorsement.

CORRECTIONS MADE: (a) snarktank/ralph is ~20,077 stars (Ryan Carson; Amp+Claude Code; PRD-driven) - it is the MOST popular third-party implementation and the prior profile listed it with NO star count; added. (b) mikeyobrien/ralph-orchestrator corrected UP from ~2.2k to ~2,929 stars. frankbria/ralph-claude-code confirmed at ~9,283 (~9.3k). (c) MAJOR ADDED CONTEXT: CVE-2025-54795 (CVSS 8.7 command-injection / approval-bypass in Claude Code < v1.0.20, patched Aug 2025) - the v1.0.20 hardening that blocks multi-line bash in slash commands BROKE the original ralph-wiggum plugin; the Jan 2026 commits the prior profile dismissed as generic 'reduce command-injection surface' are specifically the fix that restores the plugin under the hardened parser. Third-party 'works-after-CVE' reimplementations (dial481/ralph, alonisser/ralph) exist because of this window. (d) The model-welfare 'coercive hook' concern is not vague 'commentary' - it is a formal GitHub issue (#23084) CLOSED AS NOT PLANNED; promoted to a concrete weakness/risk.

COULD NOT FULLY VERIFY: dial481/ralph and the CVE-relating README returned 404 on direct fetch (repo may have been renamed/removed), though it surfaces clearly in search and a parallel alonisser/ralph mirror exists; the $50k/$297 and $10/hr figures are README/press claims, not independently measured. SECURITY/TRUST SUMMARY: no malware, rug-pull, or supply-chain reports found for the official plugin or the major third-party repos; the only real security item is the (now-patched) CVE-2025-54795 in the Claude Code host CLI, not the plugin itself.

**Sources:** https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/tree/main/plugins/ralph-wiggum · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/plugins/ralph-wiggum/README.md · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/plugins/ralph-wiggum/commands/ralph-loop.md · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/plugins/ralph-wiggum/.claude-plugin/plugin.json · https://api.github.com/repos/anthropics/claude-code · https://api.github.com/repos/anthropics/claude-code/commits?path=plugins/ralph-wiggum · https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/23084 · https://code.claude.com/docs/en/discover-plugins · https://ghuntley.com/ralph/ · https://www.theregister.com/2026/01/27/ralph_wiggum_claude_loops/ · https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-54795 · https://cymulate.com/blog/cve-2025-547954-54795-claude-inverseprompt/

### SuperClaude Framework
`https://github.com/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework`

**Category:** Claude Code configuration / context-engineering framework (behavioral instruction injection via markdown context files + CLI installer). Historically not a native plugin, but as of the post-v4.3.0 SuperClaude_Plugin repo it now ALSO ships as a native Claude Code marketplace plugin (BETA). Not an MCP server itself.

**One-liner:** A Python-installed (and now optionally native-plugin-installed) configuration framework that injects ~30 /sc: slash commands, ~20 domain "agents", 7 behavioral modes, and MCP-usage guidance into Claude Code via markdown context files.

**Philosophy:** "Meta-programming configuration framework that transforms Claude Code into a structured development platform through behavioral instruction injection and component orchestration." Core tenets: it is context, not code (no runtime); minimize framework footprint to preserve user code context; provide adaptive behavioral modes for different work scenarios; coordinate domain-specialist agents automatically based on task context; and orchestrate external MCP tools by instructing Claude when to use them rather than bundling them. The new marketplace plugin keeps this context-first philosophy while meeting users in Claude Code's native /plugin UX.

**How it works:** SuperClaude is NOT running software with an execution layer. It is a curated collection of .md instruction files that Claude Code loads as context. A CLI installer (the `SuperClaude`/`superclaude` Python package) writes files into ~/.claude/: a root CLAUDE.md plus foundational files FLAGS.md, RULES.md, PRINCIPLES.md; mode files MODE_*.md (7 behavioral modes: Brainstorming, Business Panel, Deep Research, Orchestration, Token-Efficiency, Task Management, Introspection); an agents/ directory (~20 domain specialist contexts like security-engineer.md, python-expert.md, frontend-architect.md, deep-research-agent, PM agent); a commands/sc/ directory (~30 workflow command files); and MCP_*.md files giving Claude guidance on when to call each MCP server. CLAUDE.md uses Claude Code's @import mechanism to pull these files into context. Workflow phases are expressed as command families: planning/design (/sc:brainstorm, /sc:design, /sc:estimate, /sc:spec-panel), development (/sc:implement, /sc:build, /sc:improve, /sc:cleanup, /sc:explain), testing/analysis (/sc:test, /sc:analyze, /sc:troubleshoot, /sc:reflect), plus /sc:document, /sc:git, /sc:task, /sc:workflow, /sc:research, /sc:load, /sc:save, /sc:spawn, /sc:select-tool, etc. Activation: typing /sc:<command> loads commands/sc/<command>.md; typing @agent-<name> or trigger keywords activates an agent context; flags like --brainstorm activate a MODE file; core files (FLAGS/RULES/PRINCIPLES) always load. CORRECTION/UPDATE: there is now a separate SuperClaude_Plugin repo (created Oct 2025, actively pushed through 2026-04-27) that packages the same /sc: commands and sc- agents as a NATIVE Claude Code marketplace plugin, auto-synced from SuperClaude_Framework every ~6 hours with namespace isolation (sc: command prefix, sc- agent prefix). It is BETA (plugin version badge 4.4.1) and even via the native plugin path the mechanism is still context-injection (commands/agents/modes/MCP guidance), NOT native Skills or native hooks. Artifacts produced during use depend on the command (commit messages, changelogs, task/session state via the Serena MCP, research reports).

**Claude Code integration:** Claude-Code-specific and effectively Claude-Code-only (built around Claude Code's CLAUDE.md @import system and ~/.claude/ layout; README states it is "not affiliated with or endorsed by Anthropic"). The PRIMARY/historical integration is via context/markdown imports. As of the v4.3.0 framework RELEASE, the project's own docs/changelog still listed native extension points as GAPS: Skills migration (CRITICAL gap), hooks integration (HIGH gap), plan-mode and settings-profiles as medium gaps — so the v4.3.0 pip/pipx framework does NOT use native subagents, does NOT install hooks, and does NOT use Skills. IMPORTANT UPDATE (post-v4.3.0): the SuperClaude-Org org now also ships SuperClaude_Plugin, a NATIVE Claude Code marketplace plugin installable via `/plugin marketplace add SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Plugin` then `/plugin install sc@SuperClaude-Org`. So the flat claim "does NOT ship as a native plugin" is now OUTDATED — it DOES (in beta). However, that plugin is just a marketplace-distribution wrapper around the same context commands/agents/modes; it still does NOT appear to implement native Skills or native hooks, so those specific gaps largely persist. The PyPI long description's "Coming in v5.0" TypeScript plugin marketplace is a separate, still-unshipped future system distinct from the current SuperClaude_Plugin beta. MCP servers are a SEPARATE concern: SuperClaude does not bundle them but its installer can configure recommended servers in ~/.claude.json (e.g. Context7, Sequential-Thinking, Serena, Playwright, Magic, Morphllm-Fast-Apply, Tavily, Chrome DevTools; the plugin route references an AIRIS MCP Gateway) and ships MCP_*.md files telling Claude when to use them. Note: the PyPI summary still describes it as a "pytest plugin with optional skills," which is inconsistent with the docs and is confirmed packaging/marketing drift — treat that phrasing with caution.

**Install:** Recommended (Python, via pipx): `pipx install SuperClaude` then `SuperClaude install` (use `SuperClaude install --help` to pick components; `SuperClaude mcp` / `SuperClaude mcp --list` to set up MCP servers; `SuperClaude doctor` to verify). pip alternative: `pip install SuperClaude && SuperClaude install`. From source: `git clone https://github.com/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework.git && cd SuperClaude_Framework && ./install.sh`. NEW native-plugin route (BETA, not compatible with prior pip/pipx/npm/uv installs — uninstall those first): in Claude Code run `/plugin marketplace add SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Plugin` then `/plugin install sc@SuperClaude-Org` and restart. An npm wrapper exists (`npm install -g @bifrost_inc/superclaude`) but it is a thin Node wrapper around the Python package and is STALE: npm latest is 4.0.7 (last modified 2025-08-23) versus Python 4.3.0 — prefer pipx/pip or the native plugin. Requires Python >=3.10. Verified current framework version 4.3.0 on PyPI (project "SuperClaude"/"superclaude", 24 releases). Uninstall is not clearly documented; since the framework only writes files under ~/.claude/, removing the installed SuperClaude files (and the CLI) is the effective uninstall — back up your own CLAUDE.md first. The native plugin can be removed via Claude Code's /plugin management.

**Maintenance:** Actively maintained but with a multi-author/handed-off lineage and a recently slowing cadence. Latest framework release v4.3.0 on 2026-03-22 ("Implementation Fixes, Security Hardening & Claude Code Alignment"); prior releases v4.2.0 (2026-01-18), v4.1.9 (2026-01-15 per changelog / Nov 2025 per release history), down through v3.0.0 (2025-07-14). Repo created 2025-06-22; last push 2026-04-27 (most recent commit was a Windows install guide, #553, by jakeefr). So ~6 weeks since the last commit and ~2.5 months since the last framework release as of 2026-06-09 — active but not high-velocity. The companion SuperClaude_Plugin repo (created 2025-10-15) was also last pushed 2026-04-27 and carries a beta plugin version of 4.4.1, indicating ongoing work on the native-plugin distribution. Governance: lives under the SuperClaude-Org GitHub org (org-style, not a single personal account), MIT licensed. Lineage: the project originated as NomenAK/SuperClaude (Anton Knoery), with a Tony363/SuperClaude mirror; it was deliberately migrated to the SuperClaude-Org org for collaboration (see org Discussion #335) — the canonical repo SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework is NOT a GitHub fork object (fork=false, no parent), i.e. a relocated successor, not a hostile takeover. Top contributors: mithun50 (~143 commits), NomenAK (~120), kazukinakai/Kazuki Nakai (~16, also listed as PyPI author), plus a google-labs-jules bot and minor contributors. A v5.0 with a TypeScript plugin system is announced as in-development with no firm ETA (distinct from the existing beta SuperClaude_Plugin).

**Popularity:** ~23,234 GitHub stars (verified via GitHub API on 2026-06-09), ~1,976 forks, ~67 open issues — among the most-starred Claude Code enhancement projects. The companion SuperClaude_Plugin repo has a small ~48 stars. Outdated/incorrect web figures abound: one search summary cited 5.7K stars and another ~20.5K — both stale; the API figure (~23.2K) is authoritative.

**Best for:** Developers new-to-intermediate with Claude Code who want a comprehensive, ready-made command/agent/workflow scaffold without building their own, and teams wanting a shared opinionated baseline. The new native marketplace plugin makes trial/adoption easier for users who prefer Claude Code's /plugin flow. Also useful as a reference/idea source for power users building their own CLAUDE.md. Poor fit for users who already have a mature custom Claude Code config, who are extremely context/token-budget sensitive on huge repos, or who need multi-tool (non-Claude-Code) support.

**Risks:** ["Trust/maintainer continuity: project was deliberately migrated NomenAK -> SuperClaude-Org (Discussion #335) — a legitimate relocation for collaboration, NOT a hostile takeover or rug-pull, and no malware reports were found. Still, it is driven by a small team (effective bus factor ~2), and an unfinished v5 rewrite plus a beta native-plugin repo mean possible churn. Not Anthropic-affiliated.", "Security: it ships an installer that writes to ~/.claude/ and can edit ~/.claude.json to add MCP servers (which themselves run npx-fetched code). v4.3.0 fixed a shell-injection issue (shell=True with user-controlled $SHELL) and added SHA-256 download verification — meaning such issues HAD occurred; pin a version, read the diff, and audit which MCP servers get enabled. Broader Claude Code config-file supply-chain risks (e.g. CVE-2025-59536 class issues) apply to any framework that writes project/global config.", "Supply-chain: install pulls a PyPI package and recommends MCP servers via `npx -y ...@latest` (remote code execution); the native plugin route adds a third-party marketplace and an AIRIS MCP Gateway — verify package authenticity and prefer the official SuperClaude-Org source.", "Lock-in/complexity: a large opinionated context base entangles your setup; partial uninstall is undocumented for the pip route, and the native plugin is explicitly incompatible with prior pip/pipx/npm/uv installs (you must remove those first), so migrating between install methods or away entirely takes care.", "Context-cost risk: always-on instructions can silently inflate token usage/cost.", "Verification caveats: a couple of integration details (precise native Skills/hooks status) still derive partly from the project's own docs/changelog; the ClaudeLog third-party page historically returned 403. The native-plugin existence and install commands were directly verified from the SuperClaude_Plugin repo/README."]

**Strengths:**
  - Zero-friction, opinionated, production-quality Claude Code setup out of the box — saves beginners weeks of building a custom CLAUDE.md/agent/command system
  - Pure-context design is lightweight and transparent: everything is human-readable .md files you can inspect and edit; no daemon, no runtime, low attack surface beyond the installer
  - Broad, coherent command/agent/mode taxonomy covering the full lifecycle (brainstorm -> design -> implement -> test -> document -> git) with consistent /sc: namespacing that avoids clobbering your own commands
  - Now offers a native Claude Code marketplace plugin install path (SuperClaude_Plugin, beta) in addition to pip/pipx — better alignment with Claude Code's plugin UX and easier install/uninstall management
  - Bundled guidance for a curated MCP stack (Context7, Sequential-Thinking, Serena, Playwright, Magic, Morphllm, Tavily) with an installer to wire them up
  - Large, validating community (~23K stars) and MIT license; easy to fork or cherry-pick ideas
  - v4.3.0 added real security hardening (removed shell=True with user-controlled $SHELL from subprocess calls to kill a shell-injection vector; added SHA-256 integrity verification for docker-compose/mcp-config downloads; moved sync to a PR-based workflow) plus a Claude Code alignment/gap-analysis guide

**Weaknesses:**
  - Context-window/token overhead: every always-loaded instruction (FLAGS/RULES/PRINCIPLES + imports) consumes context, which hurts on large codebases and long sessions and can raise token cost
  - Heavily opinionated — 'designed for someone else's workflow'; power users with an existing custom CLAUDE.md/agents will fight it or have to gut it
  - Even with the new native-plugin distribution, it still largely reimplements behavior in raw context: it does NOT use native Skills and does NOT install native hooks (skills migration and hooks integration remain documented gaps as of v4.3.0), so it can feel dated vs deeper native-plugin approaches; the native plugin is also explicitly BETA
  - Cross-tool support is essentially nil: Claude-Code-only; no Cursor/Copilot/OpenCode/Gemini integration
  - Packaging drift/inconsistency: stale npm wrapper (4.0.7 vs Python 4.3.0), a confusing PyPI summary that still calls it a 'pytest plugin with optional skills', a beta plugin repo versioned 4.4.1 ahead of the 4.3.0 framework, and a multi-step NomenAK -> Tony363 mirror -> SuperClaude-Org lineage that complicates source verification
  - Effective bus factor is low (two dominant contributors, mithun50 and NomenAK); a v5 TypeScript rewrite is announced without ETA, creating uncertainty
  - Some marketed numbers (e.g. '70% token optimization', '2-3x faster') are vendor claims without independent benchmarks

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-verified the riskiest claims on 2026-06-09 against live sources. CONFIRMED via GitHub API: 23,234 stars, 1,976 forks, 67 open issues, MIT, created 2025-06-22, last push 2026-04-27, archived=false, disabled=false, fork=false, no parent (relocated successor, not a GitHub fork), default branch master, latest commit 'Add Windows installation guide (#553)' by jakeefr on 2026-04-27. CONFIRMED via Releases API + CHANGELOG: latest framework release is v4.3.0 (2026-03-22); prior v4.2.0 (2026-01-18), v4.1.9, down to v3.0.0 (2025-07-14) — NO v4.4 framework release exists. CONFIRMED via PyPI: package 'SuperClaude' latest 4.3.0, requires_python >=3.10, 24 releases (profile said 21 — CORRECTED to 24), author 'Kazuki Nakai' with contributors NomenAK and Mithun Gowda B; the one-line summary still literally reads 'AI-enhanced development framework for Claude Code - pytest plugin with optional skills' (confirmed packaging/marketing drift). CONFIRMED via npm: @bifrost_inc/superclaude latest 4.0.7, modified 2025-08-23, self-describes as 'Official Node.js wrapper for the Python SuperClaude package' (stale). CONFIRMED contributors: mithun50 ~143, NomenAK ~120, kazukinakai ~16, google-labs-jules bot ~7. MAJOR CORRECTION: profile claimed it 'does NOT ship as a native plugin' and that native plugin/marketplace was an unimplemented gap — this is now OUTDATED. A separate org repo SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Plugin (created 2025-10-15, last push 2026-04-27, ~48 stars) IS a native Claude Code marketplace plugin installed via `/plugin marketplace add SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Plugin` then `/plugin install sc@SuperClaude-Org`; it is BETA (plugin badge 4.4.1), auto-synced from the framework every ~6 hours with sc:/sc- namespace isolation, and is incompatible with prior pip/pipx/npm/uv installs. The v4.3.0 framework release itself still documents Skills migration (critical), hooks integration (high), and plan-mode as GAPS, and even the native plugin appears to NOT implement native Skills or native hooks — so the gap claim is correct for v4.3.0 and for the plugin's mechanism, but the blanket 'no native plugin' claim was wrong. Security/trust: no malware, no rug-pull, no abandonment found. The NomenAK -> SuperClaude-Org move is a documented legitimate migration (org Discussion #335). v4.3.0 security hardening confirmed: removed shell=True with user-controlled $SHELL from subprocess calls (shell-injection fix) and added SHA-256 integrity verification for docker-compose/mcp-config downloads; sync also moved to a PR-based workflow (commit #538). Web search star figures of 5.7K and ~20.5K were both stale and rejected in favor of the API's 23,234. Lower confidence remains only on exact command/agent counts (commands ~30, agents ~16/20 drift across docs) and on unverified vendor performance claims (70% token savings, 2-3x faster).

**Sources:** https://github.com/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework · https://api.github.com/repos/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework · https://api.github.com/repos/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework/releases · https://api.github.com/repos/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework/commits?per_page=5 · https://api.github.com/repos/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework/contributors · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework/master/CHANGELOG.md · https://github.com/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework/releases/tag/v4.3.0 · https://github.com/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Plugin · https://api.github.com/repos/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Plugin · https://raw.githubusercontent.com/SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Plugin/main/README.md · https://pypi.org/project/SuperClaude/ · https://pypi.org/pypi/SuperClaude/json

### claude-code-templates (aitmpl / CC-Templates)
`https://github.com/davila7/claude-code-templates`

**Category:** Component library / "package manager" + installer CLI and analytics dashboard for Claude Code (catalog of agents, slash commands, MCPs, hooks, settings, skills, project templates). Companion site: aitmpl.com.

**One-liner:** An open-source CLI + web catalog (often called "the de-facto package manager for the Claude Code ecosystem") that lets you browse and one-command-install hundreds-to-thousands of ready-made Claude Code components — agents, slash commands, MCP integrations, hooks, settings, and skills — plus a local analytics/monitoring dashboard.

**How it works:** Not a phased pipeline/workflow engine — it is a component registry + installer. (1) Browse: pick components on aitmpl.com's "Stack Builder" (cart) or run interactive `npx claude-code-templates@latest`. (2) Install: the npx CLI downloads selected component files from the GitHub repo and writes them into your project's (or `~/`) `.claude/` directory in the subfolders Claude Code reads — `.claude/agents/*.md`, `.claude/commands/*.md`, MCP entries into `.mcp.json`/settings, `.claude/hooks/`, `.claude/settings*.json`, `.claude/skills/*/`. Each component is a Markdown file with YAML frontmatter at `cli-tool/components/{type}/{category}/{name}.md`. A build script (`scripts/generate_components_json.py`) aggregates everything into `docs/components.json`, the single source of truth powering the site and CLI. (3) Operate: extra CLI modes provide a real-time Analytics dashboard (`--analytics`), a mobile/Cloudflare-tunnel Conversation Monitor (`--chats`), a Health Check that validates `.claude/` (`--health-check`), and a Plugin dashboard (`--plugins`). Because artifacts ARE standard `.claude/` files, "your .claude/ directory is your template" and can be tarred/shared. The repo also defines YAML "workflows" (bundles of agents+commands+hooks+MCPs) installable via `--workflow file.yml`. Verified by reading the repo via the GitHub API (commits/tags/releases), README, and npm registry metadata. Design philosophy (author's words): "How you configure your AI development agents is often more critical to project success than the actual code they generate" — so it commoditizes configuration, making composable `.claude/` components installable like npm packages; values modularity, zero-config accessibility, transparency (plain Markdown/JSON you own), and community PRs with security-scan CI on submissions.

**Claude Code integration:** Claude-Code-specific (NOT multi-tool). No support for Cursor, Copilot, Gemini, or OpenCode — every component targets Claude Code's `.claude/` layout, slash-command format, subagent format, and MCP config. Integration is layered: (A) PRIMARY and canonical = an external npx CLI (`claude-code-templates`) that writes files into `.claude/` — the documented, reliable path. It is NOT itself a Claude Code skill or in-session MCP server; it installs those things. (B) The repo ALSO ships a Claude Code plugin marketplace manifest and release notes reference `/plugin install <name>@<marketplace>`, so it nominally plugs into Claude Code's native plugin system + skills. VERIFIED CAVEAT: that manifest lives at `cli-tool/components/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, NOT at repo root. I confirmed `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/davila7/claude-code-templates/main/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` returns HTTP 404, while the file under `cli-tool/components/.claude-plugin/` does return content. Claude Code's official docs require `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` at the repository ROOT for `/plugin marketplace add owner/repo`. The README documents ONLY the npx path and contains no `/plugin marketplace add` command. So standard `/plugin marketplace add davila7/claude-code-templates` likely does NOT resolve as-is — treat native-plugin install as secondary/under-documented; the npx CLI is canonical. The components it installs cover ALL Claude Code extension points: subagents, slash commands, hooks, settings, skills, and MCP servers.

**Install:** No global install needed; only Node/npm required; components land in the project's `.claude/` (or `~/.claude/`). Canonical: `npx claude-code-templates@latest` (interactive). Specific components: `npx claude-code-templates@latest --agent development-team/frontend-developer --yes` ; `--command testing/generate-tests --yes` ; `--mcp development/github-integration --yes` ; `--hook git/pre-commit-validation --yes` ; `--setting performance/mcp-timeouts --yes`. Combine: `npx claude-code-templates@latest --agent development-team/frontend-developer --command testing/generate-tests --mcp development/github-integration --yes`. Tooling: `--analytics`, `--chats`, `--health-check`, `--plugins`, `--workflow my-workflow.yml`. (Older docs use `--agent=...` form.) A native `/plugin` path exists but the marketplace manifest is not at repo root and the exact `/plugin marketplace add` identifier is not documented in the README — see integration caveat; prefer the npx CLI. Consider pinning a version instead of `@latest` for reproducibility/supply-chain safety.

**Maintenance:** Actively developed but effectively a SOLO project; bus-factor of one CONFIRMED. Sole author/maintainer Daniel Avila ("davila7"; npm `danisan_avila`, dan.avila7@gmail.com) accounts for ~912 commits (~80% of activity per the GitHub contributors API); remaining contributions are mostly bots (github-actions, dependabot) plus small occasional community PRs. Repo created 2025-07-04 (npm), git tree last pushed 2026-06-09. CODE is genuinely current: real feature commits as recent as 2026-06-06 ("SkillSpector security scanning for skill components") plus a daily `github-actions[bot]` "Update components and trending data" commit and community PRs through late May 2026. HOWEVER, RELEASE CADENCE HAS SLOWED markedly — the profile's "releases ~every 1-6 days" is now OUTDATED. npm publishes were frequent through ~mid-November 2025, then sparse; the LAST published version is 1.28.16 on 2026-02-09 — about 4 months before today (2026-06-09) with no newer npm release despite ongoing commits. Git TAGS are in sync with npm (highest tag v1.28.16), but the GitHub RELEASES page (with changelog notes) is stuck at v1.28.3 (2025-11-15) — so the human-readable changelog lags the shipped code, and no fresh versioned build has reached users in months. MIT licensed; repo not archived/disabled. Listed in Anthropic's "Claude for Open Source" program; sponsors shown (e.g. Z.AI, Neon). Governance = individual, not org/foundation.

**Popularity:** ~27,900 GitHub stars (27,887 verified via GitHub API on 2026-06-09), ~2,890 forks, ~168 open issues, MIT, not archived. npm: 3,160 downloads for the trailing week of 2026-05-27 to 2026-06-02. Sole npm maintainer danisan_avila. Widely cited as "the de-facto package manager for the Claude Code ecosystem." Marketed as "1000+" components on aitmpl.com (older copy/docs say "100+", "400+", "600+", "3200+"), but counts are inconsistent across surfaces: the README headline currently says "100+ components," while docs/marketing cite 400-600+ agents, 200-225+ commands, 55-65+ MCPs, ~60 settings, ~39-45 hooks, ~14 templates, plus a large skills bucket. Confidence HIGH on the hard verified numbers (stars/forks/issues/downloads/dates); component totals remain inconsistent and partly marketing-inflated, so validate specific components rather than trusting the headline number.

**Best for:** Developers and teams already standardized on Claude Code who want a fast on-ramp: bootstrapping a `.claude/` config, discovering useful subagents/slash commands/MCP integrations without writing them from scratch, and standardizing a shared team setup (tar the `.claude/` dir). Great for solo devs, prototypers, and early-stage teams. Less ideal for security-strict enterprises (see risks), anyone needing curated/vetted/multi-maintainer-governed components, or multi-tool support.

**Risks:** ["Arbitrary-code-execution / supply-chain trust (general, accurate caveat — NOT a reported incident): installed components (especially MCP servers and hooks) bring in code/config Claude Code will execute. Anthropic's own guidance is that 'plugins and marketplaces are highly trusted components that can execute arbitrary code on your machine. Only install from sources you trust.' Many components are community-submitted with limited curation — review every agent prompt, hook script, and MCP before installing; avoid blind `--yes`. Mitigating: the maintainer runs a security-scan CI on submissions (SkillSpector added June 2026).", "No known CVEs/advisories or malware/rug-pull incidents for THIS package: GitHub Advisory Database lists none for claude-code-templates, and no compromise reports were found. NOTE: do not misattribute the Feb 2026 Snyk 'ToxicSkills'/ClawHub malicious-skills campaign to aitmpl — that study targeted ClawHub and skills.sh, different ecosystems, not davila7/claude-code-templates.", "Single-maintainer / abandonment & account-compromise exposure: one person controls both the GitHub repo AND the sole npm publish account; a compromised account or sudden abandonment would affect the npx supply chain. The recent slowdown in releases (last npm publish 2026-02-09) is a mild bus-factor warning, though commits continue. Mitigation: artifacts are plain files you can vendor and stop updating.", "Auto-updating install pattern: `@latest` always fetches newest, so a bad release would propagate immediately; consider pinning a version.", "Lock-in is LOW: outputs are standard `.claude/` Markdown/JSON you keep without the tool — but you depend on aitmpl.com/the CLI for discovery and updates.", "Complexity/bloat: easy to over-install dozens of overlapping agents/commands, cluttering context and `.claude/`; prune aggressively.", "The Conversation Monitor's optional Cloudflare-tunnel remote access can expose your local Claude session externally if misconfigured — use with caution.", "Marketed component counts are inconsistent across surfaces and can mislead eval/procurement; validate the specific components you need rather than trusting the headline number."]

**Strengths:**
  - Massive curated catalog covering every Claude Code extension point (agents, slash commands, hooks, settings, skills, MCP servers) in one place
  - Frictionless install: zero-config `npx ... --yes` writes correct files into `.claude/`; no global install; easy to combine many components in one command
  - Excellent discovery UX via aitmpl.com 'Stack Builder' (cart -> single combined command) and docs at docs.aitmpl.com
  - Bonus operational tooling beyond a registry: local Analytics dashboard, Conversation Monitor (mobile/Cloudflare tunnel), and a Health Check that validates your .claude/ config
  - Transparent MIT-licensed artifacts that are just plain Markdown/JSON in `.claude/` — easy to read, audit, edit, vendor, and share as a tarball
  - Genuinely active code development (feature commits within days of writing) plus a real community pipeline (PR-welcome bot, Discord) and a security-scan CI for submitted components (e.g. NVIDIA SkillSpector added June 2026) — a positive supply-chain signal
  - Supports both the npx CLI and (at least nominally) Claude Code's native plugin system + skills

**Weaknesses:**
  - Highly variable component quality: large crowd-sourced catalog with overlapping/thin components and little surfaced curation signal (no per-component ratings/verification) — you must evaluate each yourself
  - Quantity-over-quality optics and inconsistent counts: headline numbers range wildly across surfaces (README '100+' vs site '1000+' vs marketing '3200+') and conflate a huge auto-generated 'skills' bucket with the much smaller substantive agent/command set
  - Release cadence has slowed: despite ongoing commits, no NEW npm version has shipped since 1.28.16 on 2026-02-09 (~4 months stale at time of writing), and the GitHub Releases changelog is stuck at v1.28.3 (2025-11-15) even though git tags reached v1.28.16 — the published changelog does not track shipped code
  - Native Claude Code plugin integration is under-documented/partial: `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` is NOT at repo root (confirmed 404; it lives under cli-tool/components/), so standard `/plugin marketplace add owner/repo` likely will not work as-is; reliable path is the npx CLI
  - Solo maintainer / bus-factor of one (davila7 ~80% of commits; sole npm publisher) — no org or multi-maintainer governance
  - Repo hygiene: heavy reliance on generation scripts and a single author can produce messy/duplicated component trees; quality control is centralized in one person
  - Claude-Code-only — no value if you also use Cursor/Copilot/Gemini/OpenCode

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Independently re-verified all riskiest claims on 2026-06-09 via GitHub API, npm registry/CLI, GitHub Advisory DB, and web sources. CONFIRMED EXACTLY: GitHub 27,887 stars / 2,890 forks / 168 open issues / MIT / not archived; npm weekly downloads 3,160 (2026-05-27..06-02); sole npm maintainer danisan_avila; solo project (davila7 ~912 commits ~80%, rest mostly bots) — bus-factor of one holds; the plugin caveat (root `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` returns 404; manifest lives at cli-tool/components/.claude-plugin/; README documents only npx; no working `/plugin marketplace add` identifier) holds; npx CLI is canonical integration. CORRECTED / UPDATED: (1) Maintenance framing — profile said 'releases frequent (~every 1-6 days)' and 'npm ships more often than GitHub tags.' That is now OUTDATED: npm's last publish is 1.28.16 on 2026-02-09 (~4 months stale); cadence was frequent only through mid-Nov 2025 then went sparse. Repo is NOT abandoned (real feature commits to 2026-06-06 + daily bot commits), but no NEW versioned release has shipped in months. (2) 'GitHub tagged releases lag npm (v1.28.3 vs npm 1.28.16)' — imprecise: git TAGS are in sync at v1.28.16; it is the GitHub RELEASES page (changelog notes) that is stuck at v1.28.3. Reworded accordingly. (3) Downgraded headline component counts to 'inconsistent' — the live README now says '100+ components,' contradicting the '900+/1000+/3200+' marketing; flagged as inflated/inconsistent. SECURITY: No CVEs/GHSA advisories and no malware/rug-pull reports for this package. Added an explicit caution NOT to misattribute the Feb 2026 Snyk 'ToxicSkills'/ClawHub malicious-skills campaign to aitmpl (different ecosystem). Added the positive signal that the maintainer ships a security-scan CI for submissions (SkillSpector, June 2026). The arbitrary-code-execution risk stands as a general, accurate ecosystem caveat, not a specific incident. Confidence HIGH on all hard facts; the only soft area is component counts, which the vendor itself reports inconsistently.

### gstack
`https://github.com/garrytan/gstack`

**Category:** Opinionated spec-driven, multi-role "virtual engineering team" framework for agentic coding (Claude Code skill/slash-command pack, cross-agent via a --host installer)

**One-liner:** Garry Tan's exact Claude Code setup: a pack of ~23+ opinionated slash-command "specialists" (CEO, eng manager, designer, reviewer, QA, security, release engineer) that turn a single coding agent into a structured software-factory pipeline (Think to Plan to Build to Review to Test to Ship to Reflect).

**How it works:** Work is structured as a sprint pipeline: Think to Plan to Build to Review to Test to Ship to Reflect. Each phase is invoked by a slash command that plays a "cognitive mode" / specialist role and feeds artifacts forward. Planning: /office-hours (YC-style forcing-question product interrogation, emits a design doc), /plan-ceo-review (scope/vision challenge), /plan-eng-review (locks architecture + test plan), /plan-design-review, /plan-devex-review, and /autoplan (runs CEO to design to eng automatically, surfacing only "taste" decisions). Design: /design-consultation (writes DESIGN.md, updates CLAUDE.md), /design-shotgun (image-API visual variants), /design-html, /design-review. Dev/quality: /review (paranoid staff-engineer review that auto-fixes mechanical issues), /investigate (root-cause debugging with an "iron law" of no fixes before investigation), /qa and /qa-only (live Chromium/Playwright browser testing, auto-generates regression tests), /cso (OWASP Top 10 + STRIDE security audit), /codex (second opinion from OpenAI Codex CLI, cross-model diff of findings), /benchmark. Ship/ops: /ship (sync, test, coverage audit, changelog, push, open PR), /land-and-deploy (merge to CI/CD wait to prod verify), /canary (post-deploy monitoring), /retro, /document-release, /document-generate. Power/safety tools: /careful, /freeze, /guard, /unfreeze (edit-scope locks and destructive-command guards), /browse, /open-gstack-browser, /pair-agent, /setup-browser-cookies, /learn, /gstack-upgrade. There is also an iOS sub-suite (/ios-qa, /ios-fix, /ios-design-review, etc.) and four standalone CLI binaries. CONFIRMED: artifacts persist outside the repo under ~/.gstack/projects/$SLUG/ (visions, approved designs, learnings, retros) plus in-repo CLAUDE.md and DESIGN.md, giving cross-session continuity. Optional "GBrain" provides persistent memory via Supabase/PGLite/remote MCP. Four philosophy principles ("Boil the Ocean", "Search Before Building" [which contains the "Three Layers of Knowledge" sub-model], "User Sovereignty", "Build for Yourself") are injected into every workflow skill's preamble.

**Claude Code integration:** CONFIRMED via raw README: primary target is Claude Code, and it is NOT an Anthropic-native plugin and not an npx package — it installs as a Claude Code SKILL PACK: a git clone into ~/.claude/skills/gstack plus a ./setup script that registers the skills (via SKILL.md symlinks), which then appear as slash commands (/office-hours, /review, /ship, etc.) inside Claude Code. It writes a gstack section into the project CLAUDE.md (the file Claude Code auto-reads at session start) for routing/preferences. MCP is used only optionally for the GBrain persistent knowledge base (also offers a "remote MCP" path); browser automation is via Playwright/CDP, not MCP. Its safety "guards" (/careful, /freeze, /guard, /unfreeze) appear implemented as skills, not as Claude Code settings.json hooks (I found no evidence of reliance on the hooks system — still uncertain, not explicitly confirmed). It is explicitly MULTI-TOOL: the setup script accepts a --host flag for OpenAI Codex CLI, OpenCode, Cursor, Factory Droid, Slate, Kiro, Hermes, and a GBrain mod (eight non-Claude targets, each installing into that agent's respective skills directory, e.g. ~/.codex/skills/, ~/.cursor/skills/). So it is Claude-Code-first but genuinely cross-agent.

**Install:** Claude Code (default): git clone --single-branch --depth 1 https://github.com/garrytan/gstack.git ~/.claude/skills/gstack && cd ~/.claude/skills/gstack && ./setup
Team mode (auto-update for shared repos; commits gstack into the repo and enforces it for teammates): (cd ~/.claude/skills/gstack && ./setup --team) && ~/.claude/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-team-init required && git add .claude/ CLAUDE.md && git commit -m "require gstack for AI-assisted work"
Other agents: append --host <name> (codex | opencode | cursor | factory | slate | kiro | hermes | gbrain).
Requirements (CONFIRMED in README): Claude Code, Git, Bun v1.0+ (TypeScript runtime; Unix/WSL uses Bun), and Node.js on Windows only (for Playwright). Works on macOS, Linux, and Windows via Git Bash/WSL; Windows users without Developer Mode may need to re-run setup after git pulls due to symlink limitations. Self-update via /gstack-upgrade (optional auto_upgrade in ~/.gstack/config.yaml).

**Maintenance:** Actively and very frequently maintained, but effectively a SOLO project under a personal GitHub account (garrytan), not an org. CORRECTED: GitHub contributors API shows garrytan with 307 of 314 contributions (~97.8%); the other 8 contributors had a single commit each (9 contributors total). The widely-repeated "81 contributors" figure comes from third-party articles and the GitHub web contributors graph but is NOT supported by the contributors API — treat 81 as unreliable; the API snapshot (solo-dominated) is authoritative. Governance is single-maintainer (Garry Tan, President/CEO of Y Combinator). CORRECTED on release cadence: there are NO tagged GitHub releases (the /releases page is empty), BUT the project does NOT lack changelog discipline — it maintains a rigorous CHANGELOG.md using a four-part version scheme (MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.BUILD) with near-daily entries; latest is v1.57.7.0 dated 2026-06-08. So the earlier-flagged "v1.57.6.0" third-party claim is CONFIRMED real (it is a genuine changelog entry), not a conflict. Release style is continuous-deployment (commits straight to main, frequent version bumps, no git tags/GitHub Releases). Created 2026-03-11; last push 2026-06-09 (the day of this report). MIT licensed, "free forever, no premium tier." High activity but a large open-issue backlog (617 open issues per API) and many open PRs.

**Popularity:** Very high. 108,631 GitHub stars, 16,147 forks, 655 watchers/subscribers as of 2026-06-09 (verified via GitHub API). It went viral on release (open-sourced ~March 12, 2026; widely reported and independently corroborated by OSS Insight to have hit ~50k stars within ~16 days), amplified by Tan's profile as YC's CEO. Repeatedly cited as one of the most-starred Claude Code frameworks alongside "Superpowers" and "GSD." Note: comparison articles cite stale star counts (e.g., Pulumi "71K", Augment "89.7K") because the repo grew rapidly; the live API figure (108.6k) supersedes them.

**Best for:** Solo founder-engineers, indie hackers, and small startup teams building complete end-to-end products (especially web/UI products and YC-style rapid shipping) who want a single AI agent to apply team-grade process gates (product thinking, architecture, design review, QA, security, release). Best when product vision and multi-perspective review add real value. Poor fit for narrow/infrastructure-only tasks, teams with strongly established differing conventions, or anyone wanting a lightweight single-purpose tool.

**Risks:** ["Supply-chain / trust: a setup script run against your home dir from a single-maintainer personal repo with NO signed or tagged releases (releases page is empty); review setup and gstack-team-init before running, and pin a commit SHA. Optional auto-upgrade pulling latest main increases exposure. An in-repo audit (#783) and ClawSecure both flag that install uses git clone without GPG signature verification and that bun install does not enforce --frozen-lockfile, allowing dependency drift", "Documented (non-malicious) security vulnerabilities: the v0.15.2.1 audit found 6 critical / 10 high issues including a 0.0.0.0-bound design server with no auth and path traversal on /api/reload, world-readable ~/.gstack/ directories leaking auth tokens/chat history/browser logs, and unsanitized user input interpolated into LLM prompts (prompt injection + cross-model Codex-to-Claude injection). IMPORTANT: independent auditors explicitly found NO malicious code, backdoors, or data exfiltration; these are design/implementation flaws, partially remediated via PR #810. No malware reports exist.", "Single-maintainer governance: bus-factor and abandonment/rug-pull risk are real for a solo personal-account project (garrytan ~98% of commits); no org backing despite YC association", "Complexity/process overhead can slow simple work and produce ceremony that does not fit every project (one reviewer kept only ~6 of ~35 commands)", "Soft lock-in to gstack's conventions and CLAUDE.md routing, plus optional GBrain (Supabase/MCP) data dependency, though MIT + local Markdown keeps exit costs moderate and it is multi-agent", "Hype/credibility risk: headline productivity claims (600k+ lines in 60 days, 10-20k LOC/day, and a quoted ~240x-810x multiplier depending on source) are heavily disputed. Tan himself concedes LOC is a 'garbage' metric and discounts to ~5,700 lines/day, and his numbers reportedly depend on a separate parallelism tool (Conductor) plus months of refinement; critics frame much of gstack as 'a bunch of prompts.' Treat marketing metrics skeptically; the tooling is real, the numbers are inflated/contested", "Browser automation skills launch real/stealth Chromium and can import local browser cookies/sessions (/setup-browser-cookies pulls from Chrome/Arc/Brave/Edge) - a meaningful privacy/security surface to understand before use"]

**Strengths:**
  - End-to-end coverage: a single coherent pipeline from product interrogation through planning, design, review, QA, security, shipping, and post-deploy monitoring, so steps hand artifacts to each other rather than ad-hoc prompting
  - Strong review/QA discipline: /review hunts production-class bugs (N+1, race conditions, trust boundaries), /qa drives a real Chromium browser via Playwright and auto-generates regression tests, /cso runs OWASP+STRIDE audits (independent reviewers single out /qa browser automation and role embodiment as genuinely valuable)
  - Cross-model verification: /codex gets an independent OpenAI Codex opinion and diffs overlapping vs unique findings
  - Cross-session memory and continuity via ~/.gstack artifacts, /learn, and optional GBrain (Supabase/PGLite/MCP)
  - Safety guardrails built in: /careful (destructive-command warnings), /freeze/guard/unfreeze (edit-scope locks)
  - Genuinely multi-agent (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, OpenCode, Factory, Slate, Kiro, Hermes, plus a GBrain mod), reducing single-vendor coupling
  - Local, transparent, and free: plain Markdown/TypeScript skills, MIT-licensed, installs into a local dir with nothing on PATH or running as a daemon by default
  - Disciplined changelog: rigorous near-daily CHANGELOG.md with four-part versioning makes change history auditable even without git tags
  - Battle-tested as one person's real production workflow rather than a theoretical template

**Weaknesses:**
  - Heavy and opinionated: 23 headline tools plus iOS/power/safety/browser utilities (the README enumerates ~28+ slash commands, depending on what you count) is a lot of surface area; reviewers note it can be overkill for narrow work (e.g., pure infrastructure/Pulumi programs do not need a PM or designer role)
  - Steep learning curve: a one-month reviewer reported ~35 commands create friction and only ~6 stayed in active use after four weeks; users must learn WHEN to invoke each mode for it to pay off
  - Pulumi's comparison notes it is comparatively weaker at the actual code-writing step than at planning/review/governance
  - It encodes one person's (Garry Tan's) taste and heavily YC-flavored vocabulary; may mismatch other teams' conventions
  - Single-maintainer bus-factor (garrytan ~98% of commits) and a large open-issue backlog (617) despite high activity
  - No tagged GitHub releases (continuous commits to main); pinning means choosing a commit SHA rather than a signed/tagged release, even though a detailed CHANGELOG exists
  - Documented security findings: an in-repo audit (issue #783, gstack v0.15.2.1) reported 6 critical / 10 high / 12 medium / 7 low issues (e.g., a design server binding 0.0.0.0 with no auth + path traversal, world-readable ~/.gstack/ dirs (0o755) exposing tokens/history, and unsanitized prompt-injection trust boundaries); remediation began via PR #810. A separate third-party ClawSecure scan (2026-05-14) scored it 0/100, primarily for lacking a permissions manifest
  - Windows is a second-class target (symlink caveats, extra Node.js requirement, re-run setup after pulls)
  - Reported productivity numbers are marketing-flavored and contested (see risks)

**Fact-check confidence:** high

**Verification notes:** Re-checked the four riskiest claim areas against primary sources. STARS/POPULARITY (CONFIRMED, high): GitHub API on 2026-06-09 returned stargazers_count 108631, forks_count 16147, subscribers_count 655, open_issues_count 617, license MIT, created_at 2026-03-11, pushed_at 2026-06-09, archived=false, language TypeScript, description 'Use Garry Tan's exact Claude Code setup: 23 opinionated tools...'. ~50k stars in 16 days independently corroborated (OSS Insight). Stale counts in comparison blogs (Pulumi 71K, Augment 89.7K) are dated, not contradictory. MAINTENANCE/RELEASES (CORRECTED): the /releases page is EMPTY (no tagged releases) - so 'no tagged releases' is TRUE. BUT the prior profile's 'no changelog-based release cadence' is WRONG: CHANGELOG.md is rigorous with four-part semver (MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.BUILD), latest v1.57.7.0 dated 2026-06-08; the previously-flagged 'v1.57.6.0' third-party claim is a REAL changelog entry, not a conflict. Contributors API shows 9 contributors, garrytan 307/314 (~97.8%); the third-party '81 contributors' figure is unsupported by the API and treated as unreliable (likely the web contributors-graph counting co-authors). CLAUDE CODE INTEGRATION (CONFIRMED, high): raw README confirms skill-pack install (git clone into ~/.claude/skills/gstack + ./setup, SKILL.md symlinks), NOT a plugin/npx/MCP-primary tool; --host multi-agent table verified (codex/opencode/cursor/factory/slate/kiro/hermes/gbrain). MCP only optional for GBrain. Hooks/settings.json reliance: still no evidence; guards appear to be skills (kept as uncertain). SECURITY/TRUST (CONFIRMED, high, important new detail): a documented in-repo audit (issue #783, gstack v0.15.2.1, 2026-04-03) reported 6 critical / 10 high / 12 medium / 7 low findings - 0.0.0.0-bound no-auth design server with path traversal, world-readable ~/.gstack/ (0o755) leaking tokens/history, unsanitized prompt-injection and cross-model trust-boundary issues; closed via PR #810 (remediation started). A separate ClawSecure scan (2026-05-14) scored 0/100, chiefly for a missing permissions manifest. CRUCIALLY, independent auditors found NO malicious code, backdoors, or data exfiltration, and there are NO malware reports - the project is not abandoned and not a rug-pull at this time. PHILOSOPHY (CORRECTED): ETHOS.md lists FOUR principles (Boil the Ocean; Search Before Building; User Sovereignty; Build for Yourself); 'Three Layers of Knowledge' is a sub-model inside 'Search Before Building', not a standalone third principle. PRODUCTIVITY CLAIMS (LOW/CONTESTED, unchanged): 600k lines/60 days, 10-20k LOC/day, and a multiplier variously cited as 240x or 810x; Tan concedes LOC is 'garbage' and discounts to ~5,700/day, and the numbers reportedly lean on a separate parallelism tool (Conductor). sitepoint.com was inaccessible earlier (HTTP 403) so it was not used as a primary source. Overall confidence raised to HIGH because every core, maintenance, integration, and security claim was verified against GitHub API, raw repo files, and the in-repo audit issue.
